# Chorus v1 — Master Roadmap (Foundation)

_Location: `docs/Features/Foundation/roadmap.md` · Last updated: 2026-07-23_

---

## 1. Purpose

Chorus v1 is a local-first, BYOK Electron + Vue 3 + TypeScript desktop app (Windows-only) for running multiple AI coding agents in parallel terminal panes. The **prime contract**: a developer can launch, watch, control, and persist many concurrent agent sessions across multiple projects — restart-safe and cleanly killable — from a single window.

This roadmap refines **`docs/PLAN.md` §14** into an executable, phase-by-phase program of work. It is the single master roadmap for the whole v1 effort. It records **what** each phase delivers and **why** — not how (implementation detail lives in Task docs and Implementation Specs).

---

## 2. Source Of Truth

| Authority | Owns |
|---|---|
| `docs/PLAN.md` (master plan v2.1) | Product vision, full architecture, macro-phase intent |
| `CLAUDE.md` | Non-negotiable, locked architecture rules |
| **This roadmap** | Phase status, decision log, verified ground facts |
| Workflow skills (`/architect`, `/phase-kickoff`, `/phase-prompt`) | The operating rhythm that keeps the three above in sync (see §3) |

When these disagree, PLAN.md and CLAUDE.md win on intent and rules; this roadmap wins on _current status_ and _what happens next_.

---

## 3. Governance & Workflow

Chorus is built on a repeating rhythm. One phase at a time, in the order below.

1. **`/architect`** — after each phase lands, updates this roadmap: marks the phase complete, re-verifies ground facts against the code, revises provisional phases, appends decisions.
2. **`/phase-kickoff`** — decomposes the next phase into `Phase-N-Overview.md` plus 1–5 paired `Task-N-#.md` + `ImplementationSpec-N-#.md` files under this Foundation folder (`Tasks/` and `ImplementationSpecs/`).
3. **`/phase-prompt`** — generates a verbose, self-contained execution prompt to open a fresh conversation for the phase.
4. **Execution session** — implements the tasks, **verifies by running the real app** (not just compiling), and makes **one intentional narrated commit**.
5. **Back to `/architect`** — the loop closes; the roadmap re-syncs to reality.

Council Review (§4) can interrupt steps 2 and 4 whenever a section meets its trigger criteria.

---

## 4. Council Review (CR) Mechanism

Matthew runs a **multi-LLM council** — a Cursor-based setup that uses several other LLM models for independent review and deliberation. **Claude cannot run the council.** Claude's obligations around it are:

1. **Flag** when a section meets CR trigger criteria — at kickoff time or mid-phase.
2. **Prepare a council brief**: the specific decision / design / diff in question, the development goals and acceptance criteria it must satisfy, and the specific questions Claude wants answered.
3. **Pause** and prompt Matthew to run the council. Do not proceed past the checkpoint.
4. **Record findings** in the §6 decision log — unanimous agreement, dissents, and action items — before continuing.

**Trigger criteria for a "tough section":**

- Hard-to-reverse architectural shapes — adapter interface, DB schema growth, event bus design.
- Security-sensitive surfaces — credential vault, secret injection/redaction, localhost hook listener, permission broker.
- Data-loss surfaces — worktree lifecycle, destructive git operations.
- Anything an execution session flags as **low-confidence**, or where **two approaches remain contested**.

Phases marked **[CR]** below carry at least one pre-identified council checkpoint.

**Planned migration (D27, 2026-07-20):** this externally-run mechanism is slated to become a **native Chorus feature** in **Phase 3b — Native Council Review** (§7): 3–5 configurable API-mode council members + a frontier arbiter over the Phase-3 vault/provider infrastructure, an in-app council pane, brief-`.md` in → findings-`.md` out in this same format. Until 3b lands, CR runs stay in Cursor exactly as described here.

| **(F25, found by the Task 3-3 implementer, coordinator-confirmed in code 2026-07-23) A bogus-agent session row blanks the WHOLE project view — `layout:get`'s aggregate outbound parse throws.** `sessionInfoSchema.agent` is `agentKindSchema` (the two-value enum), and the `layout:get` handler outbound-parses the entire `{layout, sessions[]}` response — so ONE row whose `agent` column holds an unknown value rejects the whole invoke, `App.vue`'s load watcher has no catch (uncaught rejection), `sessions[]` never populates, and the renderer shows the empty state for a project that has a real layout. **Pre-existing at HEAD `a0b6a5e`** — the schema and handler predate 3-3 and were unchanged by it; surfaced by 3-3's mandated unknown-agent proof. D34(c)'s "no other session is affected" **holds at the session layer** (restore heals the bogus row, healthy sessions relaunch, `session:attach` works while the row is bogus — runtime-proven) **but not at the layout-view layer**. Severity: latent (nothing writes non-enum agent values today; Phase 6's registration seam is when that changes). **Fix direction: tolerate at the projection, not the schema** — the `layout:get` handler filters unknown-agent rows out of `sessions[]` (registry lookup) with a pino warning naming the row id; the affected leaf then renders `LayoutRenderer`'s existing leaf-without-row placeholder, which is the honest degraded state. The head/tail mask is a **≥8-character substring of the real key**, rendered on screen and appended to the ring buffer. Task 3-5's scrubber matches **exact values** (D33(a)); a mask is a *derived* form, so exact-value scrubbing **cannot match it BY CONSTRUCTION** — this is not a scrubber defect, it is a boundary of the mechanism the council chose. **This sharpens D33's council risk 1 from "an agent could echo the key" to the stronger and true statement: the agent's own auth UX is a fragment-leak channel — an agent can echo a DERIVED form of a key.** **Not a Phase 3 defect:** D44 makes `claude` subscription-only by product policy, so Chorus injects no key into it and the prompt was only reachable in a deliberate probe. **Standing constraint:** **Phase 3b and every later phase must never word a transcript/exfiltration guarantee as "agents cannot echo the key"** — the honest wording is "Chorus redacts registered exact values on ingest; it cannot redact values an agent derives." The value was fully legible **on screen** and simultaneously **invisible to substring matching**, so exact-value scrubbing was defeated without any bug in the scrubber. This is the **documented ANSI-interleaving limit observed LIVE** (D33's accepted residual, Qwen's preserved dissent), not a new defect. The harness now boots with `TERM=xterm-256color` and the interleaving disappears. **Proposal, NOT done in 3-6 (env-policy changes were out of scope):** pin `TERM` deliberately in `composeChildEnv` (or in the spawn options) rather than inheriting host-shell noise — since `TERM` is a non-secret eighth variable, doing so **amends D33's seven-var allow-list** and must be ruled on, not slipped in. ### Main process

| Fact | Where | Verified |
|---|---|---|
| `SessionManager` — `Map<string, PtySession>` keyed by **stable DB session row id**; N concurrent same-kind sessions. Public: `bindStorage`, `launch(agent, cwd, sessionId)` (spawn under a caller-minted row id), **`attach(sessionId)` — single-argument pure view binding, NO spawn path** (returns `SessionSnapshot \| null`), **`restore(projectId)`** (the D16 engine: `computeRestoreSet` → heal-first → cwd-validated staggered spawns, `running` written after success, cap 16), `isRunning`, `isRestorePending`, `consumeRestoredBadge` (F10 consume-once), `onRestored`, `kill`, `write`, `resize`, `getAgent`, `onData`/`onExit` (listener Sets), `dispose`. `restore()` is the **one documented place** the manager touches storage (heal + post-spawn status writes are contract steps); rows are otherwise minted by the IPC layer. | `src/main/services/sessionManager.ts`, `src/main/services/restore.ts` | 2026-07-19 |
| **`computeRestoreSet(layout, rows, live)`** — pure, Electron-free, structurally-typed module returning `{toRelaunch, toHeal, missingRows}`; 6 unit tests cover all four populations + failed-spawn orphan + already-live. | `src/main/services/restore.ts` | 2026-07-19 |
| IPC — **20 channels**: invokes `session:attach` / `launch` / `launch-context` / `write` / `resize` / `kill` / **`restart`** / **`delete`** / **`set-title`**, `cli:detect`, `layout:get` / `set`, **`view:get`** / **`view:set`**, **`project:add`** (main-side `dialog.showOpenDialog`; cancel = structured no-op) / **`project:list`** / **`project:select`** (persists active id, lazy-restores idempotently, retitles window); events `session:data` / `exit` / **`restored`**. All Zod in **main only**; every project-scoped handler parses `project_id` (`z.uuid()`) and FK-checks it via `requireProject`. `session:launch` keeps the cwd security boundary + 16-pane soft cap; `layout:set` payload is `{project_id, layout: nullable}` — null DELETEs the row. **`view:get`** FK-checks `project_id` and returns the stored per-project view state or the filmstrip default `{mode:'filmstrip', focusedSessionId:null}` when no row exists, outbound-parsed with `viewStateSchema`; **`view:set`** FK-checks and persists. `focusedSessionId` is schema-validated as **nullable string only, never FK-checked** (F4). `session:restart` refuses live sessions, re-validates cwd, spawns under the same row id **and emits NO renderer event** (F14); `session:delete` refuses live sessions (invoke **rejection**, not a value — callers must await-catch). **`session:set-title`** (`setTitleRequestSchema` = `{sessionId: z.uuid(), title: z.string().min(1).max(120)}`) sanitizes via exported `sanitizeTitle` (strips C0+DEL), re-bounds to 120, silently no-ops on empty post-sanitize, logs `[title] persisted …` per write. **`sessionInfoSchema` grew `createdAt` (ISO string) AND `exitCode` (int nullable) in 1b-2** so filmstrip cards can compute elapsed and color their status dot from the `layout:get` rows alone (cards never attach) — the `exitCode` addition was a **flagged, ratified deviation** from the task's scope table (the spec's card-dot mapping required it; zero handler changes, the existing outbound parse surfaces both from `SessionRow`). | `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/main/ipc.ts` | 2026-07-20 |
| Preload is a **Zod-free typed forwarder**; page CSP forbids Zod's `eval`. Surface: `attachSession`, `launch`, `getLaunchContext(projectId)`, `restartSession`, `deleteSession`, **`setSessionTitle(sessionId, title)`**, `detectClis`, `getLayout(projectId)`, `setLayout`, **`getViewState(projectId)`**, **`setViewState(projectId, state)`**, `addProject`, `listProjects`, `selectProject`, `writeSession`, `resizeSession`, `killSession`, `onSessionData`, `onSessionExit`, `onSessionRestored`. `ChorusApi` is inferred from the object. | `src/preload/index.ts` | 2026-07-20 |
| Storage — better-sqlite3 (WAL) at `userData/chorus.db`; **Drizzle for typed queries only**, migrations stay a hand-rolled `MIGRATIONS` array + `schema_migrations` runner (deliberate scope cut under D7). **Applied versions: 1–11** (v3 = `ALTER TABLE sessions ADD COLUMN title TEXT;` 2026-07-19T23:47Z; v4 = `CREATE TABLE worktrees (…)` + `sessions.worktree_id`, applied in place 2026-07-20T16:57:49Z, zero data loss; v5 = `provider_configs` + `credential_profiles` (D33); v6 = `ALTER TABLE provider_configs ADD COLUMN model TEXT;` (D48); **v7 = `dispatches` + `dispatches_open` index + `attention_spans`** (Task 3a-1, `8a2e8c3`); **v8 = the mint ledger — six `ALTER TABLE dispatches` columns + `dispatches_open_ledger` index** (Task 3a-3, `f56dcaf`, applied 2026-07-25T20:46:53.759Z); runs and messages **created EMPTY**, first written in Task 3b-3, the `attention_spans` precedent) — all coordinator-verified on the real dev DB through the three-dump protocol). ~~Eleven tables: `projects` / `pane_layouts` / `settings` / `schema_migrations` / `sessions` / `worktrees` / `provider_configs` / `credential_profiles` / **`dispatches`** / **`attention_spans`** / **`model_catalog`**.~~ ~~**TWELVE tables**, listed explicitly so a future pass has something to check a claim against rather than a version range to infer one from: `attention_spans` · `credential_profiles` · `dispatches` · **`launch_profiles`** · `model_catalog` · `pane_layouts` · `projects` · `provider_configs` · `schema_migrations` · `sessions` · `settings` · `worktrees`.~~ **⚠ Both counts above are SUPERSEDED by v11 and are struck rather than removed, so the drift trail this row exists to expose stays legible.** **FIFTEEN tables**, listed explicitly so a future pass has something to check a claim against rather than a version range to infer one from: `attention_spans` · **`council_members`** · **`council_messages`** · **`council_runs`** · `credential_profiles` · `dispatches` · `launch_profiles` · `model_catalog` · `pane_layouts` · `projects` · `provider_configs` · `schema_migrations` · `sessions` · `settings` · `worktrees`. **THREE indexes**, listed for the same reason: **`council_messages_run`** · `dispatches_open` · `dispatches_open_ledger`. **FKs are ENFORCED (F16)** — the driver defaults `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` — **which is exactly why the two v7 telemetry tables and v9's `model_catalog` carry NO `REFERENCES` clause**: a dispatch and an attention span both outlive the session row they name (pane close deletes it, D16(d)), so a FK would make the next pane close throw, and a `REFERENCES provider_configs(id)` on the catalog would make the first `provider:delete` after a refresh throw for the sake of a **cache**. Each of the three handles the consequence explicitly instead: `deleteProviderConfig` purges the provider's catalog rows in its own transaction, **and the pre-existing count-and-refuse on `credential_profiles` is untouched — profiles still block a delete, a catalog never does.** **v10 takes the deliberate INVERSE ruling (D62):** `launch_profiles` carries REAL `REFERENCES` to both its route and its credential, because a launch profile is a live INSTRUCTION that becomes a lie the moment either is gone — and RESTRICT is right **precisely because** it forces a count-and-refuse to be authored in main BEFORE the statement runs. **`sessions.launch_profile_id` is the third case and takes the `dispatches` ruling** — a SOFT pointer with no `REFERENCES` (`storage.ts:279` says so), its dangling case absorbed by D61's fail-safe predicate. **⚠ v11 THEN SPLITS THE SAME RULING THREE WAYS INSIDE A SINGLE MIGRATION, which is why D62 is amended rather than merely cited (Task 3b-2, `01556a8`):** `council_members.credential_profile_id` takes the `launch_profiles` ruling — REAL `REFERENCES`, RESTRICT, because a member is a live INSTRUCTION; `council_runs` takes the `dispatches`/`model_catalog` ruling — **ZERO references on every column**, because a run is a historical FACT; and `council_messages` takes it too, because **a transcript stays true after its member is deleted** and a FK there would make deleting one member throw for every run it ever joined. Verified by `PRAGMA foreign_key_list` — **council_members 1, council_runs 0, council_messages 0** — with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1` re-confirmed. **Because SQLite will not cascade a soft pointer, `deleteCouncilRun(id)` purges its own `council_messages` in one transaction**, the `deleteProviderConfig` → `model_catalog` precedent. **`council_members` carries NO `base_url` and NO `provider_id`:** a member names a route BY NAMING A CREDENTIAL, reached through `credential_profiles.provider_id` → `provider_configs.base_url`, which is **D48's one home**; `launch_profiles` needs both columns only because **D33 clause 9** makes a route-with-no-credential first class, and a council member ALWAYS authenticates, so that case does not exist and two columns able to disagree would be a bug class. **⚠ THIS IS THE ROADMAP'S MOST DRIFT-PRONE ROW — three passes have now had to correct it LATE, and the cause is structural: it is the only §5 row a migration invalidates, while the live counts that move every task deliberately live in the task docs instead.** The failure is not slow drift but a SKIPPED READ: `6e12b0e` closed Phase 3a without adding any §5 re-verification, so v10 went unrecorded until the next pass found it. **Re-read this row against a live dump FIRST, at the start of every architect pass.** **⚠ AND THE FOURTH PASS DID NOT DRIFT — the first time that is true of this row.** v11 was recorded HERE, at the architect pass immediately following the migration, from a live dump read BEFORE the row was edited, rather than repaired after a later pass noticed. **That practice is the thing to repeat; the warning above is what it costs when it is skipped.** Re-verified against the shipped v10 **and v11** DDL and a live dump of the real dev DB 2026-07-26. | `src/main/services/storage.ts`, `src/main/db/schema.ts` | **2026-07-26** |

| **(F29, Task 3a-2 Step 0) CDP-injected input is INVISIBLE to the Windows idle timer, and this invalidates the obvious way to drive any idle-dependent proof.** `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` / `insertText` deliver into Chromium, not through the OS input stack, so `GetLastInputInfo` — which `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime()` wraps — never sees them: measured live, the counter climbed **monotonically 44 s → 86 s straight through five CDP keystrokes** and flipped `getSystemIdleState(60)` to `'idle'` mid-phase. **Any future phase proving presence, idle, attention, or away-detection must drive with REAL OS input** — the harness uses a bare `SHIFT` press+release via `keybd_event`, which travels the real input path but **inserts no character into any window**, so it is safe to fire while an arbitrary app holds focus (`_verify/3a-2/osinput.ps1`). An independent out-of-process PowerShell `GetLastInputInfo` P/Invoke **agrees with Electron's reading to within 0.89 s** across 63 aligned pairs (integer truncation + sampling skew), which is what proves Electron reports the OS counter rather than something about its own window. Two corollaries for any focus-dependent drive: the dev window is **not** foregrounded by default in a harness session (so `isFocused()`-dependent code records the blurred branch until `SetForegroundWindow` is called), and **other desktop apps steal foreground mid-run** — sample `GetForegroundWindow()` through the phase and retry rather than trusting it. | `powerMonitor`; `_verify/3a-2/step0.ps1`, `STEP0-RESULTS.md` | 2026-07-25 |
| **(F30, Task 3a-2 Step 0) `getSystemIdleState()` has NOT been observed returning `'locked'` on this machine, and the lock probe is not cheaply runnable.** Across a 150 s instrumented run only `'active'` and `'idle'` appeared, and **zero** `lock-screen` / `unlock-screen` / `suspend` / `resume` events fired. The probe was **deliberately not driven**: `LockWorkStation` is only reversible by typing Matthew's Windows password, which an agent must not handle, so in an unattended session the cost of the answer exceeds its value. **Consequence, and it is a real limit rather than a formality:** the `locked` attention class rests on the **`'lock-screen'` event alone** — a smaller claim than "event plus state cross-check", recorded as smaller in `attention.ts`'s header. Anything later that depends on lock/suspend detection (Phase 4 notifications; Phase 8's away-time) **must re-open this with a human at the keyboard** rather than inherit it as settled. | `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleState`; `_verify/3a-2/STEP0-RESULTS.md` | 2026-07-25 |

| **Harness caveats (F3/F7/F8/F9/F11):** `TaskStop` kills only the wrapper shell — kill the process **tree** (`taskkill /PID <root> /T /F`) + confirm CDP port rebind, or the "fresh boot" is the old window; a **graceful** quit test is `taskkill /PID <electron-main-pid>` *without* `/F` (delivers WM_CLOSE → `before-quit` → `dispose()`). `ComSpec` + registry PATH must be restored. `npx`/`npm run` prepend the npm-global dir to child PATH — invoke `node node_modules/electron-vite/bin/electron-vite.js dev -- --remote-debugging-port=9222` directly. electron-vite HMR covers the renderer only. Orphan checks walk the electron main PID's descendant tree, never grep `tasklist` (~16 unrelated `claude.exe` on this machine). `window.confirm` blocks the renderer thread — fire CDP clicks async, dismiss with a real mouse click. **Screenshots: `PrintWindow(hwnd, PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)`, never `CopyFromScreen`** (captures occluders). Native `#32770` dialogs: drive via `FindWindowEx` + `WM_SETTEXT` + `BM_CLICK` (SendKeys/foreground-lock is unreliable). Output-continuation assertions must match **response-only strings**, never text echoed from the prompt. **CDP on `--remote-debugging-port=9222` beat the user32 helper** (1b-1): `Runtime.evaluate` for DOM assertions (wrap in an IIFE — top-level `const` collides across evaluates), `Page.captureScreenshot` (no window-coordinate issues), `Input.insertText` reaches xterm `onData` as one chunk; `ws` installed in the session scratchpad, never the repo. `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` scripts print nothing to a PowerShell console (electron.exe is GUI-subsystem) — write results to a file. **1b-3 note:** the `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` better-sqlite3 dump script intermittently produced **no output file on first invocation** (twice, no error surfaced); an immediate retry succeeded with consistent data — **retry once before diagnosing.** | execution sessions | 2026-07-20 |
| **(F12) Title behavior binds 1b-2's filmstrip cards.** (a) Claude Code's OSC title is **live and twitchy** (~1 Hz spinner while the agent works) — cards reading titles will flicker during activity unless they render the **persisted** value or debounce re-renders. (b) Codex titles are just the **cwd basename**, so same-project Codex sessions title identically — cards must compose **agent + title**, not title alone, to disambiguate. (c) The renderer header shows the **pre-sanitize** value until the next attach; anything reading over IPC always gets the **sanitized** value. Found via the 1b-2 renderer error hook (two `null.onTitleChange` TypeErrors), root-caused as **pre-existing 1b-1 exposure amplified by 1b-2's keyed focus-swap remounts (F5)**, fixed in `de98679`. Binds any future component with awaits in `onMounted` (palette, worktree UI). **Corollary — grid mode does NOT track focus:** the minimal grid-focus-follow path was taken (`LayoutRenderer` untouched; `TerminalPane`'s focus emit is only consumed via `FilmstripRenderer`). | **(F31, Task 3a-3, 2026-07-25) ⚠ F20's WORKAROUND RESTORES THE DATABASE, NOT THE VAULT — `--user-data-dir` does not restore the DPAPI context, and credential blobs written under a different one are UNDECRYPTABLE.** Booting the dev app from a raw harness shell with `--user-data-dir=…\Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc\LocalCache\Roaming\chorus` opens the **right database** (proven: projects `985d547b…`/`f47ac10b…`, migrations v1–v7 with their real timestamps) — but **both credential blobs written on 2026-07-24 failed to decrypt**: `[vault] decrypt failed; profile marked unavailable`, for `OR milestone key` AND `Claude fake key`. **A profile created in the SAME boot decrypted fine**, so the vault mechanism is healthy and it is the CONTEXT that changed. `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` returned `true` throughout, and the user-data-dir's `Local State` file was untouched since 2026-07-18 — so the OSCrypt key file is not the variable. **The mechanism is UNPROVEN** (most likely the packaged-container process identity the 2026-07-24 session ran under versus a raw process, but that was not demonstrated and should not be repeated as fact). **Consequences:** (1) an execution session that needs a REAL credential cannot inherit one from a previous session's vault — it must be re-entered through Settings, which is a HUMAN step and belongs in the prompt's preconditions, not discovered at minute forty; (2) **D33 clause 8 behaved exactly as designed and is now proven in anger** — the rows survived, both launches were refused **by label**, and `replaceProfile` → `updateCredentialBlob` cleared `unavailable_since` on re-entry; (3) a task whose acceptance depends on live spend should verify decryptability **before** its first billable step. **⚠ AMENDED 2026-07-25 by Task 3a-4 — the "it is the CONTEXT that changed" reading is TOO STRONG, and the correction matters because it changes what a future session should plan for.** Re-probed in a single harness boot: `Claude fake key` again failed to decrypt, **but `OR milestone key` DECRYPTED FINE** — the authenticated model refresh ran on it and returned 345 models. A scratch copy of `chorus.db` taken **without `Local State`** therefore gets a **freshly generated** key on first boot — so every blob written in an earlier session is undecryptable while a profile created in *that same boot* decrypts fine. **That asymmetry is exactly what Task 3a-3 observed and could not explain.** Proven both ways in one session: copying the database alone produced two `[vault] decrypt failed; profile marked unavailable` errors and a failed attribution key census; copying **`Local State` alongside it** made the identical blob decrypt and the census return `4 live · 0 ours`. **Practical rules: (1) copy `Local State` beside `chorus.db` for any rehearsal or scratch boot; (2) treat a credential blob as bound to the user-data DIRECTORY, not merely to the Windows user — anything that relocates or recreates that directory invalidates every stored key.** | Task 3a-3 execution, `_verify/3a-3/boot1.log` + `step0-vault-diagnose.json`; amendment: Task 3a-4, `_verify/3a-4/vault-probe.json` + `step3-refresh-auth.json`; **mechanism proven: Task 3a-5, `_verify/3a-5/boot-rehearsal.log` (without) vs `boot-rehearsal2.log` (with)** | 2026-07-25, mechanism **2026-07-26** |

A blind round asks **every member CONCURRENTLY**, so their delta events interleave arbitrarily, and the store appended each incoming delta to the **most recently opened block** — so every switch between members opened a new one. The run rendered **291 message fragments for 8 turns.** Fixed by keying the block on **(member, phase, round)**; the identical re-run rendered **7**, and a regression test now drives interleaved deltas from two members. **⚠ This is a STANDING CONSTRAINT, not a closed bug: any future surface that renders more than one model stream at once inherits the class** — the **native chat pane D45 commits to** is the named next one, and a multi-agent transcript view or a streamed diff review would be the same shape. **The general rule: recency is not identity.** A demultiplexer keyed on *"the last thing I saw"* is correct only while exactly one producer exists — which is precisely the condition the council protocol is built to violate. | `src/renderer/src/stores/council.ts` + Task 3b-4 drive-3 runtime observation | 2026-07-26 |

Two further weaknesses reproduced exactly as 3b-3 predicted: **one member produced 6 of the 9 dissent entries** (talkative-member skew), and **two "dissents" are noise** — text mechanically pulled from a `DISAGREE:` label whose prose says nothing challenges the synthesis, because the matcher cannot distinguish a non-objection from an objection. **Where native is STRONGER, all three are structural rather than qualitative:** provenance is machine-generated and exact; the dissent section is core-generated so the arbiter cannot drop it; the detection path is labelled per question — at **$0.0012 against frontier models**. **The honest statement: the pipeline works; these models at this cap cannot yet produce a document of the external run's completeness.** | `_verify/3b-4/` (drive-4 findings, preserved out-of-repo) vs `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md` | 2026-07-26 |

So the duplication is **two correct mechanisms meeting**, not a defect in either. **The cost is cosmetic but real: a reader cannot tell which of the two is authoritative**, and the authoritative one is the machine-generated appendix the arbiter cannot edit. **⚠ THE CORE'S UNCONDITIONAL APPEND IS THE WHOLE ENFORCEABILITY ARGUMENT AND MUST NOT BE THE THING THAT GETS REMOVED** — deleting it to de-duplicate would hand dissent preservation back to the arbiter's goodwill, which is 5A, explicitly rejected as unenforceable. **The fix belongs on the CORE'S HEADING (distinguish it — provenance is already labelled per entry) or on the SYNTHESIS PROMPT (tell the arbiter the section is generated for it), never on the append itself.** | `src/main/services/councilCore.ts` + run `87082401-9e0a-4387-b234-638deed648df`'s findings document | 2026-07-26 |

| **(F21, found by coordinator review of Task 2-3, 2026-07-21) The `-D` branch-force escalation is gated only by the RENDERER — main permits it on a CLEAN worktree.** `worktree:remove` computes `forceBranch: req.confirmation === w.path` **outside** the dirty branch, so a request carrying a path-matching `confirmation` together with `deleteBranch: true` force-deletes an **unmerged** branch even when the worktree is clean — destroying commits. It is **unreachable through the shipped UI**: `WorktreePanel.vue` sends `confirmation` only when `needsToken` (dirty), so a clean removal always attempts plain `-d` and surfaces an unmerged refusal. **Severity: latent, not live.** But it is the one data-loss path in Phase 2 whose safety rests on renderer behavior rather than a main-side gate — inconsistent with the defense-in-depth used everywhere else in this task (main re-checks cleanliness even though the renderer already read it fresh). The implementer flagged it themselves (summary deviation 7 / concern 2) and asked for a ruling. **Ruling: their instinct is correct.** D26(j)'s "same typed-confirmation acknowledgment" was written for the dirty-removal case; overloading that one token to mean two different things (destroy uncommitted files / destroy unmerged commits) is the defect. **Fix direction (deferred, NOT Task 2-4 scope):** a distinct branch-force acknowledgment — a separate field main requires before ever passing `force: true` to `branchDelete` — so the escalation is unreachable regardless of what any renderer sends. | `main/ipc.ts` `worktree:remove`; verified against `WorktreePanel.vue:112` | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F22, ground-fact CORRECTION, coordinator 2026-07-21) The `Chorus-Second` project's `root_path` is `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished` — the PARENT directory — not `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished\Chorus-Second`.** Read directly from the real dev DB. The Task 2-2 and 2-3 execution prompts both asserted the `…\Chorus-Second` path; the directory does exist, but the project row has never pointed at it. **No live defect:** `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished` is not a git repository (`git rev-parse` there fails), so `resolveRepoRoot` returns null, the project contributes nothing to F19's union enumeration, and the "not a git repo" inline state that Task 2-2 verified against this project still renders — for a different reason than documented. **Why it now matters:** F19 made `resolveRepoRoot(project.rootPath)` run for **every** project on every boot, so this row is on a hot path it was not on before. If that parent directory ever becomes a repo (a stray `git init`), it would be scanned and its managed root would resolve to `C:\Projects\.chorus\ContactEstablished` — a different tree from Chorus's own, so still no collision, but worth knowing. Prompts must stop repeating the wrong path. | real `%APPDATA%\chorus\chorus.db` dump | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F23, found by the Task 2-4 implementer, coordinator-confirmed 2026-07-21) A palette launch REPLACES the whole layout tree — surviving sessions become invisible and die at the next boot.** `layoutStore.insertLaunchedLeaf(target, id)` falls through to `createLeaf(id)` and assigns `this.tree = { version: 1, root }` whenever `target` is null (`src/renderer/src/stores/layout.ts:55-63`). Its comment says "the single root leaf when launching from the empty state", which was correct when the empty state was its only caller — **Task 1b-3's "Launch agent…" palette command made that path reachable with a POPULATED tree** (`App.vue` passes `splitTarget.value`, which is null for a palette launch). Consequence chain: every other pane vanishes from the layout; the sessions keep running in main (PTYs are unaffected); but they are now **leafless `running` rows**, and D16 Q2's invisible-process guard heals leafless `running` rows to `exited` **before any spawn on the next boot** — so those agents are silently terminated at restart. **No on-disk data loss** (agent work lives in files/worktrees), but the session and its scrollback are gone. **Pre-existing since 1b-3; NOT introduced by Task 2-4** — the implementer hit it with a driver script and reported it. **Fix direction (Phase 3):** for a null target, append-as-split against the focused pane (or insert without discarding the tree); the empty-state case is the only one that should ever replace the root. | `stores/layout.ts` + coordinator read of `App.vue`/D16 | 2026-07-21 |
| **(D26f trigger semantics — CLARIFICATION, not a defect, coordinator 2026-07-21) A live agent that is ITSELF in a worktree does not flip the launch default to new-worktree.** `resolveRepoRoot` inside a worktree returns the **worktree's own** toplevel, so worktree-resident sessions never match the main repo root and never count toward `liveSessionsInRepo` (`main/ipc.ts` documents this as intended D22 semantics). The Task 2-4 implementer flagged that Phase-2 acceptance wording ("launching a second agent whose cwd resolves to the same repo root defaults to new isolated worktree") reads broader than the implementation. **Coordinator ruling: the IMPLEMENTATION is correct and the WORDING is loose.** The default exists to prevent two writers in one tree; a current-tree launch collides only if another session is already in the current tree. Three worktree agents plus an empty main tree is four distinct trees — safe — and the default flips to new-worktree the instant a current-tree session exists, i.e. exactly when collision becomes possible. **Action: correct the acceptance wording to "a live session IN THE MAIN TREE"; no code change.** | `main/ipc.ts` launch-context; coordinator analysis | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F15, pre-existing app-wide CSS defect, surfaced during 1b-3 — NOT from 1b-3; FIXED in Task 2-1 chore commit `624f3da`)** `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css` (lines 3–9) contains an **unlayered** `*, *::before, *::after { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0; padding: 0 }` reset after `@import 'tailwindcss'`. Unlayered CSS beats **all** `@layer` rules, so **every Tailwind v4 margin/padding utility app-wide is generated but nullified** (verified: `.pt-24` and `.mt-2` rules exist in the sheet yet compute to 0; non-margin/padding utilities like `max-h-80`, `w-[32rem]`, colors are unaffected). The whole UI (LaunchDialog's `p-5`/`mt-3`, the palette's `pt-24`, etc.) renders **tighter than designed** and has since the reset was introduced. Fix is **one line** (drop the reset or move it into a layer — Tailwind preflight already resets); **deliberately NOT fixed in 1b-3** (out of its file list). Candidate small chore before or during Phase 2. | `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css` + 1b-3 runtime observation | 2026-07-20 |

## 6. Global Decisions & Gates

### Decisions

All **RESOLVED 2026-07-18** unless noted.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | All Zod validation lives in **main only** — CSP forbids `eval` in preload. | RESOLVED |
| D2 | **No electron-rebuild.** node-pty uses shipped prebuilds; better-sqlite3 via `.npmrc` electron runtime + `/Od` source build (temporary — see §5). | RESOLVED |
| D3 | Sessions live in **main**, owned by `SessionManager`; the renderer never spawns processes. | RESOLVED (CLAUDE.md, locked) |
| D4 | Verify CLI flags against the tool's own `--help` at build time — never from training memory. | RESOLVED (CLAUDE.md, locked) |
| D5 | Child PTYs inherit env untouched; no credentials injected/logged anywhere. **Will be superseded by the Phase 3 vault work.** **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-24 by Task 3-6's `15a016e`, exactly as foreseen.** Child PTYs no longer inherit env untouched: `composeChildEnv` (`src/main/adapters/env.ts`) builds a **closed allow-list** env — `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` = `PATH, SystemRoot, TEMP, TMP, HOMEDRIVE, HOMEPATH, USERPROFILE` plus the one secret variable named by `resolveEnvVarName` — applied at `sessionManager.ts:361` for credential-bearing launches per D33(c). The second half of the original wording **still stands and is now enforced rather than assumed**: no credential ever reaches argv, logs, or transcripts (G4 clean across 6 patterns at `15a016e`). Kept here per supersede-never-delete; read D33, D46, D47 and D49 for the operative contract. | SUPERSEDED 2026-07-24 (`15a016e`) |
| D6 | Council Review runs in Cursor by Matthew; Claude flags, briefs, pauses, and records findings (§4). | RESOLVED |
| D7 | **Adopt Drizzle ORM now** — Phase 1 migrates the existing 4 tables' access code to Drizzle and defines all new Phase-1 schema in it. (Matthew's call at Phase 1 kickoff, against the deferral recommendation — typed queries from day one won.) | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 |
| D8 | **Adopt Tailwind CSS at sub-phase 1.1** — first real UI mass; existing scoped styles migrate as touched. | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 |
| D9 | Pane layout engine (CR-1.2): **Option C — owned binary split tree as persisted data model; splitpanes@~4.1.2 as dumb grid renderer behind a `LayoutRenderer.vue` adapter.** Council verdict unanimous 3-of-3 (Claude, Gemini, GPT); Gemini dissent-on-preference for full custom (B) recorded, conceded on implementation risk. **Escape hatch: if the xterm-in-splitpanes spike (timeboxed, go/no-go) fails, fall back to B — the tree model carries over unchanged.** Serialized schema: versioned binary tree, leaves bind `sessionId`, ratios clamped [0.05, 0.95], invariants Zod-enforced in main per D1. PTY resize: continuous `fit()`, debounced `pty.resize` (150 ms / drag-end). Brief: `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-1.2-LayoutEngine.md` · Findings: `docs/architecture/CR-1.2-pane-layout-council-findings.md`. splitpanes 4.1.2 existence/recency verified on npm 2026-07-18. **Spike result (Task 1-3, 2026-07-19): GO** — xterm-in-splitpanes passed all 12 checks at window widths 1024/1440/2560: canvases paint, splitter/canvas layer cleanly (screenshotted), ResizeObserver on our pane containers fires mid-drag (21 callbacks sampled with the mouse button still held; ~7 `@resize` emits per drag), `fit()` yields plausible cols/rows (e.g. 1200 px → 144 cols, 861 px → 53 rows). v4.1.2 API verified from shipped typings/source (D4): `@resize` payload is `{event, index, prevPane, nextPane, panes: [{min,max,size}]}` fired per frame during drag, `resized` fires at drag-end — ratio write-back reads `panes[0].size / 100`, NOT the spec sketch's `sizes[]`. splitpanes@~4.1.2 installed. | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (council, unanimous) |

**Decisions from the Task 1-2 completion review** (`Tasks/Task-1-2-CompletionSummary.md`), ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D10 | **Session store rekey (`AgentKind` → `sessionId`) belongs to Task 1-4, as its first step.** Task 1-3 leaves the store per-kind and keys only *pane components* by `sessionId` — with one session per kind the agent-keying is still correct, and rekeying inside a layout task is unverifiable until multi-session exists. Task 1-4 cannot skip it: two Codex sessions sharing one `sessions['codex']` slot would break the task's own headline criterion. Task-1-4.md amended 2026-07-19 (its "Initial Starting Point" had wrongly claimed the rekey was already done). | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D11 | **`storage.updateSessionStatus` is wired in Task 1-4**, at `watchSessionExits` in `src/main/index.ts` — persisted status only becomes meaningful once 1-4/1-5 restore sessions on boot. Task 1-3 confirmed the symptom (F4: closed panes leave rows stuck at `status='running'`). **Action closed 2026-07-19** — `src/main/index.ts` admitted to Task-1-4.md's scope table for this single purpose, with a step and an acceptance criterion. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D12 | **Session rows are born `status='running'` before any PTY attaches.** Accepted as informational until D11 lands; no schema change. Nothing reads `sessions[].status` today. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D14 | **Renderer→main IPC payloads must be plain objects.** Pinia state is a Vue reactive Proxy and Electron's structured clone refuses it — `Error: An object could not be cloned`, with **no compile-time signal**. Found at runtime in Task 1-3 (`setLayout(this.tree)`); fixed by sending `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(tree))` from the store's persist path. Binds every task from here on: anything sourced from a store or `reactive()`/`ref()` gets snapshotted before crossing the bridge. **Promoted into `CLAUDE.md` 2026-07-19** (Matthew's approval at the 1-4 review). | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D13 | **`getPaneLayout` normalization is read-path, in-memory only** — a corrupted-but-parseable tree self-heals on the first `savePaneLayout`, not on read. Accepted: silent write-back on read would muddy the lazy-conversion semantics. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |

Also ratified from that review, no ID needed: `z.uuid()` over the Zod-4-deprecated `z.string().uuid()`; `layout:get` responses parsed outbound in main as well as inbound; first-run seeding of two default panes stands until Task 1-4 switches it to an empty layout (already carried in Task-1-4.md).

**Decision from the Task 1-4 completion review** (`Tasks/Task-1-4-CompletionSummary.md`), ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D15 | **Task 1-4's five deviations ratified as the session-lifecycle contract.** (1) `attach` carries an optional **`respawn` flag** — plain view attach *never* spawns (F5 makes attach re-run on sibling-close remounts; an ungated attach resurrected killed sessions); only the Restart chrome sends `respawn: true`, after kill + awaited exit. (2) On a respawn attach, main flips the row to `running`/null — D11 in both directions. (3) An attach for a manager-unknown id reports `status: 'exited'` **even if the persisted row says `running`** — the row supplies only the exit code; the manager's map is the sole liveness authority within a run (F6). (4) Recent-cwds + project root travel over a dedicated `session:launch-context` channel. (5) Settings stay inline-Drizzle per key (`getWindowBounds` pattern); no generic get/setSetting pair. **Post-restart Restart is deliberately a no-op** (`respawn` on an unknown id spawns nothing) — whether that button relaunches after restart is 1-5's restore-contract decision; the `respawn` gate is the seam. **⚠ Items (1)–(2) superseded by D16 (CR-1.5 Q4, 2026-07-19):** the `respawn` flag is removed in Task 1-5; *all* respawn routes through the launch path gated on an existing row, and `attach` becomes a pure view binding with no spawn path at all. Items (3)–(5) stand. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · partially superseded by D16 |

**Decision from Council Review CR-1.5** (the session restore contract), findings filed and ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D16 | **The session restore contract** (CR-1.5, 3-model council; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-1.5-RestoreContract.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilFindings-1.5-RestoreContract.md`). **Q1 — reconcile-on-boot (C), 2-of-3.** No schema change; `status='running'` read through a deterministic boot/activation reconcile pass; quit and crash converge by construction. *Gemini dissent preserved:* a `desired_state` intent column (B) is the cleaner model — adopt it when a user-facing "don't restore" toggle lands (Phase 2+). **Q2 — restore set, unanimous:** `{leaf.sessionId | leaf ∈ layout ∧ row.status='running'}`. Leaves∩exited → exited chrome + Restart; leaves-without-row → placeholder; **`running` rows without a leaf are healed to `exited` before any spawn** (the invisible-process guard). `session:delete` IPC ships (rejects live sessions). **Q3 — guarded-auto-relaunch, 2-of-3:** cwd `existsSync` before each spawn (missing → exited chrome, "Working directory not found"); 500 ms spawn stagger (250 ms if ConPTY tolerates); a transient "Session restarted — new conversation" badge (~5 s) on every restored pane; pane cap 16. *GPT dissent preserved:* affordance-driven ("Relaunch all") is more honest — the revert is renderer-only if auto proves confusing. **Q4 — unanimous:** Restart (in-run *and* post-restart) routes through a new `session:restart` channel → the launch path under the existing row id, with cwd re-validation; the D15 `respawn` attach flag is **removed** (see D15 supersession). **Coordinator resolutions (Matthew-approved 2026-07-19):** (a) `status='running'` is written **only after** spawn succeeds — supersedes findings Q4 step 3, per the findings' own Risk 1; (b) the "PID-prefix orphan scan" mitigation is dropped (contradicts the findings' own Q5 rejection of PID tracking); (c) the "Choose directory" re-homing action is trimmed to the not-found message — re-homing is Phase 2; (d) no context-menu/session-list UI — **pane close deletes the session row** after kill/exit completes (leafless rows are unreachable under the Q2 restore set, so close-flow deletion is the coherent cleanup); channels are singular (`session:delete`/`session:restart`); cwd-missing renders as its own chrome state, not a sentinel `exit_code=-1`; findings action item 7 (layout-tree migration) was already satisfied by `81e8a0b`. **Implemented verbatim in `fb384c5`** — the five-clause contract is in that commit's message; all 14 runtime steps individually verified (see `Tasks/Task-1-5-CompletionSummary.md`). **Note (F14, 1b-3):** `session:restart` emits no renderer event — restart drivers outside `TerminalPane` must flip the session store themselves; Gemini's `desired_state` dissent above remains the natural home for a future "don't restore" toggle. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `fb384c5` |
| D17 | **Task 1-5's six deviations ratified** (completion review, 2026-07-19). (1) **Consume-once restored badge** (F10) — a `restoredUnbadged` set consumed by first attach, replacing the sketched recency window: dev cold starts outlast any fixed timing window, and the boot event fires before any pane exists. (2) **`restore()` runs on every `project:select`**, not on tracked first-activation — `computeRestoreSet`'s live-guard makes re-runs idempotent, so the activation set was needless state. (3) `storage.getSessionById` added (restart reads a row without project context). (4) `src/main/db/schema.ts` touched **comment-only** ("respawns"→"re-creation") so the `grep -ri respawn src/` criterion returns literally nothing — no schema/migration change. (5) Stagger stays 500 ms (ConPTY never showed stress; the 250 ms condition never arose). (6) `restore()` is the one documented place the manager touches storage — the spec's own §4 sketch prescribed this; `launch`/`attach` keep the IPC-owns-rows division. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |

**Decisions at the Phase 1b kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-19):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D18 | **Title source: OSC + first-line fallback.** Titles come from terminal-title escape sequences (xterm `Terminal.onTitleChange`, OSC 0/2 — API confirmed in the installed `@xterm/xterm` 6 typings, re-verified at execution per D4); fallback = the first Enter-terminated line typed into the pane, only while the title is still null. OSC keeps updating live. **No LLM summarization** (Phase 3+, when BYOK keys exist). Whether the CLIs actually emit OSC titles is unverified until execution — the fallback is the guaranteed path; the implementer reports which mechanism fired per CLI. **Outcome (`a00af48`, mechanism report in the commit message) — the "honest unknown" is resolved:** **both CLIs emit OSC 0/2 at spawn, before any keystroke**, so the first-line fallback is effectively unreachable in a live pane for these two. Claude Code 2.1.215 sets `✳ Claude Code` and **animates a spinner in the title** while working (`⠂`/`⠐ Claude Code`, ~1 Hz, settling back to `✳`); Codex 0.144.6 sets the **cwd basename** (`Chorus`). The fallback was still exercised genuinely on an **exited** pane (no OSC source): captured a typed line, sanitized in the DB (raw shown in-renderer until next attach), ellipsis-truncated with full-text tooltip, survived restart seeded purely from the DB row. Debounce on real OSC traffic: 5 changes in ~3.4 s → 4 writes (correct trailing, no per-redraw flood). D14: zero `An object could not be cloned` across the flow. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · VERIFIED `a00af48` |
| D19 | **Migration v3: nullable `title` TEXT on `sessions`**, via the hand-rolled MIGRATIONS runner + Drizzle schema. "DB schema growth" is technically a §4 CR trigger; **council waived** for one trivially-reversible nullable column (coordinator recommendation, Matthew's call). Matches PLAN §269's target schema. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D20 | **View state per-project in `settings`** (key `view_state:<projectId>`, JSON `{mode, focusedSessionId}`) over a small Zod IPC. **Filmstrip is the default** (PLAN §183), including for existing DBs; grid is the alternate. `focusedSessionId` is never FK-checked — it legitimately goes stale (F4); views resolve staleness by falling back to the first leaf. **Implemented verbatim in 1b-2; all 10 runtime items individually verified** (filmstrip default on a no-row DB, byte-identical tree across focus clicks, F5 continued-output proof, per-project persistence across a tree-kill restart, stale-focus fallback with a hand-edited bogus id, cards never badge, multi-project isolation, zero structured-clone errors); the flagged `sessionInfoSchema` `exitCode` addition and the minimal grid-focus path are recorded in the commit message. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `3a28ec2` |
| D21 | **Palette skeleton = five commands** over an extensible registry: launch agent, switch project, focus pane (by title/agent), toggle filmstrip/grid, restart focused. In-repo fuzzy subsequence filter — **no new dependency**. Further commands, shortcuts (`Ctrl+T`/`Ctrl+1..9`/`Ctrl+Tab`), and MRU are later phases. **Outcome (`72ea471`):** five commands over the pure `buildCommands` registry shipped (focus/switch groups expand per leaf/project); the **capture-phase window hotkey won** (D4 report — verified over a focused xterm; `attachCustomKeyEventHandler` stays the unused fallback); **disabled commands are omitted** (`fuzzyFilter` returns `enabled()` only); the fuzzy subsequence filter lives in-repo with no new dependency; and the **F14 store-flip deviation** (palette restart flips the session store itself because `session:restart` emits no event) was ratified. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `72ea471` |

**Decisions at the Phase 2 kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-20):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D22 | **Phase 2 workspace modes: current working tree + new isolated worktree + existing worktree.** The launch dialog ships three modes — current tree (default for a lone agent), new isolated worktree (default when a 2nd agent targets a repo another live session is already writing to), and an existing-worktree picker (re-attach to a retained worktree). **Read-only is deferred** to Phase 3+ — unenforceable for PTY agents until adapter permission modes exist (PLAN §12); shipping it as a label without enforcement misrepresents a guarantee. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D23 | **Worktree location & branch convention.** Worktrees live OUTSIDE the repo in a sibling dir: `<repo-parent>\.chorus\<repo-name>\<short-session-id>`; branches are `chorus/<repo-name>/<short-session-id>`. Outside-the-repo keeps agents and repo-scanning tools from recursing into sibling checkouts; the PLAN §5 `role` segment is dropped until Phase 3 introduces roles. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D24 | **F15 lands as a flagged chore commit at the start of the Task 2-1 execution session** — a separate micro-commit fixing the `main.css` unlayered reset (drop it or move it into a layer; Tailwind preflight already resets), then the task's own commit. G3 amended for this one chore (precedent: `de98679` standalone fix commit). The visual effect (app-wide margins/paddings return to designed values) is verified in that session's G2 runtime pass. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D25 | **F14 deferred with a recorded note.** The restart-event asymmetry (`session:restart` emits no `session:restored`) stays as-is; the decision belongs to the phase that next ADDS a restart driver. Phase 2 as scoped adds none. F14's documentation in §5 remains the binding warning for any interim driver. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |

**Decision from Council Review CR-2.0** (worktree lifecycle & crash reconciliation), findings filed 2026-07-20:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D26 | **The worktree lifecycle contract** (CR-2.0; 3-model council Kimi K2.7 / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT 5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-2.0-WorktreeLifecycle.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-2.0-Findings.md`). **Unanimous 3-of-3 on all four questions.** **Q1 — hybrid owned-with-retention (C):** a worktree is created by and attributed to a session but outlives it by design; session close offers removal only when clean; a dirty close always detaches (`status='detached'`, session link cleared, surfaced in a retained-worktree list, never silently destroyed); `session:delete` detaches rather than cascades. **Q2 — DB-first journaled creation:** states `creating → provisioning → active → detached → removing`; the row is inserted before any filesystem/git operation; the path derives deterministically from the worktree id; `active` only after full success. **Q3 — boot reconcile runs after storage init and BEFORE D16 restore (awaited),** classifying all five populations (row × git-entry × directory) with keep/promote/surface/delete rules; `git worktree prune` only ever after user confirmation, never automatic. **Q4 — destruction:** clean-enough-to-auto-remove = empty `git status --porcelain`; committed-but-unmerged work never blocks removal (object store keeps it; the branch ref stays); **`--force` is never used in any code path**; dirty removal requires typed confirmation showing the dirty file list; branches are never auto-deleted (explicit opt-in checkbox only). **Coordinator resolutions (a–h, flagged at findings review 2026-07-20):** (a) both pointer columns ship per PLAN §13, but `worktrees.session_id` is authoritative and both are written in one synchronous transaction; (b) reconcile classifies by EVIDENCE first (git entry × directory), journal status second — `creating`/`provisioning` rows with a valid entry + directory are promoted (to `active` when the owning session row stands, else `detached`), closing the creating-with-git-entry crash gap the findings' Q2 rules skipped; (c) population-4 adoption rows are born `detached`, not the findings' `active` (no owning session = detached under the council's own state model); (d) population 2's "session still alive" branch is vacuous (reconcile runs pre-restore, when nothing is alive) — the rule collapses to surface-as-prune-candidate; (e) `removing` crash rule (unspecified in findings): re-classify by evidence — git entry and directory both gone → delete row; anything remaining → revert to `detached` and surface; (f) the auto-worktree trigger is precisely: the launch dialog's mode DEFAULT flips to new-worktree when ≥1 other LIVE session's cwd resolves to the same repo root (computed in main, delivered via `session:launch-context`); the chosen mode always travels explicitly in the launch payload — main never silently overrides (aligns findings action 4 with D22); (g) the retained-worktree list ships as a minimal overlay dialog (LaunchDialog idiom) + palette command — NOT a settings panel (none exists; jumping ahead to settings screens is barred by CLAUDE.md); columns per findings risk 6 (path, branch, clean/dirty, Remove); (h) the D23 `<short-session-id>` path/branch segment becomes the WORKTREE row's short id — worktrees outlive sessions, so a session-derived name goes stale on detach/re-attach; D23's sibling-dir location and `chorus/` prefix stand. Findings risk 5's rename scheme is superseded by (h) + D23; retry-with-fresh-suffix on `git worktree add` collision is kept as cheap defense. **Amendments at the Phase-2 doc review (Matthew, 2026-07-20):** (i) **clause 7 amended** — `--force` is never used EXCEPT inside the single dirty-removal path, after main's typed-confirmation gate has passed: a targeted `git worktree remove --force <path>` on the confirmed worktree only. The drafting-stage no-force workaround (Node `fs.rm` + repo-wide `git worktree prune`) is REJECTED — `prune` is repo-wide and would silently resolve other surfaced prune-candidates without their own confirmation, and bespoke recursive deletion is a worse data-loss surface than git's own targeted, validated removal. (j) Branch deletion (the opt-in checkbox) runs `git branch -d`; an unmerged refusal is surfaced, and `-D` escalation requires the same typed-confirmation acknowledgment. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (council, unanimous) |


**Product decision (Matthew, 2026-07-20):**

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D27 | **Council Review becomes a native, first-class Chorus feature (new Phase 3b).** Matthew's Cursor/Kilo-Code multi-LLM council workflow (§4) is productized: 3–5 council members (highly capable, cheaper models) independently review a brief, discuss, and on disagreement a designated **frontier arbiter** rules on the better idea. OpenRouter-first configuration — per-member API key, base URL, model identifier, params — via a real configuration UI. **More than a skill:** invoked from a pane/window (palette command); input = the path to a brief `.md`; output = a findings `.md` (findings, risks, dissents, action items — the CR-x.y format already in §6 use), which a frontier model (e.g. Fable) then reviews as coordinator. **Placement rationale:** depends on Phase 3's DPAPI vault (member keys are credentials, never plaintext config), `provider_configs`/`credential_profiles`, and the api-mode adapter path — hence Phase **3b**. New scope relative to PLAN §14 (which lists council models only as an OpenRouter route); recorded here per the §2 authority split. Until 3b lands, §4 CR runs stay external. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (product decision, Matthew) |

**Decisions at the Phase 3 kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-21):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D28 | **Phase 3 is scoped to its own stated milestone.** The provisional §7 line listed ten deliverables; the milestone ("agents launch with injected BYOK credentials, keys never touching args/logs/transcripts") needs four. **Ships in Phase 3:** safeStorage/DPAPI vault · `credential_profiles` + provider configs · `AgentAdapter` interface + capabilities · provider/credential settings UI · env-var injection into child PTYs (supersedes D5) · pino secret-redacting logging · a single-probe test-key. **Moves to a new provisional Phase 3a — Profiles & Catalog:** `model_catalog` + refresh, effort normalization (Fast/Balanced/Deep/Max), `launch_profiles`, `usage_records` capture. Rationale: roughly double the milestone's requirement in one phase, and the security-critical vault should not share execution sessions with UI polish. `usage_records` in particular has no honest producer until an api-mode adapter exists — PTY agents emit no token counts. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D29 | **Phase 3 ships Chorus's first real Settings view.** `CLAUDE.md`'s "do not jump ahead to UI/settings screens" barred building settings *before their phase*; PLAN §14 places "provider/credential settings" in Phase 3, so this arrives on schedule rather than ahead of it. D26(g)'s overlay-not-settings-panel ruling was correct **for Phase 2** and is not a precedent against this. Consequences: `App.vue` gains a workspace ⇄ settings view switch — Chorus's first navigation concept — which Phase 3b's council configuration UI (D27) then inherits instead of inventing a second one. **The overlay idiom is not abandoned:** `LaunchDialog.vue` and `WorktreePanel.vue` stay overlays; only durable configuration moves into the view. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D30 | **`pino` is added as a dependency** — PLAN §2 names it in the locked stack; `CLAUDE.md`'s list did not, hence the ask. It arrives with a **redacting serializer** (declared redact paths **plus** a key-shape regex scrub over free-text messages, since pino's `redact` covers object paths only) in **Task 3-1 — before any secret exists in the codebase**, so the vault is written into an already-safe logging environment rather than retrofitted. File rotation is **deferred to Phase 7** with the installer work. `pino` is the ONLY new runtime dependency approved for Phase 3; anything else needs a fresh ask. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D31 | **Two council briefs, both issued before coding.** `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault.md` (vault security — storage shape, decrypt lifetime, injection surface, failure modes, redaction coverage incl. the PTY-stream question) and `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` (interface surface, pty/api split, capability granularity, `detectState`, registry/identity). Task docs for 3-2 and 3-3 — and therefore 3-4/3-5, which consume their shapes — are authored **after** findings are filed and ratified as **D33** and **D34**. The two questions have little overlap, and the adapter is the interface Phases 3a/3b/4/6 all build on; a combined brief would shortchange whichever the council found less interesting. **Status update 2026-07-22:** CR-3.0 findings filed and recorded as **D33** (coordinator resolutions a–e); its interface-relevant outcomes were folded into the CR-3.1 brief's §3 before that council ran. CR-3.1 findings filed the same day and recorded as **D34** (coordinator resolutions a–f). **Both gates CLOSED — Tasks 3-2…3-5 are clear to author at the re-kickoff.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 · BOTH CRs CLOSED 2026-07-22 |
| D32 | **F21 and F23 land as a flagged chore commit at the start of Task 3-1** (precedent: D24's F15 chore in Task 2-1, and the standalone `de98679` fix commit). **G3 is amended for that one session: 3-1 makes two commits** — the chore, then the logging-spine task commit. Both are inherited defects that belong in neither the logging narrative nor a five-task wait; F23 is a live session-loss path. **F21's fix is a main-side gate only** — a distinct `branchForceConfirmation` naming the BRANCH, separate from the dirty-removal `confirmation` naming the path, because one token cannot honestly license two different destructions. No renderer affordance is added, leaving `-D` with zero callers (the dormant state `--force` sat in between Tasks 2-1 and 2-3). | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D33 | **The vault security contract** (CR-3.0; 3-model council Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault-Findings.md`). Council verdict REVISE, resolved into a committed contract at filing. **Q1 — envelope blob (B), 3-of-3:** encrypt `{key, baseUrl?, extraHeaders?}` via safeStorage/DPAPI; plaintext metadata columns (`provider_id`, `label`, `fingerprint_hash`, `created_at`, `last_verified_at`); salted SHA-256 fingerprint in the clear (unanimous). **Q2:** decrypt-per-launch with variable isolation (C, 2-of-3); constructed allow-list env (unanimous — scope pinned by resolution (c)); **write-only inbound IPC** — no channel ever returns a key or fingerprint (unanimous); launch names a `credential_profile_id`, main resolves and decrypts (unanimous). **Q3 — refuse, never degrade (unanimous):** encryption unavailable → refuse creation; undecryptable/corrupt blob → mark `unavailable_since`, keep the row, refuse launch naming the profile by label only; `shouldReEncrypt` → re-encrypt on next write, throttled via `reencrypted_at`. **Q4:** full redaction coverage (structured paths + free-text scrub + Error messages/stacks + IPC-if-logged + crash artifacts + SQL); **PTY stream: SCRUB ON INGEST, 2-of-3** — a per-session exact-value streaming scrubber runs before ring-buffer append, renderer replay, and any future transcript write, replacing with `[REDACTED-CREDENTIAL]`, chunk-boundary carry-over required. *Qwen dissent preserved:* the PTY stream is an agent-controlled surface and accept-and-document would be honest; rejected by majority on Constraint-1 grounds (the ring buffer and transcripts are Chorus-controlled). **Q6:** no alternative shape load-bearing. **Coordinator resolutions (a)–(e), Matthew-ratified 2026-07-22:** (a) **clauses 4 and 7 of the filed contract contradict each other** — exact-match scrubbing REQUIRES retaining the injected plaintext for the session's lifetime; clause 4 gains that carve-out (the per-session scrubber match set: main memory only, never persisted, cleared on session end), and the crash-dump exposure window widening from milliseconds to session lifetime is a NAMED LIMIT — sound without a council round-trip because a heap-reading attacker can already read the child's environment block (same excluded threat class; longer duration, no new class); (b) **fingerprints are MAIN-SIDE ONLY** — the council's own clause 3 bars them from IPC, making clause 2's "UI can display" rationale impossible as written; their purpose becomes duplicate detection at creation ("already stored as profile X") and rotation detection; the mandatory label disambiguates in the UI; (c) **the allow-list env applies ONLY to credential-bearing launches** — a no-profile launch inherits `process.env` wholesale per clause 9/Constraint 2 (exactly today's behavior; ambient keys riding along on a no-profile launch is today's behavior and stays); the council's mandated allow-list test against both installed CLIs is a hard step of the injection task, with the findings' Q6 fallback if an agent breaks; (d) **the honest guarantee gains a Test-key carve-out** ("…except the explicit Test-key action, which sends an authentication probe to the provider's own API at your request") — the brief omitted the test-key feature entirely, a coordinator error flagged as such; the council could not have known; (e) **spec notes** (no contract change): scrubber carry-over bounded at maxSecretLen−1 bytes with a short timer flush (a TUI pausing mid-prefix must not stall rendering); `shouldReEncrypt` is reported only by `decryptStringAsync`, so rotation detection needs the async API (D4-verify against installed Electron typings); provider-level plaintext `base_url`/`extra_headers_json` vs the envelope's encrypted fields need an explicit precedence rule (envelope overrides provider default) and the plaintext provider-level values are documented non-secret. **Scope note:** the scrub ruling adds a streaming scrubber at SessionManager ingest that was not in the phase sketch — task placement (inside 3-5 vs its own task) is decided at the post-councils re-kickoff. Interface-relevant outcomes were folded into `CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` §3 before CR-3.1 ran: allow-list + adapter-declared env vars, the `env_var_name` placement question, secret-distinguishable launch payloads (scrubber registration), optional credential in `buildLaunch`. | RESOLVED 2026-07-22 (council majority + coordinator resolutions, Matthew-ratified) |
| D34 | **The `AgentAdapter` interface contract** (CR-3.1; 3-model council Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` — run in its 2026-07-22 amended form carrying the D33 seam facts; findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface-Findings.md`). Council verdict REVISE resolved into a committed shape at filing. **Q1 — narrow core + capability-gated extension interfaces (C), 3-of-3:** always-implemented `BaseAgentAdapter` (`id`, `displayName`, `executionMode`, `detectInstallation`, `getAuthMethods`, `getCapabilities`); domain concerns live on optional extension interfaces (`SupportsMcp`, `SupportsHooks`, `SupportsResume`) callers narrow to via type guards — "supported" and "implemented" become the same fact. **Q2 — discriminated union, 3-of-3:** `PtyAgentAdapter \| ApiAgentAdapter`; the session level eventually splits the same way, but **Phase 3 restructures only the adapter side** — `SessionManager` stays PTY-only. **Q3 — descriptors over booleans, 3-of-3:** `reasoningEffort: EffortDescriptor \| null`, `mcp: McpDescriptor \| null`, etc.; booleans only for truly binary facts; each descriptor carries `mode: 'static' \| 'dynamic'`, with dynamic ones populated by `detectInstallation()` through a `mergeCapabilities(base, detected)` utility. **Q4 — `detectState` OUT, 2-of-3:** removed from the interface entirely; Phase 4 designs its own buffered, stateful interpreter if hooks prove insufficient. *Qwen dissent preserved:* declaring a stateful interpreter seam now would cost little and give Phase 4 a concrete target. **Q5 — 3-of-3:** the adapter `id` IS the persisted `sessions.agent` value (no mapping layer); a static frozen registry for Phase 3 with a registration seam deferred to Phase 6; unknown persisted ids refuse cleanly, never crash. **Q6:** no alternative shape load-bearing. **Coordinator resolutions (a)–(f), Matthew-ratified 2026-07-22:** (a) **the findings' verbatim TS contradicted its own Q4 majority** by declaring `SupportsStateDetection`/`OutputInterpreter` (the outvoted Qwen position, even named inconsistently vs the synthesis text) — both are STRUCK from Phase 3's declaration set; the majority ruling stands; (b) **the `AgentKind` derivation flips to respect the process boundary** — the findings derived the type from the main-process registry (`keyof typeof staticRegistry`), but shared/renderer code can never import main-side modules, and the TS as written had the explicit `Record<string, …>` annotation widening `keyof` to `string` anyway; instead `agentKindSchema` in `src/shared/ipc.ts` remains the wire authority and the registry is typed **`Record<AgentKind, AgentAdapter>`** via a type-only import — same single source of truth, statically-forced exact coverage, no boundary violation; (c) **the unknown-agent rule is mapped onto the schema that exists** — there is no `'failed'` status (`running \| exited` only; PLAN §4's machine is Phase 4+) and no notification center until Phase 4; unknown-agent rows flow through the EXISTING D16 spawn-failure heal path (`getAdapterOrThrow` throws → caught → healed to `exited` → pino-logged → exited chrome), and `session:restart` swaps its `agentKindSchema.parse(row.agent)` throw for a registry lookup returning `{ok:false, reason}` inline; (d) **env policy has ONE owner: main** — the findings' `PtyLaunchRequest.env` carried the full environment (each adapter re-implementing the D33 allow-list) while `requiredEnvVars` separately existed so main could build the same list; instead `buildLaunch` contributes adapter-specific additions + `secretEnv`, and main's single launch path composes the child env per D33 (wholesale inherit for no-credential; baseline + `requiredEnvVars` + additions for BYOK) **[⚠ clause (d)'s parenthetical AMENDED 2026-07-27 by D89 — `envAdditions` applies on BOTH branches. The wording above was written when `envAdditions` had one producer that always declared `{}`, so "additions for BYOK" described a distinction with no observable difference; Task 3a-5's `launch_profiles.env_json` made it observable and broke on it.]**; `cols`/`rows` are session geometry, not adapter knowledge — they stay with `SessionManager`; (e) **`env_var_name` precedence** (the amended brief's fact-2 question, accommodated but never ruled): the adapter's `AuthMethodDefinition.requiredEnvVar` is the default, a nullable `provider_configs.env_var_name` overrides it for custom/OpenAI-compat endpoints, main composes `ResolvedCredential.envVarName`; (f) **removing the renderer's hardcoded `labels`/`AGENT_KINDS` requires display data on the wire** — `cli:detect`'s response gains adapter-supplied `displayName` (+ an agent flag), additive and required-nullable per house style; without it the capabilities-not-names principle dies on day one. **Spec notes:** the findings' type guards do not compile as written (`a.capabilities` property vs the base's `getCapabilities()` method — the 3-3 spec picks one shape and the guards follow); PTY model listing arrives in Phase 3a as an extension interface (its absence from `PtyAgentAdapter` is deliberate); `InstallationStatus.authenticated` stays unset in 3-3 (an auth probe would break the behavior-neutral gate). The 3-3 ImplementationSpec authored at re-kickoff is the **normative** interface text; the findings' TS is its strong draft. | RESOLVED 2026-07-22 (council + coordinator resolutions, Matthew-ratified) |

**Decision at the Phase 3 re-kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-22):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D35 | **The D33 PTY scrubber gets its own task, placed BEFORE injection — Phase 3 grows from five tasks to six.** D33's Q4 majority added scope the phase sketch never contained: a per-session streaming exact-value scrubber at `SessionManager` ingest, with bounded carry-over and a timer flush. It becomes **Task 3-5**; BYOK env injection + test-key becomes **Task 3-6**; Tasks 3-2/3-3/3-4 keep the numbering already published in `Phase-3-Overview.md`, the §7 phase table, and D33/D34 themselves. **Rationale:** (1) it preserves the operating principle Task 3-1 established — redaction machinery lands *before* the secret it protects against exists, so the secret is written into an already-safe environment rather than retrofitted into an audited-by-nobody one; (2) folding it into 3-6 would have put the launch-payload change, credential resolution, allow-list env composition, adapter wiring, a streaming scrubber, the launch-dialog UI, and a live network probe into a single commit — the phase's largest and most security-critical session; (3) split, each half gets its own runtime proof (3-5 against a planted fake value with no vault involved, 3-6 against a scrubber already known to work); and (4) renumbering 3-2…3-4 would have churned documents that are already ratified. Rejected alternatives: folding into 3-5 (five tasks, no renumbering, but one overloaded session), and landing the scrubber immediately after 3-1 (strongest form of the principle, but renumbers everything downstream). **Consequence:** Task 3-5 ships a registration seam with **zero callers**, verified via temporary instrumentation reverted before commit (the Task 2-4 precedent), and Task 3-6 is its one legal caller. | RESOLVED 2026-07-22 |

|---|---|---|

The three: **(a) F24** — a pino `serializers.err` entry applying `scrubSecrets` to serialized Error `message` + `stack` (closes the gap vs D33 redaction item 3; latent today, real exposure at 3-6's fetch errors); **(b)** a provider-scoped duplicate-fingerprint check in `CredentialVault.replaceProfile` (3-2 implementer proposal F-4 — creation-time detection exists, replace bypasses it; the check must exempt the profile's OWN row so a same-key rotation still works); **(c)** `.strict()` on `credentialProfileMetaSchema` (3-2 F-5b — makes the "fails loudly" prose literal; the shipped clause-3 test's assertion flips from stripped-output to throws, which is the point). Rationale for placement: all three are one-to-three-line changes to freshly-landed 3-2 surface, cheap now and awkward later; none belongs in the adapter refactor's narrative, and 3-3 is the next session to open. Kept OUT of the chore: any change to `unavailable_since` semantics (F-5a stays contract-literal — clears on replace/re-encrypt writes only). | RESOLVED 2026-07-23 |

**Decision at the Task 3-3 completion review** (Matthew, 2026-07-23):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D37 | **F25 lands as a flagged chore commit at the start of Task 3-4's execution session** (precedent: D24, D32, D36). **G3 is amended for that one session: 3-4 makes two commits** — the chore, then the renderer-only Settings task. The fix: the `layout:get` handler filters unknown-agent rows out of its `sessions[]` projection via a `getAdapter` registry lookup, logging one pino **warn** naming the filtered row id and agent value; the affected leaf then renders `LayoutRenderer`'s existing leaf-without-row placeholder — the honest degraded state, and D34(c) extended to the layout-view layer. The leaf is NOT removed from the tree and the row is NOT healed or deleted by this path (reconcile/restore own row state; the projection only decides what the renderer sees). **Rejected alternative:** widening `sessionInfoSchema.agent` to `z.string()` — it ripples raw unknown strings into every renderer consumer (`TerminalPane`'s label lookup renders blank) for no Phase-3 benefit. Runtime proof required in BOTH view modes (filmstrip and grid), with the hand-edited row restored afterwards. | RESOLVED 2026-07-23 |

**Product decision at the arrival of the Claude Design mockups** (Matthew, 2026-07-23):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D38 | **The Claude Design mockups in `docs/design/` are Chorus's visual north star, adopted in two stages.** Seven screens (Workspace, Attention Inbox, Launch Dialog, Settings — Providers & Keys, Micro-surfaces, Startup, plus an Overview with a **system crib sheet**: colorblind-safe state vocabulary dot+shape — green dot running / amber diamond needs-you / red triangle error / gray square done; project identity by hue only; agent identity by glyph only, never color; jade `#3BCFAE` app accent deliberately split from signal-green; a five-surface elevation ladder `#0A0B0D → #12151A`; Archivo for UI + JetBrains Mono for data). **Stage 1 — skeletons now:** where a Phase-3 task builds a screen the design covers, it builds the design's LAYOUT SKELETON in the app's current neutral idiom (first application: Task 3-4's settings shell — left settings nav + grouped provider cards with nested credential rows + Esc-to-close). **Stage 2 — the visual system via a new provisional Phase 3c — Design Adoption** (§7), deliberately placed BEFORE Phase 4's chrome expansion: design tokens, both fonts **bundled locally** (never Google's CDN from an Electron app), the frameless custom titlebar (a `BrowserWindow` change), the left project rail replacing `ProjectTabs`, the right filmstrip rail, the status bar, and the state-vocabulary components. **Two standing override rules:** (1) **ratified security contracts beat the mock** — the Settings mock shows masked key hints (`sk-ant-…Xq4F`), which D33 clause 3 forbids; the hint column renders the auth method instead, and no hint of any fidelity is ever built; (2) **the mock's future-phase content is not license to build early** — it deliberately depicts 3a (catalog, defaults, costs), 3-6 (test-key), 4 (attention inbox), 5 (mission control, push-to-talk), 6 (neo4j), and 7 (pop-out) surfaces; each arrives with its own phase. The mock's own "open questions for iteration" (filmstrip right-rail vs bottom, pulse strength, inbox mode vs overlay, mission-control orientation, card width) are Phase-3c kickoff questions. | RESOLVED 2026-07-23 |

**Product decision (Matthew, 2026-07-23):**

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D39 | **Sub-agent visibility is read-only awareness, folded into Phase 4 — not separate sessions, not per-sub-agent control.** When a PTY agent spins up sub-agents (e.g. Claude Code's Task tool), those children run **in-process inside the one CLI** — not new OS processes, not new PTYs. Chorus's unit of isolation is the top-level session (one PTY owned by `SessionManager`), and it sees only that session's scrubbed byte stream; there is **no semantic channel** into the child structure (D34(a) struck `OutputInterpreter`/`SupportsStateDetection` from the adapter interface).
