So today sub-agents appear **only as inline terminal output in the parent pane** — no separate container, no independent stop/watch/interact. **Chosen: Option 2 — surface sub-agent lifecycle as read-only events via Phase 4's already-planned hook bus** (`agent_events` + the per-session event timeline sidebar): the parent pane can show "N sub-agents running / done" and list children in the timeline. This is *watching*, riding infrastructure Phase 4 already builds — no new process model, no output demuxing. **Explicitly OUT of scope:** (a) representing sub-agents as separate panes/containers (would require demuxing one interleaved byte stream — exactly the interpretation D34 declined); (b) per-sub-agent stop/interact (infeasible for in-process children absent a CLI control API, and adjacent to the "orchestration roles" §8 bars from v1). The honest path to real per-agent isolation/control already exists: **launch each agent as its own top-level Chorus session** (N concurrent PTYs, each with its own worktree and controls). **Caveat (D4):** the exact Claude Code sub-agent hook surface (event names) is verified against the CLI's own docs when Phase 4 is built, never asserted from memory now. | RESOLVED 2026-07-23 (product decision, Matthew) |

**Decision at the Task 3-5 completion review** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D40 | **Task 3-5's commit boundary is a ratified G3 deviation: the code lives in a docs commit, narrated by a fileless anchor.** Mid-session, the three scope files (`scrubber.ts`, `scrubber.test.ts`, `sessionManager.ts`) were swept into **`d3b6f30` "Design and Phase 3 docs"** by an add-all-style commit that was **pushed before runtime verification finished**. Rewriting pushed history to split it was **rejected**; instead `ddb5454` (`--allow-empty`) carries the Task 3-5 narrative and points at `d3b6f30` for the diff. **What makes this safe rather than merely tidy:** the committed content is byte-identical to the fully verified state — `git diff d3b6f30 -- src/` is **empty**, independently re-confirmed by the coordinator both before and after a full instrumentation cycle, so every gate and all eight runtime items provably ran against exactly what is committed. G3 ("one intentional narrated commit per execution session") is satisfied in substance (one commit, fully narrated) but not in form (the commit that carries the code is not the commit that carries the narrative). **Standing consequence:** `d3b6f30` is a mixed docs+code commit, so anyone bisecting or reading Phase 3 history must not assume docs commits are code-free. **Rule going forward:** do not `git add -A` while an execution session's verification is still open — stage the scope files explicitly, and never push a task's code before its runtime pass concludes. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Product decision — new feature admitted** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D41 | **Mission Control is admitted to the roadmap as provisional Phase 8 (POST-v1), with its telemetry slice pulled forward into Phase 3a.** Spec: `docs/Features/Mission Control/chorus-mission-control-spec.md` — authoritative on design; §7's Phase 8 entry records only placement, dependencies, and conflicts. A CPM scheduler over a committed task-graph seed with attention-minutes and tokens as extra cost dimensions and the agent fleet as the resource pool; **derived, never stored** is its organising principle. Captured now, at Matthew's request, so it is not lost — explicitly **"low priority — must not derail current phase work"** (spec §Priority), which this placement enforces structurally by putting it after Phase 7. **Four things this admission forces, none of which the spec could know:** (a) **§8 is amended, not contradicted** — "Task board / card-dispatch; automation scheduler" was an unqualified v1 exclusion and Mission Control is literally that; it now reads as a v1 boundary revisited in Phase 8, and nothing before Phase 7 may build toward it beyond the Phase-3a telemetry. (b) **A naming collision exists today** — Phase 5's "mission-control overlay", D38's mock inventory, and Phase 3c's "mission-control orientation" question all denote an always-on-top **pane switcher**, an unrelated feature; recommendation is that this feature keeps the name and the overlay is renamed (e.g. Fleet Switcher), pending Matthew's ratification. (c) **The spec's open question 1 is already answered** by verified ground facts — session identity is the stable `sessions` row id surviving restart — with two caveats the spec must absorb: a restored session is a fresh conversation, and pane close deletes the row, so dispatch records cannot assume referential integrity. (d) **The spec resolves a blocker this roadmap had already recorded** — Phase 3a's `usage_records` was parked on "needs an api-mode producer, PTY agents emit no token counts"; per-dispatch LiteLLM virtual keys are that producer, so the two are **one piece of work**, built in 3a, with `tokens_cached` tracked separately. **Sequencing rationale for the pull-forward:** historical actuals cannot be backfilled, so every week without capture is a week the estimator cannot calibrate; the capture is ~half a day and has no UI. **Not yet decided:** whether Phase 8 gets its own `docs/Features/Mission Control/roadmap.md` and kickoff (the per-feature pattern) or stays a phase of this master roadmap — revisit when it is next in view, not now. **LiteLLM is a new external dependency** outside the locked stack and needs an explicit ask per `CLAUDE.md` before any code depends on it. **⚠ SUPERSEDED IN PART, same day, by D42:** item (d)'s producer is **OpenRouter, not LiteLLM**, and the dependency ask is therefore **moot** — OpenRouter is a base URL + key over `fetch`, requiring nothing outside the locked stack. Everything else in D41 stands. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 (product decision, Matthew) · (d) superseded by D42 |

**Decisions from the gateway & model-identity discussion** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D42 | **OpenRouter is Chorus's single gateway. LiteLLM is dropped, and token attribution is keyed on AUTH MODE rather than on the gateway.** **Verified against OpenRouter's own docs this session (D4, not training memory):** the Provisioning API (`POST`/`GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/v1/keys`, authenticated by a separate Management key) mints keys programmatically with a `limit` credit cap, auto-disables them on exceed with optional daily/weekly/monthly reset, and reports `usage`, `usage_daily|weekly|monthly`, `limit_remaining` and BYOK variants. That covers **all three** things the Mission Control spec wanted from LiteLLM — attribution, budget guardrail, revocation — so LiteLLM is not required. **The decider is consolidation, not capability:** D27 already commits Phase 3b's council to "OpenRouter-first configuration", and running two gateways for one job is unjustified. The cost argument also collapses — OpenRouter BYOK costs 5% of the equivalent OpenRouter price with the first 1M BYOK requests/month free. **Knowingly given up:** LiteLLM's ability to front local/self-hosted models (Ollama, vLLM), to keep the request path off a third party, to work offline, and its richer routing modes. None is a v1 requirement, but **local models would resurrect the question**, so the gateway client must sit behind a narrow interface — swapping it should be one module, not a redesign. **Attribution keyed on auth mode:** routing a subscription-authenticated agent through *any* gateway converts a flat-rate subscription into per-token API billing, so a cost-tracking feature would *increase* cost. `AuthMethodDefinition.type` (`'subscription' \| 'api_key'`), shipped per-adapter in 3-3, is the discriminator — `api_key` sessions get a per-dispatch OpenRouter key; `subscription` sessions are **never** gateway-routed and are metered from the CLI's own local session logs, accepting lower fidelity to keep the flat rate. Track **"% of spend attributed"** so the gap is visible. This encodes Matthew's standing intent: never run Claude or Codex on per-token billing; on hitting a 5-hour or weekly subscription limit, **switch model, not billing mode**. **Operational note for the builder:** OpenRouter's **Management API key is a distinct, higher-privilege credential class** — it mints keys but cannot do inference — so it needs D33-grade vault protection at least equal to a provider key, and neither `provider_configs` nor `credential_profiles` has a slot for it today. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D43 | **The launchable unit is the triple (agent × route × model); `launch_profiles` carry an immutable id and a renameable display label.** A model is not one thing in Chorus — "DeepSeek via OpenRouter" and "DeepSeek direct" are distinct entries that merely share a model name, and the user selects by friendly name. **The route half already exists and needs no schema change:** `provider_configs` (`name`, `adapter_type`, `auth_mode`, `env_var_name`, `base_url`, `extra_headers_json`) *is* the route, and `name` is already user-authored. **Subscription routes are first-class rows, not a special case:** a `provider_config` with `auth_mode='subscription'`, `base_url` NULL and **zero** `credential_profiles` — representable today because the FK runs profile→provider, not the reverse — which matches D33 clause 9's ruling that subscription launches are the first-class path, not a fallback. The model half is Phase 3a's `model_catalog`; the pairing is its `launch_profiles`, so **the friendly-name picker IS the launch-profile picker**. **Stable id vs display label is load-bearing, not cosmetic:** Mission Control's routing policy is a *committed* file and dispatch records persist, so anything that stores a reference stores the **id**, while the label — defaulted to `<provider name>/<model display name>` — stays freely renameable. Renaming a profile must never break a committed file (the same lesson as the seed's stable task ids). **Labels are user data on user-created rows**, which keeps CR-3.1's "capabilities, not provider names" rule intact; hardcoding a friendly-name list in the renderer would violate it. **Known gap, must not be designed around silently:** `SessionManager` is PTY-only (D34 Q2) and `ApiAgentAdapter` has zero implementations, so an api-route profile is launchable today *only* as a CLI pointed at an OpenAI-compatible endpoint via base URL + key + model flag (the D34(e) case). **Whether any installed CLI accepts BOTH a custom base URL AND an arbitrary model id is unverified — added to Task 3-6's D4 list.** If none does, api-route profiles wait for api-mode sessions. **Naming:** avoid a `Native/` prefix — "native" already denotes `claude.exe`'s native exe versus the npm shim; prefer `Direct/`. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Decisions from the native-CLI / OpenRouter session-model discussion** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D44 | **Chorus has exactly three NATIVE CLI agents — Claude Code, Codex, and Kimi CLI — and all three are SUBSCRIPTION-ONLY by product policy. Per-token BYOK targets everything else, reached through OpenRouter.** Standing intent: these three are the workhorses and always run on their flat-rate subscriptions; on hitting a 5-hour or weekly limit the answer is to **switch model, never to switch billing mode**. **This dissolves two premise-threatening unknowns in Task 3-6:** Step 1a (does an injected `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` beat an existing OAuth login?) and Step 1b (does Codex read `OPENAI_API_KEY` at runtime?) become **moot rather than merely unresolved**, because Chorus never injects a key into those CLIs *for those models*. The reasoning stays in the task docs as history rather than being deleted. **⚠ Capability is NOT policy — do not collapse them.** `claudeAdapter` and `codexAdapter` must keep honest `getAuthMethods()`/`getCapabilities()` declarations of what the binary *can* accept; the policy lives at the **route** level, where a `provider_config` for Anthropic-direct simply never gets created (D43). Setting `apiKey: false` on those adapters would both lie about the binary and block the vehicle below. **Critically, the CLI and the model are separate axes (D43):** "Claude Code the binary" is not "Claude the model", so pointing an installed CLI at OpenRouter to drive a non-Claude/non-GPT model **is** BYOK and costs Anthropic and OpenAI nothing per token. That — not an Anthropic-direct key — is where Phase 3's BYOK milestone gets its vehicle. **Kimi CLI is a NEW third adapter and is not free:** per the standing constraint in Phase 6 (from the 3-4 review), `agentKindSchema` and the static registry **must widen together**, since widening one alone reintroduces F25's `layout:get` fragility exactly. Placement of that work is a kickoff decision, not settled here. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D45 | **Chorus will support BOTH pane types — an agent CLI pointed at OpenRouter (PTY) and a native api-mode chat pane — sequenced agent-CLI-first, with four mitigations accepted as BINDING constraints rather than later cleanup.** A raw OpenRouter model **is not an agent**: the installed CLIs have tool loops, file editing and shell access; a chat-completions endpoint has none. The two panes are genuinely different products and both are wanted. **Sequence — (a) agent CLI over OpenRouter FIRST:** it fits the existing architecture exactly (just another `PtyAgentAdapter`, with scrubber, restore, worktrees and layout all working unchanged) and it is what gives Task 3-6's BYOK milestone a vehicle. **(b) native api-mode sessions SECOND**, with or after Phase 3b, whose council needs that machinery anyway. **The four binding mitigations (accepted as part of the decision):** **(1) ONE ingest-scrub seam** — scrubbing must be a property of *"a session emits text"*, not *"a PTY emits text"*, so a new session type cannot silently ship unredacted. 3-5's scrubber sits on the PTY `onData` only, and an api session arriving via `receive()` would bypass it entirely — **the exact shape of F26**, which the restore path just proved is easy to miss. **(2) `ApiSessionHandle` is the SINGLE primitive** (`send` / `receive(): AsyncIterable<string>` / `dispose`, already declared in `types.ts`); Phase 3b's council must be an **orchestrator over** that handle, never a parallel implementation, or the blind-round batch shape and the interactive-chat shape diverge into two mechanisms. **(3) Pane type is a versioned, MIGRATED layout-schema change** — the layout tree is Zod-validated, versioned and persisted, with leaves that bind a `sessionId` and render a `TerminalPane`; a second pane type needs a migration, and **auto-titling needs a non-OSC mechanism**, since D18's titles come from terminal escape sequences a chat session never emits. **(4) HARD SEQUENCING — no api-mode work before Task 3-6 lands and Phase 3b kicks off**, because the standing risk is destabilising the phase's most security-critical task. **Risks ACCEPTED, not solved:** the eventual `SessionManager` session-type split (deferred by D34 Q2) lands inside the class carrying the D16 restore contract, F5/F6 and the 3-5 scrubber wiring — `write`/`resize`/`kill`/`exitCode`/`dispose` are all PTY-shaped and mean nothing to a conversation, so regression risk concentrates exactly there; **restore semantics will differ BY DESIGN** (api-mode can genuinely resume a conversation, whereas a restored PTY session is deliberately a fresh one per D16) and must be stated rather than discovered; capability divergence (a chat pane cannot use a worktree) pushes more capability-driven branching into `LaunchDialog`, the worktree panel and the diff summary; and two routes to one model need **agent-ness conveyed in the D43 label**, since one can edit files and one cannot. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Decision at the Task 3-6 scoping pass** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D48 | **`provider_configs` gains a nullable `model` column (migration v6): the ROUTE carries its own default model, replacing D47(3)'s launch-dialog free-text field.** Matthew's call, overriding the coordinator's lighter-weight recommendation. **Rationale:** D43 defines the launchable unit as **(agent × route × model)**, and a route that cannot say which model it drives is only two-thirds of one. `provider_configs` already holds the route's other non-secret connection metadata (`base_url`, `env_var_name`, `extra_headers_json`), so the model belongs beside them; an ephemeral dialog field would have to be retyped every launch and would persist nowhere. **Cost, accepted knowingly:** this puts a MIGRATION into the phase's largest and most security-critical task — precisely what D47(3) had avoided. **Therefore v6 carries the FULL Task 3-2 migration protocol, not a lighter one:** a single statement (`ALTER TABLE provider_configs ADD COLUMN model TEXT;` — nullable, the v3 `sessions.title` pattern), applied **in place** by the hand-rolled `MIGRATIONS` runner, with the **three-dump verification** (pre / post / second boot) proving v1–v5 `applied_at` byte-identical, every pre-existing table row-identical, and v6 **not re-applied** on boot 2. **The coordinator re-verifies on the REAL dev DB** (`985d547b…`), because F20 means the implementer's dump describes a different database — this is exactly how 3-2's v5 had to be re-driven. **Semantics — a DEFAULT, not an authority:** it is the route's default model, which Phase 3a's `launch_profiles` overrides once profiles exist. Recording it this way keeps 3a's design open instead of creating a second competing home for "which model". **It is NOT a `model_catalog`:** the Phase 3 non-goal barring catalogs, caching and refresh stands **unamended** — this is one nullable scalar per route, hand-entered in the provider form, with no list, no fetch, no refresh. **Nullable because subscription routes have no model to name**; the launch path reads it only when the provider carries one. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D47 | **Task 3-6 gains the OpenRouter ROUTE as its BYOK vehicle so the phase milestone closes on a real end-to-end proof — and it needs NO new adapter.** Matthew's call, taken because the alternative left Phase 3 shipping the BYOK machinery unexercised. **The enabling fact, verified 2026-07-24 against OpenRouter's own API docs and Codex provider documentation (D4):** `codex` supports custom providers via `[model_providers.<name>]` carrying `base_url`, **`env_key`** — *the name of the environment variable Codex reads at RUNTIME for the bearer token* — and `wire_api` (`"chat"`, since OpenRouter exposes only an OpenAI-compatible `/api/v1/chat/completions`). **`env_key` IS Chorus's injection mechanism**: the key arrives in the child's environment block, never in argv, never through `codex login`, never written to disk — so it clears D33 clause 5 and the §3a.4 bright line without contortion. **Why this is cheap:** no new agent kind, so Phase 3's "no new agent kinds" non-goal **and** D34 Q5's frozen registry both still hold **unamended**; no new CLI to install; and it is the first thing to exercise the dormant `provider_configs.base_url` and D34(e) `env_var_name` columns end to end. **Fully consistent with D44** — this is codex *the binary* driving a non-GPT model, not GPT on per-token billing; the CLI and the model are separate axes (D43). **Rejected alternative — Claude Code pointed at OpenRouter — is architecturally impossible, not merely unverified:** OpenRouter speaks only the OpenAI wire shape while Claude Code speaks the Anthropic Messages shape, and no environment variable bridges that; the thing that would bridge it is a translating proxy of precisely the kind D42 declined. **Side benefit — it dissolves the hazard that made the old Step 1a dangerous:** pointed at OpenRouter, a wrong-credential choice fails **visibly** (a ChatGPT subscription token is not valid there) instead of silently billing the subscription, so the silent-no-op failure mode cannot occur. **Three implementation constraints:** (1) the provider block is supplied **per-launch via `-c` dotted-path overrides**, not by Chorus writing the user's `~/.codex/config.toml` — nothing is persisted, and `-c` carries only NON-SECRET values (base URL, the env-var *name*, `wire_api`) while the key itself travels in the environment; (2) `requires_openai_auth = false` may be needed, since OpenRouter keys use an `sk-or-` prefix Codex may otherwise validate; (3) ~~the model id for 3-6 is one optional free-text field on the launch dialog~~ — **⚠ SUPERSEDED same day by D48: the model becomes a nullable `provider_configs.model` column (migration v6), so the ROUTE carries its own default model.** The rest of D47 stands. **All of this is D4 material and must be re-verified against the installed codex 0.145.0 at execution** — the sources are OpenRouter's own cookbook plus third-party tutorials, **not** Codex's `--help`, which documents `-c` and `--model` but not the `model_providers` schema. **⚠ AMENDED AT EXECUTION 2026-07-24 by the installed binary itself (Task 3-6, `15a016e`; ratified as D49). Two of D47's stated mechanics were WRONG against codex 0.145.0 — the decision stands, the mechanism text does not.** **(F-36-1) `wire_api = "chat"` is HARD-REJECTED at config load**, verbatim: `Error loading config.toml: wire_api = "chat" is no longer supported. How to fix: set wire_api = "responses" in your provider config.` The vendor config reference (developers.openai.com/codex/config-reference, fetched 2026-07-24) states **`responses` is the only supported value and the default when omitted**. Chorus therefore ships `wire_api="responses"` — pointed at OpenRouter's **`https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/responses`**, which is **BETA and STATELESS-ONLY** (it 400s on `store: true` and on `previous_response_id`). **Consequence, recorded as a live risk rather than papered over: single-turn is PROVEN end-to-end** (kimi-k3 answered the token prompt; evidence `_verify/3-6/d47-answered.png`), **multi-turn and compaction are UNPROVEN**, and the endpoint may change under us. Whichever phase next ships conversational work over this route (3a, or the api-mode work in 3b) **must prove multi-turn before depending on it**. **(F-36-2) `model_providers.<id>.name` is REQUIRED by 0.145.0** — omitting it fails with `provider name must not be empty` — and it was absent from D47's `-c` argument list. The **user-authored `provider_configs.name`** is emitted for it; this is **non-secret argv content** and so stays inside D47's constraint (1) bright line: `-c` carries base URL, the env-var *name*, `wire_api` and now the provider display name, while the key itself travels only in the environment. **D47's core ruling is untouched:** `env_key` is still the injection mechanism, no new agent kind was added, and D34 Q5's frozen registry still holds unamended. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 · MECHANISM AMENDED 2026-07-24 (D49) |
| D46 | **D45's mitigation (1) — the session-shaped ingest-scrub seam — is folded into Task 3-6 as a flagged behaviour-neutral CHORE COMMIT that lands FIRST. G3 is amended for that session: 3-6 makes two commits.** Precedent: D24 (F15 in 2-1), D32 (F21/F23 in 3-1), D36 (three hardenings in 3-3), D37 (F25 in 3-4). **Why now rather than in Phase 3b, where D45 originally placed it:** the scrubber is currently **dormant** — zero registered secrets, exactly one call site — so a mistake today cannot leak a real secret and the blast radius is minimal. The moment 3-6's second commit wires a live credential through that path, the same refactor means operating on a proven security path with a real secret flowing through it. This is the cheapest the change will ever be, and 3-6's own BYOK work then exercises the new seam immediately with a planted key, so the refactor gains a real consumer in the same session instead of sitting unproven until 3b. **What it is:** `SessionManager.spawn` inlines the whole output pipeline (scrubber construction, the `emit` helper's buffer-append + trim + broadcast, the clear-push-reschedule `onData` block, the exit flush) inside the function that spawns a PTY — structurally PTY-bound, not merely PTY-adjacent. It is extracted into `src/main/services/sessionOutput.ts`, a `SessionOutput` owning scrubber, carry-flush timer, ring buffer and broadcast, exposing `ingest`/`flush`/`buffer`/`dispose`, free of electron and node-pty. A PTY drives it from `onData`; a future api-mode session drives it from `receive()` — **one seam, so a second session type cannot ship unredacted by forgetting a second wiring point, which is precisely the F26 failure shape.** **Proof obligation, same burden as Task 3-3's behaviour-neutral refactor:** the 19 scrubber unit tests pass unchanged, `sessionOutput.test.ts` covers the five preserved invariants (one push per chunk; clear→push→reschedule ordering; flush-before-exit-notify; timer cleared on exit and dispose; the closure *is* the match-set storage per D33(a)), and **Task 3-5's runtime items 1–4 are re-driven against the refactored seam before the BYOK commit begins**. A redaction-path refactor that is not re-proven at runtime is believed-neutral, not proven-neutral. **Scope fence:** no api-mode code, no `ApiSessionHandle` implementation, no `SessionManager` session-type split — **D45(4) still binds.** This is a reshaping of the existing PTY path into a form a second type could later reuse, nothing more. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Decision at the Task 3-6 completion review** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D49 | **Task 3-6's four deviations are RATIFIED, including the one scope addition — and the coordinator review is accepted as the whole review.** **Process note first, because it is a departure:** §4's council trigger was arguably met (a security-sensitive surface plus a schema change), and **Matthew declined an external Fable/Cursor council pass on this one**, accepting the coordinator's independent re-run — typecheck 0, 273/273 across 14 files, `grep:secrets` clean, every claim re-read in the shipped code, and the DB re-dumped on the **real** path — as sufficient. Recorded so the trail shows a deliberate waiver, not an omission. **(F-36-1) `wire_api="chat"` → `"responses"`, forced by the binary.** codex 0.145.0 hard-rejects `chat` at config load; the vendor reference makes `responses` the only supported value. Ratified, with the **beta/stateless-only OpenRouter Responses endpoint** and the **single-turn-proven / multi-turn-unproven** limit carried forward as a live risk on D47's amendment — **D47 is amended, not rewritten away**, because its ruling (the OpenRouter route via `env_key`, no new adapter) survived contact intact. **(F-36-2) `model_providers.<id>.name` is required and was absent from the spec's arg list.** Ratified: the user-authored `provider_configs.name` is emitted, non-secret argv content, bright line unbroken. **(F-36-3) SCOPE ADDITION — an optional Base URL text input on the provider form (`SettingsProviders.vue:127`) — RATIFIED, and it was the right call.** Without it the D47 route could only be assembled by **hand-editing the database**, which would have hollowed out the milestone's "configured through the real Settings UI" proof into a lab result. It is non-secret per D33(e), and the launch **envelope's `baseUrl` still overrides the provider's**, so no new authority was created. **(F-36-4) `moonshotai/kimi-k2.7` is not a real OpenRouter slug — NOT a code defect.** The sanitized `Unexpected response (400).` correctly proved **auth passed and the model id was wrong**, incidentally exercising the "other status" mapping against a live provider rather than a mock; re-picked as `moonshotai/kimi-k3`. **Also ratified in the same pass, each carrying its own evidence:** the `skills: false → true` D4 correction; `--remote-auth-token-env` probed and **rejected** as a BYOK path (it is a remote app-server websocket bearer, not provider auth); `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` **declared null** (absent from `claude --help` — the spec's sanctioned branch; nothing is blocked because the codex route carries its base URL in argv, and the claude base-URL mapping defers to Phase 3a if ever needed); and **ZERO allow-list additions beyond D33's council seven** — both CLIs launch and run on `PATH, SystemRoot, TEMP, TMP, HOMEDRIVE, HOMEPATH, USERPROFILE` + the secret, **D33's Q6 fallback was not invoked**, and the extra variables seen in child envs (`COMSPEC`/`PATHEXT`/`PROMPT`, `CODEX_MANAGED_*`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `AI_AGENT`) are added downstream by `cmd.exe` and the npm shims, not required from Chorus. **Notably both CLIs worked WITHOUT `APPDATA`** — §2's worry that excluding it might break them is **empirically settled for launch + TUI**; deeper features (MCP servers, plugin sync) were **not exercised and remain unknown**, so the settlement is bounded, not general. **Step 7 = decision (b), implemented and runtime-proven — this CLOSES F26.** Credentialed sessions are marked via a `settings` id list (`credentialed_sessions`, read by `storage.ts:286`) — **data, not schema, so deliberately no second migration**; restore **heals them to `exited`** with the title "Credential not re-supplied — relaunch from the dialog to re-enter it" (`sessionManager.ts:203-215`) rather than relaunching keyless; `session:restart` refuses inline; `session:delete` clears the mark (`ipc.ts:665-666`). **Option (a) — re-resolve the credential at restore — was DECLINED for two substantive reasons**, not for convenience: it requires **unattended boot-time decryption** (a vault-lifetime change D33 did not sanction), and it would have put a **second schema change on `sessions`** into the same session as v6. **Phase 3a's `launch_profiles` is option (a)'s natural home**, and it likewise **supersedes the marked-session list**, which is **global rather than per-project** — an accepted **Phase-3-only expedient** (ids are uuids; cross-project collision risk is nil). | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Sequencing decision after Phase 3 closed** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D50 | **Phase 3a — Profiles & Catalog runs NEXT, ahead of 3b and 3c. The decisive argument is asymmetric decay, not relative value.** Phase 3a carries Mission Control's **spec-Phase-0 telemetry capture** (D41), and **historical actuals CANNOT be backfilled**: the projection phases are worthless without **3–4 weeks of data**, so **every week 3a waits is a week of data permanently lost**, while 3b and 3c cost only calendar time and lose nothing by waiting. Of the three candidates, only 3a has a clock running against it. **Supporting, in descending weight:** (1) **`launch_profiles` is where the deferred restore option (a) lands** (D49 / F26's closure) and it **supersedes 3-6's global marked-session list**, so 3a is already load-bearing for a debt Phase 3 knowingly took on; (2) Task 3-6 has **just proven the OpenRouter route end-to-end**, which is exactly the producer `usage_records` needs (D42) — building it while that knowledge is freshest is materially cheaper than rediscovering it in three phases' time; (3) `provider_configs.model` (v6) now exists as the route's default model, so the model half of D43's triple has a live anchor to build against. **The counter-case for 3b first — "retire the external council loop and stop paying its tax" — is explicitly considered and REJECTED on two grounds:** Matthew **is no longer running the Cursor council pass** (D49 records the waiver), so the tax 3b would retire is already largely unpaid; and 3b still carries **D45(1) as a hard prerequisite** — the ingest-scrub seam must be **unified** before api-mode sessions ship or they ship unredacted (the F26 failure shape), and `sessionOutput.ts` (D46, `e3d72c5`) is the **first step toward that, not the finish**. **3c — Design Adoption holds until after 3a:** it is a pure restyle with **no dependents until Phase 4**, and its D38 placement rationale (land the design frame before the notification chrome) is satisfied as long as it precedes Phase 4, which this ordering preserves. **Phase 3a has NOT had its own `/phase-kickoff`** — it stays labelled provisional until it does; this decision fixes only its **position in the queue**, not its contents. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |

**Decisions at the Phase 3a kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-24):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D51 | **Phase 3a's telemetry slice lands IN FULL — wall-clock + attention + tokens/cost — not in instalments.** The two lighter options (wall-clock only; wall-clock + attention with tokens deferred) were **rejected on the phase's own logic**: token history is precisely the thing that cannot be backfilled, so deferring it defeats the reason D50 sequenced this phase first. Acceptance is the Mission Control spec's own §9 Phase 0 wording — **dispatches appear in the store with non-zero token counts attributed to the right agent and model** — not merely "rows exist". **Cost, accepted knowingly:** this makes Task 3a-3 a live-credential-minting task with real spend attached, which is why its gates are the heaviest in the phase. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D52 | **The two new PTY adapters move OUT of Phase 3a into a new provisional Phase 3d.** Kimi CLI (the third native subscription adapter) and the OpenAI-compatible **agent CLI** adapter are adapter work, not catalog/profile work, and folding them in would have pushed the decay-sensitive telemetry later for no gain. **The argument that previously bound them to 3a is now dead:** the roadmap held that a profile like "Aider × OpenRouter × DeepSeek" is meaningless until an OpenAI-compatible CLI exists — but **Task 3-6 proved codex-the-binary over OpenRouter end-to-end**, so profiles already have a real vehicle and 3a is fully meaningful without either adapter. **D34 Q5's frozen-registry ruling travels WITH the adapters** and is lifted in Phase 3d as its own numbered decision, together with widening `agentKindSchema` and the static registry in the same change (Phase 6's standing constraint). | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D53 | **Restore stays decision (b); Phase 3a adds a one-click relaunch rather than unattended re-resolution.** Option (a) — re-resolve the credential at restore — **stays declined**: it requires decrypting from the vault at boot with **no user present**, a vault-lifetime change D33 never sanctioned and which would be a §4 CR trigger in its own right. Task 3a-5 instead gives a healed credentialed session a **relaunch action that resolves the credential from its `launch_profile` with the user at the keyboard**, replacing today's retype-everything-in-the-dialog flow. **The no-boot-decrypt boundary is a hard invariant with a two-part reviewer check** (structural greps proving no decrypt call site is reachable from the restore path, plus an instrumented cold boot showing zero decrypts across a boot that healed a credentialed session, and exactly one after a Relaunch click). | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D54 | **`TERM` is pinned at spawn — D33's seven-variable allow-list becomes eight.** Rationale is **F28**, observed live: an inherited `TERM=dumb` put codex 0.145.0 into a fallback renderer emitting cursor-advance escapes **between individual characters**, leaving a secret fully legible on screen and simultaneously invisible to the exact-match scrubber. Chorus therefore pins `TERM=xterm-256color` (with `COLORTERM`) in `composeChildEnv` rather than inheriting whatever shell launched the app. **Per-adapter declaration was rejected** — env policy keeps one owner (D34(d)), and a per-adapter knob has no consumer today; **keep-inheriting was rejected** because it leaves a security-relevant behaviour dependent on the developer's shell. **It lands as a flagged behaviour-scoped CHORE COMMIT at the head of Task 3a-1** (precedent D24/D32/D36/D37/D46), narrated as an allow-list amendment — a non-secret eighth variable added deliberately, never slipped into `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` unremarked. | RESOLVED 2026-07-24 |
| D55 | **No attention number may be obtained without its denominator, and the rule is enforced by the SCHEMA rather than by discipline.** Settled in practice by Task 3a-2 (`fd4c9e5`) and recorded here because it **binds Phase 8**, whose `[CR]` question names attention measurement as one of two top risks. The Mission Control spec calls attention-minutes *"the most valuable output and the least reliable input"*; the failure mode is therefore not a wrong number but a **confident-looking** one. **The ruling:** `attention:summary` carries **no `minutes` field at all** — minutes are *derived* by the caller from `samples × tickSeconds`, and the response carries `byClass` (all five classes, exhaustive), `samples`, `expectedSamples`, `missingSamples`, `coveragePct` and a required `estimateBound: 'lower-bound'` in the same record. **A denominator-less response fails the outbound `.parse` in main**, so a future edit that drops `coveragePct` throws instead of shipping a bare figure — the same move D33 clause 3 used to make "no key material on the wire" a fact about the code rather than a promise about the author. **Two consequences that are easy to lose later:** (1) **if the capture layer does not record the denominator, no later layer can invent it** — a window where the app was shut for six hours and a window of six hours at 8 % attention are indistinguishable from minutes alone, which is why this is a Phase-3a schema decision and not a Phase-8 display decision; (2) **the number must not acquire authority before the readout exists** — the spec's own §10 Q3 ("is focus-plus-idle a good enough proxy, or is explicit start/stop needed?") is answered by **weeks of this data**, not by this design, and the coverage figures plus the eventual correction rate ARE that readout. **The bias direction is fixed and stated in the payload, not just in docs:** the figure is a **lower bound**, biased against reading-heavy and multi-window work (a 20-minute uninterrupted read counts as one minute; work outside the Chorus window lands in `blurred`). **Any surface that renders attention — Phase 8's board, any export, any PM report — inherits this rule.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-25 (Task 3a-2) |
| D56 | **⚠ THE MODEL-PRECEDENCE ORDER, NORMATIVE — and `model_catalog` is not in it.** Three artefacts now talk about models, and exactly ONE order resolves a launch: **(1) `launch_profiles.model`** — the choice for THIS launch (Task 3a-5) · **(2) `provider_configs.model`** — this route's DEFAULT (v6, D48) · **(3) nothing** — the CLI's own default, no `-m` emitted. **`model_catalog` is a LIST OF WHAT EXISTS. It has no rank, it is never authoritative over either other home, and it NEVER writes to them.** A catalog miss **warns**; it never blocks, clears, defaults, or substitutes — the provider is the authority on whether a model id resolves (**F-36-4**), and a stale cache used as a gate turns a warning into an outage. Three enforcement sites, deliberately structural rather than aspirational: the refresh diff **has no field able to express a write to either home** (asserted over its whole key set, including the input where the route's default is the id that just went missing); the provider form's model input **stays free text with an additive suggestion list, never a closed `<select>`** — a dropdown would make the catalog authoritative by UI construction with nobody deciding to; and the diff was driven at runtime with the route's default pointed at a missing model, the `provider_configs` row coming out **byte-identical**. D48 exists because "which model" briefly had two homes; this is how it gains a third ROLE without a third AUTHORITY. **Binds Task 3a-5 and every later phase that touches model selection.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-25 (Task 3a-4, `3433c91`) |
| D57 | **Refresh does NOT double as Test key — an evidence-backed REVERSAL of this roadmap's own Phase 3a scope line.** The scope line proposed "the 'list models' call doubling as Test key", and the merge has a genuinely good case: one call, one failure vocabulary, no token spent, and a list call cannot be confused by a bad model id the way `probeCredential` can (F-36-4 exactly). **It is rejected on a measured fact, re-verified at execution: `GET /models` returns 200 with NO `Authorization` header** (F32). A "test your key" built on it would report success for a garbage key, an empty key, or no key at all — on the one provider route the app ships. That is not a weaker test; it is a test that **cannot fail**, presented to the user as proof their credential works. **`credential:test` and `probeCredential` are untouched**, and a successful refresh deliberately does **not** write `last_verified_at` (proven at runtime: unchanged at `2026-07-25T20:13:22.131Z` across an authenticated refresh). The pleasant corollary of the same fact is that **the credential is OPTIONAL** — a catalog can be populated before any key exists. **If a later phase revisits this, the fact to re-measure is whether the provider's `/models` rejects an invalid key — and even a "yes" does not license the merge, because it would then hold for one provider and silently not for another.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-25 (Task 3a-4, `3433c91`) |
| D58 | **D33 resolution (d)'s Test-key carve-out WIDENS BY EXACTLY ONE CALL — `model:refresh` — on five named constraints.** D33 gave the honest "your key never leaves this machine" guarantee a single carve-out; this is the second path that sends a user's stored inference credential, and it is admitted on the same terms, each proven rather than asserted: **user-initiated only** (grep-verified — one caller, a click handler; no boot hook, no timer, no settings-open hook, no watcher) · **decrypt at the moment of the call and drop it** (no module-level variable, no memo; unit-tested as two calls, two decryptions) · **refused for a profile carrying `unavailable_since`, BY LABEL, without re-attempting decryption** (D33 clause 8 — driven against the real marked row, and proven by its `unavailable_since` **not moving**, with a control showing the same provider *did* reach the network without that credential) · **refused outright when the provider's `auth_mode` is `'management'`** (driven against the real management row, which *has* a base URL, so the check is what stopped the call) · **optional** (no profile → no `Authorization` header at all). The transport inherits `probeCredential`'s discipline verbatim with **one deliberate departure, split explicitly in the code**: `probeCredential` cancels the response body **always** because a 401 body can echo the submitted key, whereas a refresh must **read** it on success — so the rule becomes read-on-2xx-only, size-capped from a measurement (8 MB against a measured 0.51 MiB), **cancelled unread on every other path**, and the parsed value never interpolated into an error. **Any future third key-bearing call must be admitted the same way: numbered, constrained, and narrated — never slipped in.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-25 (Task 3a-4, `3433c91`) |
| D59 | **Effort precedence: a raw `extra_args` override beats the app-level slider and SUPPRESSES it ENTIRELY — Chorus never emits both.** Order: **(1)** the user's raw override, in the CLI's own vocabulary · **(2)** the Fast/Balanced/Deep/Max level, mapped per adapter · **(3)** nothing. When `extra_args` carries a token Chorus recognises as *that adapter's* effort knob, Chorus emits **zero** effort tokens of its own — it does **not** emit both and lean on the CLI's last-wins parsing, which is per-CLI, unverified, and differs between an argv flag (`claude --effort`) and a config override (`codex -c model_reasoning_effort=`). One authority per launch, decided in one pure function — the same shape as `composeChildEnv`'s ordering under D54. **The mapping has ONE home: the adapter's `EffortDescriptor`.** `EffortOption.cliFlag: string` was **replaced** (not supplemented) by **`args: readonly string[]`**, because a single string cannot express `['-c','model_reasoning_effort="high"']` without a whitespace split that breaks on quoted values — and the alternative, a per-adapter `switch` in `buildLaunch`, would have given the mapping two homes in the very task whose headline output is a one-home ruling. Free at execution: `cliFlag` had **zero producers and zero real consumers**. The suppression predicate **must stay specific** — a substring or case-insensitive match silently disables the whole feature the first time a user passes an unrelated argument, and looks like it works. **⚠ STANDING WARNING FOR TASK 3a-5, which ships the `extra_args` INPUT SURFACE this task deliberately withheld: `extra_args` becomes argv, and argv is WORLD-READABLE (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`).** A free-text argv field is a blast-radius decision in its own right and must not ride along unexamined. | RESOLVED 2026-07-25 (Task 3a-4, `3433c91`) |
| D60 | **⚠ THE NO-UNATTENDED-DECRYPTION GUARANTEE HAS TWO CREDENTIAL CLASSES, AND ONE OF THEM WAS WIDENED WITHOUT A DECISION.** Task 3a-5's governing docs stated the phase's hard invariant as *"no code path reachable without a user gesture may call `vault.decryptForLaunch`… exactly TWO call sites, both inside `registerIpc`… `index.ts` shows the vault constructed and passed to `registerIpc`, and nothing else."* **That was already FALSE at `3433c91`, before 3a-5 wrote a line, and nothing in the roadmap recorded it.** Task 3a-3 (`f56dcaf`) added a management-key thunk in `src/main/index.ts` and calls it from `app.whenReady` → `attribution.reconcileOrphanedKeys()` → `keys.list()` → `getManagementKey()` → `vault.decryptForLaunch`. **Observed, not inferred:** an instrumented cold boot at the unmodified HEAD logged **three** decrypts of "OR Management Key" before the window existed, with no user present. **So D49/F26's "we declined option (a) because it needs unattended boot-time decryption" had, in a narrower sense, already been overtaken — for a different credential class, with good reasons written in the code, but never as a numbered decision and never reflected in D33's carve-out list.** **The ruling, in two parts. (1) THE GUARANTEE IS RESTATED BY CREDENTIAL CLASS, not by user gesture:** *no code path reachable without a user gesture may resolve a **LAUNCH** credential — an inference credential used to spawn a PTY.* `SessionManager` holds **zero** vault references, `restore()` decrypts nothing, and a credentialed session heals rather than relaunching. **This is a NARROWING and is recorded as one:** the old sentence covered every decrypt; this one covers the inference class. The reason it is nonetheless the honest boundary is that D49/F26's concern was always *spawning a session on a user's key with nobody watching*, and a management key **cannot spawn anything** — it mints and revokes gateway keys, it cannot do inference, `resolveCredential` refuses it **before** decryption, and it never reaches a child PTY. The credential CLASS was always the real boundary; "user gesture" was a proxy that stopped being accurate the moment 3a-3 landed. **(2) THE MANAGEMENT CLASS IS ADMITTED TO UNATTENDED BOOT-TIME RESOLUTION, EXPLICITLY, on four constraints** — resolved **per use and never cached** (no module-level variable, no memo); **never injected into any child process**; **refused by `resolveCredential` before the decrypt** so it cannot reach a launch even through a hand-edited row; and **degrading to no-attribution rather than to an error** when absent or undecryptable. Anything beyond boot reconciliation and per-dispatch mint/revoke is a NEW carve-out and needs its own decision. **Enforcement is a discriminating probe, not a counter:** a raw decrypt COUNT cannot express a class-scoped invariant, so the proof logs the profile id **and its provider's `auth_mode`** and asserts *zero decrypts where `authMode !== 'management'` across a boot that genuinely healed a credentialed session*, plus *exactly one `api_key` decrypt after one Relaunch click*. Both halves were driven on the real dev DB in one continuous instrumented boot, with a non-credentialed relaunch as the negative control so "nothing decrypted" could not be confused with "restore is broken". **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON: a phase-level security sentence that names call-site COUNTS goes stale the moment any task adds a caller, and a stale invariant is worse than a loose one because it will be cited as proof.** Future statements of this guarantee name the credential CLASS and the reachability condition, never a count. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (Task 3a-5, `341ea5c`) |
| D61 | **The credentialed-session mark is DERIVED and FAIL-SAFE, and "no profile" is NOT the same fact as "no credential".** Task 3-6's global `credentialed_sessions` settings row — an explicitly-labelled Phase-3-only expedient — is retired into `sessions.launch_profile_id`, written on the **same INSERT** as the row (closing the window in which a crash left a live credentialed session unmarked) and dying with the row (so `session:delete` needs no unmark call). The predicate reads the pointer through the profile: NULL → not credentialed; resolves with no credential → not credentialed; resolves with one → credentialed; **unresolvable → CREDENTIALED, FAIL-SAFE**, because Chorus cannot *prove* such a session was keyless and the only safe reading of "cannot prove" is "do not restore it keyless" (F26's shape, already paid for once). **⚠ THE DEFECT THIS DECISION EXISTS TO RECORD:** `ImplementationSpec-3a-5.md` §6.1's own handler code leaves `launchProfileId` NULL for a launch on a **bare credential with no profile** — still a first-class path (D33 clause 9 / the 3-6 dialog flow, which 3a-5 deliberately does not remove). NULL reads as *not credentialed*, so the restore engine **would have relaunched those sessions KEYLESS** — the agent running on ambient credentials while the user believed it ran on their key. **The retirement would have reintroduced the exact failure the thing it retired existed to prevent.** Caught at execution and fixed with the mechanism already in hand: such sessions get the **same unresolvable sentinel** the migrated legacy rows get. The sentinel therefore covers **two populations** and its doc comment says so — the retired list recorded session ids and nothing else, so there is no data from which to synthesise a profile, and synthesising one would put a fake row in the user's picker. **Nothing special-cases the sentinel**: a legacy row, a bare-credential row and a deleted-profile row all take the ordinary unresolvable path, which is what makes the retirement honest and is asserted by a named test. **The fail-safe branch is MUTATION-TESTED** — inverting `return true` to `return false` fails exactly the two tests written for it — because it is a one-character change that compiles and passes every happy path. **Also settled here:** the last-used pointer is **per-project** (`last_launch_profile:<projectId>`, the `view_state` precedent) and stores the **immutable id, never the label** (D43), since retiring one global-by-default fact while creating another in the same commit would be indefensible; `recent_cwds` remains the cautionary global precedent, observed and deliberately not fixed. A dangling pointer resolves to "no default", never to a fuzzy label match. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (Task 3a-5, `341ea5c`) |
| D62 | **`launch_profiles` carries REAL `REFERENCES` — the deliberate INVERSE of `dispatches` (v7) and `model_catalog` (v9) — because the FK's job is to make the refusal MANDATORY, not to author it.** Three tables in one phase now take two opposite FK rulings, so the reasoning is recorded rather than left to look like an inconsistency: a **dispatch** is a historical fact and a **catalog row** is a cache — both stay true (or harmless) once their subject is deleted, so both tolerate dangling and carry no `REFERENCES`, and `model_catalog` is purged explicitly inside `deleteProviderConfig`'s own transaction because *a cache must never break a user flow*. A **launch profile is a live INSTRUCTION** that becomes a lie the moment its route or credential is gone, so RESTRICT is correct — and correct **precisely because** it forces a count-and-refuse to be authored in main before the statement runs. **`credential:delete` had NO guard at all before this task** and would have thrown `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` straight through a flow working since Task 3-2; `provider:delete` gained a second count beside its existing credential-profile one. Both were driven live, **with the new provider guard isolated on a route that had a launch profile and no credential profiles** so the pre-existing guard could not mask it — an earlier run proved nothing precisely because the 3-2 guard fired first. Zero `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` in any log. **`sessions.launch_profile_id` is the third case and takes the `dispatches` ruling** — a SOFT pointer with no `REFERENCES`, because a session row is history and sessions are deleted on pane close (D16(d)); a FK there would make deleting a profile throw for every session that ever used it. Its dangling case is exactly what D61's fail-safe predicate absorbs, which is what makes the soft pointer safe rather than sloppy. **Also settled at execution:** a launch profile may not name a **management** route — it was accepted, rendered as launchable, and would have launched credential-less on a route that cannot do inference; now refused at create **and** at resolve, mirroring `resolveCredential`'s own pre-decrypt refusal (D42's operational note). **⚠ AMENDED 2026-07-26 (Task 3b-2, `01556a8`) — THE RULING NOW SPLITS THREE WAYS INSIDE ONE MIGRATION, and the third way is new.** Migration **v11** applies all three at once: **`council_members.credential_profile_id` → REAL `REFERENCES`, RESTRICT** (a member is a live INSTRUCTION, the `launch_profiles` case); **`council_runs` → ZERO references on EVERY column** (a run is a historical FACT, the `dispatches`/`model_catalog` case); **`council_messages` → ZERO as well**, and this is the case v10 did not have to decide — **a transcript stays true after its member is deleted**, and a FK there would make deleting ONE member throw for EVERY run it ever joined, which is the `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`-through-a-working-flow failure this decision exists to prevent, pointed at the council instead of at Task 3-2's guard. Verified rather than asserted, by `PRAGMA foreign_key_list`: **council_members 1, council_runs 0, council_messages 0**, with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1` re-confirmed (F16). **The soft-pointer consequence is handled explicitly, exactly as the v7/v9 tables handle theirs:** SQLite will not cascade a pointer it does not know about, so **`deleteCouncilRun(id)` purges its own `council_messages` in one transaction** — the `deleteProviderConfig` → `model_catalog` precedent. **The count-and-refuse was ADDED TO the existing guard, not set beside it as a second one:** `credential:delete` now refuses on `countLaunchProfilesForCredential(id) + countCouncilMembersForCredential(id)`, 3a-5's count neither replaced nor duplicated, and **both are named distinctly in the refusal** so the message says what to remove rather than merely that something blocks. **And the guard was driven ISOLATED, which is the half 3a-5 had to learn twice:** `launch_profiles` held **ZERO rows** for the drive, so only the new council-member count could produce the refusal — an earlier 3a-5 run proved nothing precisely because the older guard fired first. **Zero `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` in any log.** **⚠ ALSO SETTLED HERE, AND IT CORRECTS THIS ROADMAP'S OWN PHASE-3b TEXT: `council_members` carries NO `base_url` column and NO `provider_id` column.** A member names a route **by naming a credential** — reached through `credential_profiles.provider_id` → `provider_configs.base_url`, which is **D48's one home and D56's normative order**. `launch_profiles` needs both columns only because **D33 clause 9** makes a route-with-no-credential first class; **a council member ALWAYS authenticates**, so that case does not exist, and two columns that can disagree are a bug class rather than a convenience. **The management refusal is at BOTH ends, at two named call sites** — `validateMemberShape` (create) and `resolveCouncilMember` (resolve) — because one without the other is exactly the 3a-5 defect the paragraph above records. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (Task 3a-5, `341ea5c`) · **AMENDED 2026-07-26 (Task 3b-2, `01556a8`)** |

**Decisions at the Phase 3b kickoff** (2026-07-26):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D63 | **The api-mode session producer** (CR-3b.0; 3-model council Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md`). Verdict **PASS**, confidence 8/10. **Q1 — Option D, 3-of-3 unanimous:** `ApiSessionHandle` is produced by a **standalone factory**, `src/main/services/apiSession.ts` exporting `createApiSession(spec, deps)`, living **outside the agent registry**. `agentKindSchema` stays `'claude' \| 'codex'`, `staticRegistry` stays frozen, the F25 filter and the restore contract are untouched, and **D52 keeps the D34-Q5 registry lift with Phase 3d** — no reviewer needed it moved. `ApiAgentAdapter.startApiSession` stays **dormant but documented**, becoming a one-line delegation to the factory when the registry is lifted; Option C was explicitly discarded as contradicting D45(2), Option B rejected for forcing a wire-enum widening into the same session as the first-ever api transport, and Option A rejected because a dead declaration with no adoption path fails type-level honesty. **Q2 — OUT, 3-of-3:** a council member **never enters `SessionManager`**; `CouncilService` owns the handles, no `sessions` row is written, and therefore **the boot restore engine cannot resurrect a council run** — a crashed run is lost, deliberately, which is how constraint 6.5 (nothing resolves an inference credential without a user gesture) is satisfied structurally rather than by a guard. **Q3 — CORRECT, 2-of-3:** `send` / `receive(): AsyncIterable<string>` / `dispose` stands unchanged for both the blind-round council and the future interactive chat; the council streams deliberation live, so `AsyncIterable` is load-bearing rather than ceremony. **`dispose()` is the SOLE cancellation mechanism** — per-operation cancellation is deferred, and the interface must say so in a docstring rather than leave it implicit. **Q4 — SCRUB, 3-of-3:** api text routes through `createSessionOutput().ingest()` driven from `for await (… of handle.receive())`, with the minted council-run key registered as a secret. **⚠ AND THE COVERAGE CLAIM IS BOUNDED IN THE SAME BREATH: the scrubber protects against injected credential ECHO, not against brief-quoted content** — it exact-matches *registered* values, so a key quoted inside the brief `.md` a council member is asked to read is **not** covered by it. Overclaiming here would be F27's failure shape in a new place. **Q5 — no alternative shape, 3-of-3.** **Coordinator resolutions (a)–(g), from verifying the findings against the code at `341ea5c` — the council had the brief, not the repo, and its verbatim TypeScript does not compile as written:** **(a)** it imports `Scrubber` from `./sessionOutput`, which does not export it — the interface is declared at **`scrubber.ts:34`**, and `sessionOutput.ts` merely imports `createScrubber` from it; **(b)** its import specifiers carry `.js` extensions, which no module in `src/` uses; **(c)** it reads the bearer token as `spec.credential.key`, a field that **does not exist** — `ResolvedCredential` is `{ envVarName, value, isSecret: true }` and the token is **`.value`**; **(d) ⚠ `ApiSessionDeps.scrubber` is STRUCK, because it contradicts the council's own Q4 ruling.** Q4 puts scrubbing at the **consumer**; a `scrubber` dep puts a second one **inside** the factory. That is not merely redundant — `createSessionOutput` builds its own via `createScrubber(opts.secrets)`, and a scrubber holds a **carry** across chunk boundaries, so chaining two of them breaks the invariant proven for exactly one (`scrubber.ts:50–51`: *"the concatenation of every `push()` return, followed by `flush()`, equals the concatenation of every input chunk with secrets replaced"*). **The factory emits raw text; the consumer scrubs. One seam, per D45(1)** — a second scrub path inside the producer is the very shape `sessionOutput.ts` was extracted to prevent. **Three gaps the council did not raise, added here:** **(e)** **nothing bounds the stream in bytes or time.** One minted key per run caps **spend**, not **volume** — a model that streams indefinitely under the cap is unbounded, and D58 already made read-on-2xx-only-and-size-capped a numbered constraint with `MODELS_RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES = 8_000_000` as the precedent; the factory needs its own byte **and** wall-clock cap; **(f)** the brief-sanitization pre-pass the findings recommend must reuse **`src/main/services/secret-patterns.json`**, already the ONE list shared by `logger.ts`'s `scrubSecrets` and `scripts/secret-grep.mjs` — authoring a second pattern list would let the gate and the sanitizer test different shapes, which is the exact drift that file's header exists to prevent; **(g) ⚠ Q3 AND D64(2) ARE IN TENSION AND THE COUNCIL ANSWERED NEITHER SIDE OF IT.** Q3 ratified `ApiSessionHandle` **as declared** — `receive()` yields `string` — while D64(2) requires **per-member token usage**. A stream that yields only strings has **nowhere to report what it cost**. The fix is **not** to add `usage` to the handle: that is the SHARED primitive D45(2) binds the future chat pane to, and a pane that meters nothing would carry a field it never reads. Usage is reported through an optional **`onUsage` callback on `ApiSessionDeps`** — the factory's own contract, not the shared one — so a metering consumer opts in and the primitive stays exactly as Q3 ruled. It also keeps usage **out of the text stream**: a final `receive()` yield would flow through the scrubber and the ring buffer and be rendered in the transcript as though the model had said it. **⚠ Whether `usage` is obtainable at all on a STREAMED response is a D4 obligation, not an assumption** — several OpenAI-compatible APIs omit it from stream chunks absent a `stream_options`-style flag. Task 3b-1 answers it from the vendor's documentation and **reports a negative as a finding**, because 3b-3's whole cost model rests on the answer. **Dissent preserved — Kimi:** `send()` should take an optional `AbortSignal` **now**, while the interface has zero implementations, so an interactive chat can stop a generation without destroying conversation context; the arbiter ruled with GLM and Qwen that `dispose()` suffices for 3b. **Revisit the moment the chat pane design begins** — by then the interface has implementations and the change is breaking. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 · coordinator resolutions (a)–(g) **Matthew-ratified 2026-07-26** |
| D64 | **Three Phase 3b scope rulings, settled at kickoff before any task doc was written** (Matthew, 2026-07-26). **(1) The council surface is a VIEW/ROUTE, not a layout pane** — the `SettingsProviders.vue` precedent. This is what keeps **D45(3) entirely out of the phase**: no versioned migration of the persisted layout tree, no non-OSC auto-titling mechanism, and no capability divergence pushed into `LaunchDialog` or the worktree panel. The pane-type work stays where D45 put it, with the native chat pane. **(2) Cost is bounded by ONE minted OpenRouter key per RUN**, carrying a hard limit, with per-member token and cost granularity read from each response's own `usage` block — rather than one mint per member (5× the mint/revoke traffic and five orphan-reconcile paths per run) or no mint at all (no pre-authorization ceiling, so a stuck arbiter loop spends until someone notices). **⚠ 3a-3's measured lesson binds the cap: a hard cap is a PRE-AUTHORIZATION ceiling, not a budget** — OpenRouter refuses the request outright when the requested max output exceeds the remaining limit, so the council's cap must clear the members' **max output allocation**, not their expected spend. **⚠ Also inherited: `usage_records` IS A DEAD NAME.** This roadmap's Phase 3a scope line and the Phase 3b line both still say `usage_records`; the table was superseded by **`dispatches`** plus 3a-3's mint ledger (`storage.ts:139–141` says so in the code). Any 3b doc citing `usage_records` is citing a table that does not exist. **(3) The pre-identified `[CR]` checkpoint on the deliberation & arbitration protocol is DEFERRED to Task 3b-3's kickoff**, not waived — briefing a council on a protocol that has not been designed yet spends the pass on a blank page. The trigger fires once a protocol design exists to review, and §4's obligation stands until it does. **This is a deferral with a named firing condition, deliberately not the D49 waiver.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 |
| D65 | **Task 3b-1's five deviations from `ImplementationSpec-3b-1.md` §3's "exact exported surface" are RATIFIED — four additions and one CORRECTION TO THE SPEC ITSELF.** The D49 precedent applies: deviations found at a completion review are ratified or reversed explicitly, never absorbed. **(1) `onRefusal?: (reason: string) => void` added to `ApiSessionDeps`.** The spec's own §6 response schema requires `reason: z.string().nullable()`, and **nothing in the specified surface could supply it**: `receive()` yields `string`, throwing out of the iteration is precisely what unit case 11 forbids, and a refusal *yielded as text* would flow through the scrubber and the ring buffer to be rendered as though the model had said it. It is **D63(g)'s ratified pattern applied to the sibling problem** — the shared `ApiSessionHandle` stays exactly as Q3 ratified it, and the **factory's own contract** carries the fact. **(2) `maxOutputTokens?: number` added to `ApiSessionDeps`.** D63(e) named two caps — byte and wall-clock — and **NEITHER bounds cost.** This is the third bound and the only one that bounds spend. It is **deliberately optional and deliberately without a default**, because a silent output cap truncates a council member mid-sentence and reads as a model behaviour rather than as configuration — and **F34, found in this same task, is exactly what that looks like when the cap is set carelessly.** **(3) `api:probe`'s request gained three fields.** `max_tokens` is **required, not optional**, so the cost envelope is enforced at the wire boundary rather than by convention; `dispose_after_chunks` and `planted_secret` are **effectively mandated by `Task-3b-1.md`'s own drives 3 and 5** ("register a planted secret… assert the ingested text is redacted") and those drives are **unprovable without them**. **(4) `api:probe`'s response is a discriminated `z.union` with `.strict()` arms, not the spec's flat `{ok: z.boolean(), …}`.** House idiom over sketch: **17** sibling response schemas in `src/shared/ipc.ts` already use it, and it stops a failure arm from carrying a meaningless `text`/`chunks`. **(5) ⚠ A CORRECTION TO THE SPEC ITSELF, recorded because later specs may copy the pattern.** §5's assertion sketch — `type _X = typeof createApiSession extends (…) => ApiSessionHandle ? true : never` — **COMPILES VACUOUSLY**: a type alias that evaluates to `never` is not an error, so it would have been cited as delegation coverage while proving nothing at all. The shipped form **constrains** it — `type _Assert<T extends true> = T`, then `type _StartApiSessionIsDelegable = _Assert<… ? true : false>` — turning a mismatch into a **typecheck ERROR**. **Verified to actually fail, in both directions**, by deliberately breaking the factory's `spec` parameter and, separately, its return type: each produced `types.ts: error TS2344: Type 'false' does not satisfy the constraint 'true'`. **A type-level assertion that cannot fail is worse than no assertion, because it gets cited as proof** — the same failure shape D60 named for a stale call-site count. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (Task 3b-1, `e7ca62a`) |

**Decision before Task 3b-3's execution, from authoring its kickoff prompt against the code** (2026-07-26):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D66 | **⚠ THE BOOT RECONCILE CANNOT SEE A COUNCIL RUN, IN EITHER DIRECTION, AND MIGRATION v11 DID NOT CHANGE THAT — the ownership predicate and the ledger input widen to cover both tables, as a flagged CHORE COMMIT at the head of Task 3b-3.** Found by the coordinator 2026-07-26 while authoring `Task-3b-3-ExecutionPrompt.md` against the code at `01556a8`, **not** by reading the task docs, which assert the opposite: `Task-3b-3.md` step 2 requires that *"boot reconciliation must see an open ledger row (`revoked_at IS NULL`) exactly as a dispatch's does"*, and `ImplementationSpec-3b-3.md` §3(2) calls `revoked_at IS NULL` *"the open-row predicate boot reconciliation queries — the same definition v8 uses, deliberately."* **Both are false today, and writing the row does not make them true. THE GAP, VERIFIED IN THREE PARTS. (1)** `storage.listOpenMintLedger()` (`storage.ts:1113`) selects **from `dispatches` only**; its own docstring calls it *"THE BOOT RECONCILE'S INPUT: every OPEN ledger row"*, and after v11 that sentence is simply wrong — `council_runs` carries the same four mint columns and the same open-row predicate, deliberately, and **none of them is read**. **(2)** `isChorusMintedName` (`attributionCore.ts:421`) tests `name.startsWith(MINT_NAME_PREFIX)` where `MINT_NAME_PREFIX = 'chorus-dispatch-'` (`attributionCore.ts:138`), and `computeKeyReconcile`'s §6.1 matrix **row 4 is FIRST and unconditional**: *"a live key whose name does not start with MINT_NAME_PREFIX produces NO ACTION… nothing below may re-open the question."* **(3) THEREFORE BOTH DO-NOTHING OPTIONS ARE WRONG, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — which is why this could not be left to the execution session's judgement.** A council key minted under a **different** prefix hits **row 4 → NO ACTION, forever**: a live, funded key with **no backstop at all**, strictly worse than the position `dispatches` was in before 3a-3 built the ledger. A council key **reusing** `chorus-dispatch-` hits **row 3 → `revoke-unattributed`**, which is *accidentally* the right outcome and *definitely* the wrong bookkeeping: that branch does **not** call `readAndRevoke`, so **usage is never read back** (breaking 3a-3's read-before-revoke, and revocation is a `DELETE`), `council_runs.revoked_at` **stays NULL forever** so the ledger row never closes, and the reconcile **reports it as a mint whose record was lost** — a false statement about what happened, written into the one log that exists to be trusted. **THE RULING, in five parts. (a) ONE MECHANISM, NOT TWO.** The ownership predicate and the ledger input widen to cover both tables; a parallel council reconcile is **rejected**, because two reconcilers over one live-key list is how a key gets classified twice, and row 4's absolute prohibition only protects the user's own keys if **exactly one place decides ownership**. **(b) The council mint gets its OWN prefix** (`chorus-council-`), with the predicate widened to a **closed, case-sensitive, index-0-anchored SET** rather than a single literal — reusing `chorus-dispatch-` would put a false statement into a string **sent to a third party** and would make the census log (`[attribution] key census: … ours=[…]`) name a council run as a dispatch. **The three false-positive arguments in `isChorusMintedName`'s docblock are unchanged and apply to every member of the set**, and `buildMintRequest`'s `DISPATCH_ID_SHAPE` guard travels with it: *"the only thing allowed into it besides the prefix is the id."* **(c) `OpenLedgerRow` is DISCRIMINATED BY KIND, not by a shared id field.** Both id spaces are uuids so collision risk is nil (the D61 reasoning), but a `dispatchId` field holding a run id is **a type that lies**, and `read-and-revoke`'s handler writes `attribution_state` on a `dispatches` row — pointed at a council run id it would silently update **nothing**, which is the failure that looks exactly like success. **(d) ⚠ THE ORDERING CONSTRAINT IS INHERITED WHOLE, AND IT IS THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE GOT WRONG.** `index.ts:259–269` states the position is *"LOAD-BEARING IN BOTH DIRECTIONS"* — reconcile runs **AFTER** `dispatches.healOrphansAtBoot()` because the classifier's *"is it still running?"* input is read from the table (*"run it before the heal and every crashed dispatch still reads as RUNNING, so matrix row 1 never fires"*) and **BEFORE** `sessions.restore(...)`. **A `council_runs` row left `status='running'` by a crash needs the same heal, before the reconcile, or the classifier reads a dead run as live (row 2) and row 1 never fires — the reconcile appears to work while doing nothing, on exactly the rows it exists for.** D63 Q2 makes that heal trivially correct: a council writes no `sessions` row and **cannot** be restored, so **every `council_runs` row still open at boot belongs to a run that is already over** — the same reasoning as `healOrphansAtBoot` one layer up and as F6's *"persisted 'running' means WAS running when last observed."* **(e) PLACEMENT — a flagged, behaviour-scoped CHORE COMMIT at the head of Task 3b-3**, the D46 / D54 precedent (with D24 / D32 / D36 / D37). Three reasons: it is **provably behaviour-neutral for dispatches today because `council_runs` is empty**, which is the cheapest this change will ever be (D46's own argument, verbatim); it lands **before the first council key is ever minted**, so no window exists in which a real run's key has no backstop; and it keeps 3b-3's own commit reviewable as council work rather than as council-work-plus-a-reconcile-refactor. **⚠ G3 IS THEREFORE AMENDED FOR TASK 3b-3 TO THREE COMMITS** — the chore, the CR-checkpoint docs commit (brief + recorded findings, which must be timestamped **before** the protocol implementation), and the task itself. Recorded here rather than discovered mid-session. **PROOF OBLIGATION, the same burden every prior behaviour-neutral refactor carried:** the existing `attributionCore.test.ts` reconcile cases pass **unchanged** — the §6.1 matrix is **not** being changed, only its inputs widened — new cases place a council key at each matrix row, and **the false-positive guard is re-asserted for the widened predicate** (`Chorus-Council-…`, `' chorus-council-x'`, `'backup of chorus-council-'`, and a nameless key are all NOT ours). ***A false positive here deletes a credential the user created and depends on, with no notification and no undo*** — that sentence is `isChorusMintedName`'s own, and it now guards two prefixes instead of one. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT LICENSE:** no retention policy and no council-run pruning (`deleteCouncilRun` still has no caller); and **no expiry-based classification** — `computeKeyReconcile` still takes no clock, because D4 obligation 5 could not confirm OpenRouter stops honouring a key at `expires_at`, and classifying on it would lean on an unverified guarantee **to decide whether to destroy a credential**. **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON, and it is the second time this phase has produced this exact one:** a task doc that asserts an existing mechanism *already* covers a new table is asserting something about **code the task does not own** — `Task-3b-3.md` and its spec both did, in good faith, and both were wrong. **D62's amendment came from building the thing the roadmap described; this came from authoring a prompt against the code rather than against the docs.** Neither was found by reading. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (coordinator, pre-execution) |
| D67 | **CR-3b.1 — the deliberation & arbitration protocol.** Council-ruled 2026-07-26 (Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.1-DeliberationProtocol.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.1-Findings.md`). Verdict **REVISE**, confidence 9/10 — and **⚠ THE VERDICT HEADER IS ABOUT THE BRIEF'S OWN STATUS LINE, NOT ABOUT THE DESIGN.** All three reviewers flagged CRITICAL for the same meta-reason: the document they were handed said *"OPEN — awaiting findings"*, so they read the task as reviewing a document rather than answering its questions, then answered Q1–Q6 anyway. **The consequence is recorded rather than smoothed over: the per-question vote counts were SYNTHESIZED BY THE ARBITER from reviewers' meta-level feedback** (*"where the council reviewers implied positions through their feedback, those are noted"*), so a "3-of-3 unanimous" here carries materially less evidentiary weight than CR-3b.0's did, where each model answered each question directly. **THE RULINGS. Q1 — 1B, blind + ANONYMISED critique:** within-round blindness is already structural (the `readonly CouncilAction[]` return means no member's prompt can contain another's answer, because none of those answers exists when the batch is handed out); the critique round additionally strips labels, with the core retaining the mapping so the transcript and findings still attribute everything. **1C (decorrelated framing) REJECTED with the sharpest argument in the findings** — *"a protocol that produces dissent on command is no better than one that averages it away; the dissent is equally synthetic."* **Q2 — 2A, exactly ONE critique round** (2-of-3): a real objection is either a fact error, which one pass catches, or a values disagreement, which a second pass re-expresses rather than resolves; the protocol's value is detecting and preserving disagreement, not talking it out. **Q3 — 3C, HYBRID detection, and briefs MUST carry enumerated questions** (2-of-3, GLM dissenting for pure 3A): members return a verdict token per question plus prose, the core diffs the verdict vectors, and a member whose answer does not parse is a **question-level** structural refusal — not a global one — whose prose is still preserved and routed to the arbiter, with the detection path LABELLED per question in the output. **Q4 — 4A, THE ARBITER ALWAYS RUNS** (unanimous), and on unanimity its prompt explicitly asks whether the agreement is warranted and what the council collectively missed. The dispositive evidence is this project's own: **CR-3b.0 was 3-of-3 unanimous on four of five questions and still shipped four compile errors and three unraised gaps — unanimity was not protection.** **Q5 — 5C, the CORE generates the dissent section from the transcript; the arbiter may add narrative but cannot remove an entry** (unanimous), plus a council contribution not in the brief: **each dissent is LABELLED BY PROVENANCE** (`[Structural — Qn]` vs `[Critique — R1]`) so a reader can weigh a computed disagreement differently from a parsed aside. 5A rejected as unenforceable; 5B rejected because **a sycophantic arbiter always passes it** — it can satisfy a re-ask with a compliant-looking empty array. **Q6 — the refusal floor is STRUCTURAL:** a non-arbiter refusal continues only while ≥2 non-arbiter members remain, the arbiter refusing aborts the run, and a partial run is mechanically labelled `PARTIAL RUN — X of N`; Qwen dissents that ANY mid-run refusal makes output uninterpretable, *"because you can't know whether the refusing member would have been the dissenter who caught the error."* **⚠ COORDINATOR VERIFICATION AGAINST THE CODE AT `525c7f3`, BEFORE ANY OF IT WAS BUILT ON — six corrections, and two of them matter.** **(a) Action item 12 is unimplementable as written.** It asks for *"a Zod validation that `brief.questions` is a non-empty array"*; **no such object exists.** `council:start` carries `brief_text: string`, deliberately, because file I/O and the brief's structure belong to Task 3b-4. Corrected: a PURE question parser in `councilCore.ts` over the brief TEXT, with `assembleRun` refusing a brief whose questions it cannot find.
