The ruling is honoured; its mechanism was wrong. **(b) ⚠ THE SYNTHESIS-VALIDATION ABORT CONTRADICTS THE FINDINGS' OWN CODE-ENFORCES ITEM 9, AND IT DESTROYS A FULLY-PAID-FOR RUN.** The pseudocode aborts when `validateDissentPreservation` fails — after every member has answered, every critique has landed and the arbiter has ruled — on a string check the findings' own **R7 admits is fragile in the FALSE-NEGATIVE direction** (*"more likely with creative reframing"*). Meanwhile item 9 and R1's mitigation both say the core appends its dissent section **unconditionally**. If that is true, the abort protects nothing and costs everything. Corrected: **the core's appendix is unconditional, so the guarantee holds without the check; an elision is recorded as an OBSERVATION in the findings** — which is itself signal about the arbiter — **and never aborts.** That is strictly stronger: dissent preservation stops depending on a string comparison being right. **(c)** the pseudocode carries `disagreement` between `nextAction` calls; the function is PURE and holds no state, so it is recomputed from the transcript every call. **(d)** `turn.memberLabel` is not a field on `CouncilTranscriptEntry` (it carries `memberId`); the label is resolved from `state.run`. **(e)** `brief.questions[i]` in `extractDissentEntries` — same non-existent object as (a). **(f)** *"cost estimate per model"* in the synthesis step would require a **price table, an explicit non-goal** (D56's one-home rule; inventing a price source is a decision to raise). Corrected: per-member TOKENS are reported, and the run's one dollar figure comes from the minted key's own usage, which the provider computes — one number, one authority. **NOT CHECKABLE, and recorded as unchecked rather than accepted:** whether one critique round is empirically enough, whether cheap models comply with verdict tokens often enough for the structural arm to carry its weight, and whether `parseCritiqueSections` is too noisy in practice. All three are predictions; the task's drives give first evidence and **do not settle them.** **THE STANDING LESSON, AGAIN AND FROM THE FEATURE'S OWN SUBJECT MATTER: a council's output is deliberation, not verified fact** — true of the council that ruled this, and true of every run the feature will produce. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (CR-3b.1) |

| D68 | **⚠ TASK 3b-4's EXACT SCOPE IS TOO NARROW TO BUILD WHAT ITS OWN SPEC REQUIRES — it widens by exactly two files, and one of its acceptance criteria rests on a DEFECT nobody has seen yet.** Found by the coordinator 2026-07-26 while authoring `Task-3b-4-ExecutionPrompt.md` against the code at `456d3d7`, **not** by reading the task docs — the D66 pattern, for the third time this phase. **(1) ⚠ THE HEADLINE, AND IT BREAKS THE PHASE MILESTONE: `parseBriefQuestions` CANNOT FIND THE QUESTIONS IN A REAL BRIEF.** `Task-3b-4.md`'s dogfood drive says to *"point the council at an actual brief in `docs/Features/Foundation/CouncilBriefs/`"*. Run against the shipped parser (measured, not reasoned about): `CouncilBrief-3b.1-DeliberationProtocol.md` yields **21** "questions" and `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` yields **23** — and **not one of the first twelve of either is a question.** They are §6's constraints list, §7's weighted rubric and §4's binding rulings; the real §8 questions are drowned. The parser scans the WHOLE document for enumerated lines, which was correct against the short synthetic briefs 3b-3 tested it on and is wrong against every brief this feature exists to read. The consequence is not cosmetic: members would be asked for a verdict token on *"Windows-only v1"*, the disagreement vector and the dissent appendix would be computed over noise, and every member pays input tokens for all of it. **The fix is to scope the parse to the questions SECTION — a heading matching `/questions/i`, falling back to the whole document — and it lives in `councilCore.ts`, which 3b-4 does not own.** **(2) `assembleFindingsDocument` emits NEITHER of the two things `ImplementationSpec-3b-4.md` §3 requires it to** — verified by grep, count **0**: no **provenance** (which members ran, on which models, when, under which run id) and no **standing caveat** that findings are *model deliberation, not verified fact*. §3 calls the second one mandatory *"or a later reader will cite it as verification"*, and it is the one sentence CR-3b.0's four compile errors exist to justify. Both belong in the pure core beside the dissent appendix they sit next to — **again `councilCore.ts`**, and the run id and timestamp arrive as PARAMETERS because the core has no clock. **(3) `App.vue`'s `activeView` is `ref<'workspace' | 'settings'>` — a TWO-VALUE UNION, not a router.** D64(1) rules the council surface a view/route on the `SettingsProviders.vue` precedent, and that precedent is literally this union plus a conditional render. A third view cannot exist without widening it, so **`src/renderer/src/App.vue` is in scope** — and the task's own grep gate *"exactly 1 new `.vue` file"* still holds, because App.vue is edited rather than created. **(4) `council:start` does not need "widening", it needs its authoritative input REPLACED.** `Task-3b-4.md` says the channel is *"widened to take a validated path"*; as shipped it carries `brief_path` (a recorded LABEL main never opens) **and** `brief_text` (required, `min(1)`, the real input). 3b-4 makes the path authoritative and removes the text, which is a breaking change to a schema and a test that already exist — a replacement, not an addition, and the commit should say so. **THE RULING: 3b-4's Exact Scope gains `src/main/services/councilCore.ts` (+ its test) and `src/renderer/src/App.vue`, and NOTHING ELSE.** Everything outside that stays a raise. **Finding (1) is a BUG FIX and lands as this task's own flagged first commit** — the D46/D54/D66 precedent — because it is provably behaviour-neutral for the synthetic briefs the existing tests use, it is the thing the milestone depends on, and shipping the view on top of a parser that cannot read a real brief would make the dogfood drive discover it at the most expensive possible moment. **⚠ G3 IS THEREFORE AMENDED FOR TASK 3b-4 TO TWO COMMITS** — the parser/findings-document fix, then the task. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT LICENSE:** no protocol change (3b-3's ruled protocol is consumed, not revised), no run-archive UI, no second pattern list, and no relaxation of the enumerated-questions requirement itself — D67 Q3's ruling stands, and this fixes only where the parser looks for them. **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON, NOW THREE FOR THREE: a task doc that asserts an existing mechanism already covers a new case is asserting something about code the task does not own.** D62's amendment came from building the thing the roadmap described; D66 came from authoring a prompt against the code; this came from *running the shipped parser over the actual input the milestone names.* **None of the three was found by reading.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (coordinator, pre-execution) |

| D69 | **The two rulings `ImplementationSpec-3b-4.md` §6 deliberately left open, both settled at execution — and both went the way that costs the user less.** **(1) AN EXISTING FINDINGS FILE IS NEVER OVERWRITTEN — IT IS SUFFIXED** (`<Brief>-Findings-2.md`, then `-3.md`, …). The two rejected alternatives fail in opposite directions: **overwriting** destroys a prior run's only artifact silently, and **refusing the run** is **the D67(b) mistake in a different suit** — by the time a findings file exists to collide with, **the deliberation is already paid for**; every member has answered, every critique has landed, the arbiter has ruled, and the minted key has been spent. Aborting at the write step throws away a completed, billed run to protect a file that a suffix protects for free. **Suffixing is the only option that loses nothing.** **Proven live:** a re-run on the stub brief wrote `StubBrief-Findings-2.md` and left the original's byte length unchanged. **(2) A SANITIZATION HIT REFUSES THE RUN RATHER THAN REDACTING IT.** Redaction was rejected on two independent grounds. It **quietly changes the text several models are about to reason about** — a member would reason over a redaction placeholder and never know the brief said something else, which corrupts the deliberation the feature exists to produce. And it **buries the warning**: a redacted run succeeds, so the user never learns their brief carries a key, and the file on disk still does. The refusal reports **pattern name and 1-based line, never the value**, and it fires **before any API call**, so it costs **$0**. | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (Task 3b-4, `426ee81`) |

| D70 | **⚠ A SCOPE RAISE THE EXECUTION SESSION MADE WITHOUT ASKING — recorded as a numbered decision precisely because D68 said everything outside its two named files "is still a raise, not a licence."** **THE FACT:** the attention report's `view` field was a **two-value enum** (`workspace \| settings`) in `src/shared/ipc.ts`, and the council view is a **third**. **⚠ THE ARGUMENT THE SESSION MADE IS WRONG ON A CHECKABLE FACT, AND THE RATIFICATION CORRECTS IT RATHER THAN ADOPTING IT.** The session argued that reporting a council view as `settings` would have produced the **right CLASS (`overhead`) for the wrong reason** and written ~~a false value into a telemetry field Phase 8's estimator will read~~, invoking D50's no-backfill rule. **THAT PREMISE IS FALSE AND IT FALSIFIES IN ONE GREP: `attention_spans` HAS NO `view` COLUMN** — `schema.ts:249` and the v7 `CREATE TABLE` at `storage.ts:198` carry `class`, `tick_seconds`, `source` and timing, and nothing else. The `view` value crosses the wire on `attention:report`, is read **exactly once** at `attention.ts:273` into `rendererView`, and is consumed **solely** by `classify()` (`attentionCore.ts:120`), which collapses **every** non-workspace view to `overhead`. **It is never persisted and never logged.** Therefore widening and reverting produce **BYTE-IDENTICAL rows, today and permanently**, and **D50 does not bear on this decision at all**. The enum was widened and `classify`'s branch rewritten from `=== 'settings'` to **`!== 'workspace'`**, so the *next* view added cannot be silently forgotten the same way. Touched `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `attentionCore.ts`, `reporter.ts` and two tests. **NO schema change and NO migration** — the `attention_spans.class` vocabulary is untouched, so nothing already recorded is reinterpreted. **⚠ THE ALTERNATIVE IS STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE THE SESSION CHOSE AGAINST IT: a one-line mapping in `App.vue`** reporting the council view as `settings` would have kept the change inside D68's boundary at the cost of a knowingly false telemetry value. **The session chose the honest-but-wider option and is flagging it rather than absorbing it.** **It is CLEANLY REVERTIBLE:** the change is additive at the type level and behaviour-neutral for the two pre-existing views, so a revert restores the two-value enum and the `=== 'settings'` branch with no data migration and no reinterpretation of existing rows. **✅ RATIFIED BY MATTHEW 2026-07-26 — AND ON THE REAL REASON, NOT THE ONE THE SESSION GAVE.** `!== 'workspace'` is right because the property that makes a view `overhead` is that **no terminal pane is mounted to attribute the tick to** — true of every non-workspace view **by construction** — so the rule **cannot be forgotten** when a fourth view arrives, whereas a list of view names has to be maintained by whoever remembers. Reporting the truth on the wire is then merely free rather than load-bearing. **THE PROCESS OBJECTION WAS REAL AND WAS OVERRULED, NOT DISMISSED:** the session widened a schema D68 had not given it, and the cost of enforcing the boundary here would have been one revert of a change that turned out to be correct. **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON IS ABOUT THE ARGUMENT, NOT THE CODE — and it is the reason this row is now the longest in the table for a five-line diff.** A scope raise was defended with an **unchecked claim about stored data**, and that claim came within one ratification of being adopted as fact by the document that governs the project. **Flagging the raise was right; the reasoning attached to it was never verified before it was written down.** A raise flagged for review must state what it believes and **how that belief was checked** — a decision entry is not a place to reason from memory about the schema. | **✅ RATIFIED 2026-07-26 (Matthew) — rationale corrected; the session's stated premise was false** |

| D71 | **⚠ THE REAL COUNCIL ROSTER AND THREE COUPLED DIALS — ONE DECISION, BECAUSE THEY ARE ONE CONFIGURATION, AND THE COUPLING IS THE DURABLE LESSON.** Post-close tuning at Matthew's direction (2026-07-26, after Phase 3b closed at `d0cb138`): the council was swapped to the models he would genuinely pick, and the output allowance raised to match. **THE ROSTER**, swapped in through the app's own **`council-member:update` channel — not SQL** — all four on the standing OpenRouter route, so **the credential is unchanged and only `model` moved**: `moonshotai/kimi-k3` (member, `CR Kimi (k3)`) **$3.00/M in · $15.00/M out · ⚠ reasoning · max_completion_tokens UNSTATED by OpenRouter**; `z-ai/glm-5.2` (member, `CR GLM (5.2)`) **$0.67/M in · $2.10/M out · ⚠ reasoning · 131,072**; `qwen/qwen3-coder` (member, `CR Qwen (3-coder)`) **$0.30/M in · $1.00/M out · 65,536**; `anthropic/claude-opus-5` (**ARBITER**, `CR Arbiter (opus-5)`) **$5.00/M in · $25.00/M out · ⚠ reasoning · 128,000**. All rates and limits read from OpenRouter's **free, unauthenticated `GET /api/v1/models`** on 2026-07-26 — the F32 instrument, used again. **THE THREE DIALS, final values in `councilService.ts`:** `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_CEILING` **4,000 → 16,000 → 32,000**; `COUNCIL_MINT_LIMIT_USD` **$1.00 → $5.00 → $10.00**; `COUNCIL_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` **NEW — the transport's 120,000 ms default was inherited before — → 10 min → 15 min**. Per-member `params_json`: **arbiter 32,000, the three members 16,000**, and that asymmetry is measured rather than guessed — **the largest member answer was 11,796 output tokens**, so the members demonstrably do not need more. **⚠ `COUNCIL_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` IS SET IN `councilService.ts`, NOT IN `apiSession.ts`** — injected through the **already-existing `maxWallClockMs` seam**, so the transport keeps its 120 s default for the future interactive chat pane D45(2) binds it to, and **`apiSession.ts` stayed BYTE-IDENTICAL** across both commits. **⚠ THE HEADLINE IS THE COUPLING, AND IT WAS DISCOVERED THE EXPENSIVE WAY, IN THIS ORDER. (1) Ceiling alone (16,000) with the cap still at $1:** a single 16,000-token Opus-5 request **pre-authorizes $0.40 — 40% of the entire cap** — so the run would 402 part-way through **after paying for everything before it**; OpenRouter pre-authorizes each request against the key's REMAINING limit and refuses it before doing any work, measured in Task 3a-3, which is what makes that arithmetic rather than a surprise. **(2) Cap to $5, ceiling still 16,000: the run aborted anyway — `kimi-k3` AND `glm-5.2`, BOTH reasoning models, BOTH returned `The response exceeded its time limit and was stopped.`** while non-reasoning `qwen3-coder` answered fine in **1,479** output tokens. Cost **$0.00388**, **1 of 4 members**. **Raising the output allowance without raising the deadline is what did it: generation time tracks tokens produced.** **(3) Deadline to 10 min: the FIRST COMPLETE frontier run** — but the arbiter hit **16,000 EXACTLY on BOTH turns** and truncated again, now mid-sentence at **~95%** through instead of F38's ~5%. **$0.5586, ~12 min.** **(4) Arbiter to 32,000 + cap $10 + deadline 15 min: truncation CLOSED.** **THE RULE THIS BUYS: do not move one of these three alone.** Output ceiling, spend cap and wall-clock deadline are one setting wearing three names — raise the ceiling and the cap must clear the new per-request pre-authorization *and* the deadline must clear the new generation time, or the run fails in a way that bills for everything up to the failure. **THE BLAST RADIUS, STATED PLAINLY: $10 per run**, pre-authorized on the minted key, per D64(2)'s one-key-per-run. **⚠ AND THE HONEST LIMIT: $10 clears THIS FOUR-PARTICIPANT ROSTER, not `MAX_COUNCIL_PARTICIPANTS`** — at the 12-participant maximum a run is roughly **50 requests ≈ $40**, so the constant is sized to the roster in the DB today and **is not a bound on what the code permits.** **⚠ AND THE ROSTER IS DATABASE STATE, NOT REPO STATE:** these four rows live in the dev DB only; a fresh machine still gets whatever its own `council_members` rows say, and only the three dial constants travel in the repo. **Evidence: run `87082401-9e0a-4387-b234-638deed648df`** on `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` — arbiter **26,106** tokens at arbitration and **16,203** at synthesis, **both inside 32,000, so neither turn was cut off**; **$0.83109262 against the $10 cap · ~14 min wall clock · key revoked · ZERO open ledger rows · `grep:secrets` clean over the written file.** **Two problems remain and are filed separately: F39** (kimi refuses every run on the 4 MB transport cap, reports no usage, and leaves the council sitting exactly on D67 Q6's two-member floor) **and F40** (the duplicated dissent heading). | RESOLVED 2026-07-26 (post-close tuning, `edc3133` + `ad64e66`) |
| D84 | **⚠ `provider_configs.adapter_type` NAMES THE HARNESS THAT RUNS THE TRAFFIC — AND `'none'` IS A LEGAL ANSWER.** Settled 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-1), taken **out of phase order at Matthew's request** because it blocks his daily use of the app. **THE PROBLEM: one column doing two jobs.** `adapter_type` carried *(1)* "which CLI will run this" — the launch path's ownership check, correct and load-bearing: a credential for a Claude provider must not launch under codex — and *(2)* "which service is being talked to", which is what a council member and a model list actually need and which **has nothing to do with a PTY agent**. Every provider had to answer (1) even when only (2) was true, so an OpenRouter or DeepSeek route had to **claim `codex` or `claude`** — a false statement the launch dialog then acts on. **THE RULING: `adapter_type` answers (1) ONLY, and `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE = 'none'` (`src/shared/ipc.ts`) is the honest answer when nothing local runs it.** It is a **provider-type** value exactly as `MANAGEMENT_AUTH_MODE` is an **auth-mode** value, and it lives on the wire contract for the same reason: `adapter_type` is already `TEXT NOT NULL` / `z.string().min(1).max(60)` on both sides, so **there is no schema change, no wire-schema change and NO MIGRATION — `MIGRATIONS.length` stays 11.** **⚠ `agentKindSchema` AND `staticRegistry` ARE UNCHANGED, and that is the point rather than a side note.** They must widen **together** or **F25** returns (`layout:get`'s filter treats `getAdapter(row.agent)` membership as proof of `agentKindSchema` validity), and that lift is **D34 Q5**, owned by **Phase 3d proper** (D52), **re-affirmed by D63 Q1**. `getAdapter('none')` is `undefined`, `agentKindSchema` is still `['claude','codex']`, and the registry still holds exactly two frozen entries — asserted in `adapters.test.ts` rather than assumed. **WHAT ACTUALLY CHANGED — THE HEADLINE IS A DELETION.** *(i)* **Blocker A is gone.** `resolveMemberRoute` (`ipc.ts`) used to `agentKindSchema.safeParse(provider.adapterType)` and **refuse** *"is not configured for a known agent"* on failure, purely to manufacture an `AgentKind` argument for `resolveCredential` — whose ownership check the comment directly above it already described as **"a no-op HERE"**, because it compared `provider.adapterType` against a value derived *from* `provider.adapterType`. The gate protected nothing and its only real effect was to reject precisely the providers this task exists to admit, **at council run time, after Settings had accepted the configuration**. It now passes `null`. *(ii)* `resolveCredential`'s second parameter is `AgentKind | null`, where **`null` means "the caller is not an agent CLI"** — only the council passes it. **⚠ BLOCKER B IS GATED, NOT WEAKENED:** all three launch call sites pass a real `AgentKind` and get the identical comparison they always got. *(iii)* The two pre-lookups in `resolveMemberRoute` went with the parse they fed — they re-emitted two refusals `resolveCredential` already emits **word for word**, i.e. the "second, shorter refusal ladder that drifts from the first" its own docstring warns against. *(iv)* `adapter:list` publishes **one extra descriptor**, `NO_HARNESS_DESCRIPTOR` (`src/main/adapters/noHarness.ts`), appended **last** so the form's default is unchanged. **⚠ THE WIRE WAS ALREADY SHAPED FOR THIS AND HAD NO PRODUCER:** `adapterDescriptorSchema.id` is `z.string()` (not `agentKindSchema`) and `executionMode` is `z.enum(['pty','api'])` — **`'api'` had ZERO producers before this file.** **⚠ AND `SettingsProviders.vue` IS UNTOUCHED — ZERO LINES.** Everything its selects render already came from `adapter:list`, so routing the new type through main rather than appending a `MANAGEMENT_METHOD`-style pseudo-entry in the Vue file meant the renderer needed **no** new hardcoded label, env-var string or auth mode — which is also what kept this task off **3c-5**'s styling target entirely. **⚠ `requiredEnvVar` ON THE HARNESS-LESS AUTH METHOD IS NON-NULL BECAUSE THE RULING REQUIRES IT, not as a style choice:** `resolveCredential` refuses outright on a null env-var name, and a harness-less provider is resolved on the **council** path — a null default would ship exactly the *"valid in Settings, dead at spend time"* failure this decision exists to prevent. It is **metadata only** on that path (the transport sends `Authorization: Bearer <minted key>`), it defaults to OpenRouter's conventional name because `routeAcceptsMintedKey` refuses any route outside that gateway anyway, and a provider's own `env_var_name` overrides it (D34(e)) — which is how a DeepSeek-direct row says `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`. **WHAT HOLDS THE UNLAUNCHABILITY IN PLACE, none of it new code:** `LaunchDialog.vue`'s `eligibleProfiles` filters `adapter_type === agent`; `validateProfileShape` refuses at create; `resolveLaunchProfile` refuses at resolve — all three already reject `'none'` for every `AgentKind` **because it is not one**. Regression tests were added at each site so a future relaxation cannot silently make an unlaunchable route launchable. **ALTERNATIVES REJECTED.** **(b) Split the column** into "harness" and "route/service": cleanest semantics, and **rejected on cost/benefit rather than on principle** — it needs a migration and touches every provider read, to express a distinction that a single reserved value in an already-open vocabulary expresses with no migration at all. **It stays the right answer if a provider ever needs to name a harness AND a service simultaneously**, which nothing today does. **(c) A provider-type registry separate from the agent registry**, agents as one kind: the most general and the most work; **it is what (a) becomes if a second harness-less type ever needs its own capabilities or auth methods**, and `noHarness.ts` is deliberately shaped as its first entry rather than as an inline literal, so that promotion is a move rather than a rewrite. **(d) The naive fix — a renderer-side pseudo provider type mirroring `MANAGEMENT_METHOD` — was rejected outright**: it lets the user *create* the provider and then dies at Blocker A on the first council run, and it re-introduces into a `.vue` file exactly the hardcoded auth-method vocabulary Task 3-3's `adapter:list` exists to remove. | RESOLVED 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-1) |
| D85 | **⚠ THE MODEL SHORTLIST IS USER INTENT AND LIVES IN ITS OWN TABLE — NOT A FLAG ON THE CACHE.** Settled 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-2), on Matthew's report that *"I don't want to list all 320 models available through OpenRouter… there are probably only 10 models I plan on using."* **THE MEASUREMENT: OpenRouter's `/models` returns 341 on this machine** (F32's instrument, re-run this session). A launch picker built on that number is not a picker. **THE RULING: a new `model_shortlist(provider_id, model_id, added_at)` table (migration v12, `sqliteTable` 15 → 16), written ONLY by a click and read by the launch path.** **⚠ THE REJECTED ALTERNATIVE IS THE INTERESTING ONE: a `favourite` column on `model_catalog`.** It is one line of DDL instead of a table and it was rejected on a property, not on taste — **v9's catalog is a CACHE**, written wholesale by `applyCatalogDiff` and made explicitly non-authoritative by **D56**. A curation column on it would make **one table mean two things**, and the first person to tidy a cache with a `DELETE FROM model_catalog` would destroy a list the user built by hand. The two facts have **opposite writers** (a provider's HTTP response vs a human click) and **opposite lifetimes**, so they get opposite homes. **⚠ AND NO FOREIGN KEY ONTO `model_catalog`, WHICH IS THE SAME RULING AS D48/D56 WEARING A THIRD HAT.** A user must be able to shortlist an id **no refresh has ever returned** — the freedom those decisions protect by keeping every model input FREE TEXT with a `<datalist>` rather than a closed `<select>`. An FK here would make the catalog authoritative **BY SCHEMA**, which is precisely what they exist to prevent. Consequences, all proven on the running app: a shortlisted id **survives the model going missing, survives the catalog being emptied, and survives never having been in it.** **THE PROOF THAT MATTERS, MEASURED AGAINST THE LIVE API:** a real `model:refresh` rewrote **341 catalog rows (340 updated + 1 added)** and the shortlist came back **byte-identical**, including `vendor/never-catalogued-on-purpose`, an id OpenRouter has never heard of. **WIRE SHAPE:** `modelListResponse` gains `shortlist: string[]` — **a flat array BESIDE `models`, deliberately not a `shortlisted` flag ON each entry**, because a flag can only describe ids the catalog currently holds, so a shortlisted model that went missing would vanish from the user's own list the moment the provider stopped mentioning it. It is **required-nullable-style (required, not optional)** so a producer that forgets it fails the outbound parse (the 1b-1 `title` discipline). **ONE new channel, `model:shortlist-set` (IpcChannel 56 → 57, handlers 51 → 52)**, carrying the **DESIRED STATE rather than a toggle** — a toggle double-fires on a fast double click and silently undoes itself — and answering with the list **AFTER** the write so the renderer never renders its own optimistic guess. Idempotent both ways: re-adding is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, which also **preserves `added_at`** and therefore the user's own ordering (a shortlist is ordered by when you chose things, not alphabetically — the alphabet destroys information here). **⚠ THE HANDLER IS THE CHEAPEST IN `ipc.ts` AND MUST STAY THAT WAY: no network call, no decryption, no credential.** A convenience refresh-on-shortlist would send the user's key on a gesture that asked for nothing of the sort — the same rule that keeps `model:refresh` to exactly one caller, a click. **Purged in `deleteProviderConfig`'s existing transaction**, beside `model_catalog`, for `model_catalog`'s own reason: FKs are ENFORCED (F16) and RESTRICT would make provider:delete throw. **⚠ ONE GUARDRAIL TEST WAS DELIBERATELY UPDATED, NOT LOOSENED.** `ipc.test.ts` asserts `Object.keys(IpcChannel)).toHaveLength(56)` — **Phase 3c's** purity bound. It moves to **57** because this is **Phase 3d work, outside that contract**, so the contract is intact rather than spent. It was NOT relaxed to `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual`, which is what would actually destroy the test: the assertion it really guards is **four `window:` channels**, and a fifth would then slip through with the total still "passing". | RESOLVED 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-2) |
| D86 | **⚠ THE D34 Q5 REGISTRY FREEZE IS LIFTED. `agentKindSchema` AND `staticRegistry` GO FROM TWO ENTRIES TO THREE, TOGETHER, IN ONE CHANGE.** Settled 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-3). D34 Q5 froze the adapter registry for Phase 3 and **D63 Q1 re-affirmed it**; **D52 assigned the lift to Phase 3d**, and this is it — performed as a numbered decision rather than as an edit, which is what the roadmap's standing warning (*"consciously lifted rather than quietly ignored"*) asks for. **THE ONE RULE, AND IT IS F25's: the enum and the registry widen TOGETHER OR NOT AT ALL.** `layout:get`'s projection filter treats `getAdapter(row.agent)` membership as **proof** of `agentKindSchema` validity — true only while the registry is keyed by the enum. An id admitted to one and not the other passes the filter and then **fails the outbound parse, taking the whole aggregate down over one row**. What makes the lift safe rather than brave is **D34(b)**: `staticRegistry` is typed `Readonly<Record<AgentKind, AgentAdapter>>`, so coverage is **compiler-enforced in both directions** — a kind without an adapter is a build failure and so is the reverse. That property is precisely why D34(b) rejected *deriving* `AgentKind` from the registry, and it paid for itself here: **the typecheck found all seven `Record<AgentKind, …>` sites** (`notifications.ts`, `palette/commands.ts`, and label/code maps in `FilmstripRenderer`, `LaunchDialog`, `TerminalPane`) **rather than a user finding a blank agent name at run time.** The two-letter tile code for kimi is **`km`** (beside `cc`/`cx`). **⚠ `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE` DID NOT JOIN THE ENUM** — D84's `'none'` names the *absence* of a harness and must never become launchable; that separation is asserted in `adapters.test.ts`, and the two D84 tests that pinned the registry to a literal `['claude','codex']` were **rewritten to assert the PROPERTY** (`Object.keys(staticRegistry).sort() === agentKindSchema.options.sort()`, and `'none'` in neither) rather than re-pinned to three — a headcount assertion would make **every future adapter fail a test about something else**. **Ground fact corrected: Kimi CLI IS installed on this machine** — `kimi.exe 0.29.1` at `C:\Users\matth\.kimi-code\bin`, on PATH, already authenticated (`source=oauth`). The Phase 3d line saying it "is not yet verified as installed" was stale. `DETECTED_TOOLS` gains `'kimi'`; `cli:detect` now reports it `found=true version=0.29.1 agentKind=kimi`. | RESOLVED 2026-07-27 (Task 3d-3) |
| D87 | **⚠ KIMI HAS NO PER-LAUNCH CONFIGURATION DOOR, AND THAT ONE FACT DETERMINES THE WHOLE ADAPTER.** D4-verified 2026-07-27 against the installed `kimi.exe 0.29.1` by **running the binary**, not by reading about it. **THE TRAP, STATED FIRST BECAUSE IT IS THE EASIEST MISTAKE IN THIS PHASE: `-c` IS `--continue` ON KIMI, NOT `--config`.** On codex, `-c key=value` carries the entire OpenRouter route (D47) and is the reason `~/.codex/config.toml` is **never written**. Copying that `buildLaunch` shape to kimi would **silently resume a stale session** instead of configuring anything. `kimi --help` in full offers `-V -S -c(--continue) -y --auto -m -p --output-format --skills-dir --agent --agent-file --add-dir --plan -h` — **no config override of any kind**. Providers, model aliases, base URLs and keys are **persistent state in `~/.kimi-code/config.toml`**. **CONSEQUENCES, ALL DECLARED HONESTLY IN `kimi.ts` PER SPEC §4.2 / D34 Q1:** **`apiKey: false`** — there is no `--api-key` flag and no env var kimi reads for one, so Chorus cannot hand this CLI a key at launch the way it does claude and codex; declaring `true` would put a dead option in the provider form that the launch dialog would then act on. **`reasoningEffort: null`** — kimi *does* have an effort ladder (`[thinking] effort`, and per-model `support_efforts` / `default_effort`) but it lives in config.toml with **no CLI flag**, and Chorus's slider emits argv; **⚠ and the ladder is THREE levels (`low`/`high`/`max`), not four**, so even given a flag it would not map onto Fast/Balanced/Deep/Max without inventing a position — the same objection 3a-4 raised against stretching claude's five across four. **`skills: true`** (`--skills-dir`, verified). `mcp`/`hooks`/`sessionResume` **null** despite `-S`/`--continue` existing, because the extension methods are unimplemented and D34 Q1 makes *declared* and *implemented* one fact. **`-m` TAKES AN ALIAS, NOT A MODEL ID** — a key into kimi's own `[models."…"]` table (`kimi-code/k3`, `k3-256k`, `kimi-for-coding`, `kimi-for-coding-highspeed` on this machine), **not** a vendor id like codex's `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`. Passing an unaliased vendor id is a launch-time failure, not a fallback. **⚠ THE STANDING AUTHORIZATION, GRANTED BY MATTHEW OVER THIS SESSION'S STATED OBJECTION, AND SCOPED: Chorus MAY write `~/.kimi-code/config.toml` ADDITIVELY** so kimi can host non-Kimi models. **THE RULE IT BREAKS IS NAMED RATHER THAN GLOSSED:** `codex.ts` states that `~/.codex/config.toml` **is never written**, and this is a deliberate departure from that principle for a second agent. **THE RISKS, WRITTEN DOWN AS PROMISED:** *(1)* an inline provider carries **`api_key` in PLAINTEXT** in a home-directory file, outside `safeStorage`/DPAPI — the vault's entire premise; *(2)* Chorus becomes a **co-owner of a file the user also hand-edits**, with no merge story; *(3)* a malformed write **breaks every kimi session**, including ones Chorus did not start. **⚠ AND THE FEATURE MATTHEW ACTUALLY ASKED FOR DOES NOT NEED IT — SAID PLAINLY BECAUSE THE AUTHORIZATION WAS GRANTED BEFORE THIS WAS KNOWN.** He asked for a **KIMI button, subscription-based**; that ships with **zero config writing**, on the OAuth login already present. **⚠ WHAT THE AUTHORIZATION WOULD BUY, AND IT IS GENUINELY NEW: `kimi doctor` names the valid provider types — `anthropic \| openai \| kimi \| google-genai \| openai_responses \| vertexai`** — and hand-written `type = "openai"` blocks with a `base_url` were **accepted and listed as `source=inline`** (verified in an isolated home; **`USERPROFILE` relocates kimi's config dir**, so this was tested without touching the real one). `type = "openai"` is **chat/completions-shaped**, which is **exactly the wire DeepSeek-direct speaks and the one codex cannot do** (Task 3d-1 Probe 2: codex is `responses`-only). **So Kimi — not OpenCode — is the answer to Task 3d-1's second-harness question**, and D87's authorization is what unlocks it. **STATUS: AUTHORIZED, NOT YET IMPLEMENTED.** `kimi.ts` writes nothing today and its comments say so; a future task claiming this authorization must address risk (1) before it ships a key to disk. | AUTHORIZED 2026-07-27 (Matthew, over objection) · adapter facts RESOLVED (Task 3d-3) |
| D88 | **⚠ `env.ts` GAINS A THIRD MECHANISM, AND ITS DIRECTION IS THE WHOLE POINT: A LIST OF NAMES TO *REMOVE* FROM THE PARENT ENV.** Settled 2026-07-27 on Matthew's report that **every Claude pane in Chorus was printing, in its own terminal, `⚠ Transcript saving is off — inherited CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION marker`**. **THE CAUSE WAS CHORUS, NOT THE CLI.** Chorus started from inside a Claude Code session carries `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1` in `process.env` (confirmed set on this machine); **D33 resolution (c)'s no-credential branch inherits wholesale**, so the marker reached every pane and each `claude` **correctly** concluded it was a nested child and disabled transcript persistence. **Chorus was silently turning off transcript saving for its users' sessions on the strength of who happened to launch Chorus** — a pane is a **TOP-LEVEL session by construction**, and the launcher's identity is not session state Chorus may pass on. **⚠ AND IT IS THE F28/D54 SHAPE A THIRD TIME: THE TWO POLICIES DISAGREED.** The credential-bearing branch already excluded these markers **by omission** (they are not on the allow-list); only the inherit-wholesale branch leaked them. Fixed **structurally rather than twice**: `stripHostAgentSessionVars` is applied to `parentEnv` **once, before either branch reads it** (`src/main/adapters/env.ts:87` / `:109`), so the property belongs to the function instead of being an accident of which names happen to sit on the allow-list. **FOUR NAMES, and the last three are not padding:** `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION` (the observed cause) plus `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_HOST_SESSION_ID` and `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`. **Stripping the marker ALONE would have traded one bug for a worse one** — panes inheriting the launcher's session id, each believing it is the same session, **writing over one another's transcript**; the fix would have "worked" and corrupted the thing it restored. `ENTRYPOINT` names how the *launcher* was entered (`sdk-ts`, …) and mislabels an interactive pane at its own source. **⚠ REJECTED: A `CLAUDE_CODE_*` PREFIX WILDCARD.** One line, reads like the general fix, and **breaks a Bedrock user's routing** — the same prefix carries legitimate user configuration (`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`) that a developer sets in their own profile and expects honoured. It is the over-broad-fix shape `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` warns about, wearing the opposite hat: **an over-broad ALLOW-list leaks host state, an over-broad STRIP-list destroys host intent.** Entries are added **by observation, with the observation recorded**, exactly as the baseline demands. **⚠ REJECTED: SETTING `CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SESSION_PERSISTENCE=1`**, which is what the CLI's own warning suggests. It **treats the symptom** and would go on suppressing the banner if a **future, genuine** reason to skip persistence ever arose. Removing the false premise leaves the CLI's own default — save — in charge; the override remains available if a hard guarantee is ever wanted. **D4 EVIDENCE, TAKEN FROM THE BINARY RATHER THAN FROM THE ON-SCREEN MESSAGE:** both `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION` and `CLAUDE_CODE_FORCE_SESSION_PERSISTENCE` occur as strings inside the installed `claude.exe` (**26 and 6 occurrences**). **AND THE LINK THAT WOULD HAVE MADE THE WHOLE FIX USELESS WAS PROVEN LIVE RATHER THAN ASSUMED: node-pty on Windows REPLACES the child environment, it does not merge it with the parent** — spawned with a composed env minus the marker, the child reports it undefined. Had it merged, deleting a key would have changed nothing and every test above would still have passed. **⚠ THE DURABLE TRAP FOR WHOEVER EDITS THIS FILE NEXT — THREE LISTS, THREE DIRECTIONS:** `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` = names to **COPY FROM** the parent · `PINNED_ENV_VARS` = values to **IMPOSE** · `HOST_AGENT_SESSION_VARS` = names to **REMOVE**. Putting a name in the wrong one **compiles, reads as the fix, and does the opposite** — precisely the failure D54's own comment was written to prevent. Guarded by three tests (`env.test.ts` 12 → 15): the marker is gone on the no-credential branch with the wholesale inherit otherwise untouched; **every** listed name is dropped on **both** branches *even when an adapter asks for it via `requiredEnvVars`* (so the list is covered the moment a name joins it); and legitimate `CLAUDE_CODE_*` user config **survives**. Typecheck clean, full suite **994/994** at `e87037a` + working tree. | RESOLVED 2026-07-27 |
| D89 | **⚠ `envAdditions` APPLIES ON BOTH BRANCHES OF `composeChildEnv` — AMENDING D34(d), WHOSE LETTER SANCTIONED THE DEFECT.** Settled 2026-07-27, found while writing up D88. **THE DEFECT: a launch profile's `env_json` was silently dropped on every SUBSCRIPTION launch.** The no-credential branch early-returned `{ ...parentEnv, ...PINNED_ENV_VARS }`; only the BYOK branch ran `Object.assign(out, envAdditions)`. **⚠ AND THE REASON THIS IS A DECISION RATHER THAN A BUG FIX IS THAT THE ROADMAP SAID TO DO IT.** D34(d) words the composition as *"wholesale inherit for no-credential; baseline + `requiredEnvVars` + additions for BYOK"* — the shipped code matched its own governing clause **exactly**. **WHY THE CLAUSE WAS HARMLESS WHEN WRITTEN AND POISONOUS LATER:** on 2026-07-22 `envAdditions` had **exactly one producer — adapters — and every adapter declares `{}`** (still true of `claude.ts`, `codex.ts`, `kimi.ts`). The clause therefore described **a distinction with no observable difference**, and was accurate. **Task 3a-5 (2026-07-26) added the SECOND producer** — `launch_profiles.env_json`, resolved by `resolveLaunchProfile` and merged at `sessionManager.ts` — and stated the **opposite** intent in its own code comment: *"a profile can shape a launch but can never displace an injected credential."* Under D34(d)'s letter a profile could shape only a **key-bearing** launch. **⚠ THE TWO HALVES OF THE CONTRADICTION WERE WRITTEN IN DIFFERENT FILES BY DIFFERENT TASKS, WHICH IS WHY NOBODY SAW IT:** the intent went in at the **call site** (`sessionManager.ts`), the rule lives in the **policy function** (`env.ts`), and neither file quotes the other. **THE FEATURE WAS NOT MERELY BROKEN, IT WAS INVERTED — AND THE SCHEMA SAYS SO IN ITS OWN WORDS.** `credential_profile_id` is NULL for *"the plain 'Claude Code on my subscription' profile most users save first"* (`schema.ts:369`, verbatim), so `env_json` worked **only on the BYOK minority** and failed on the profile the schema names as the common case. **THE RULING: apply `envAdditions` on both branches, precedence UNCHANGED and now IDENTICAL on both sides — inherited < pins < additions < `secretEnv`.** **⚠ THE SECURITY ORDERING IS UNTOUCHED, AND THE ARGUMENT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CAREFUL:** on this branch `secretEnv` is **empty by construction** (it is the branch predicate), so admitting additions here **cannot** displace a credential — there is none to displace — while on the BYOK branch additions still **LOSE** to `secretEnv` exactly as D33 requires. **D33 resolution (c) is not weakened**: it is about **NOT STRIPPING** the developer's ambient environment, and nothing is stripped; what widened is what may be **ADDED**, which resolution (c) never spoke to. **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON, AND IT RHYMES WITH D60's:** D60 recorded that *an invariant naming call-site COUNTS goes stale the moment any task adds a caller*. This is the same failure one level up — **a clause that describes a distinction with no observable difference is not safe merely because it is accurate; it becomes normative text, and the task that finally makes the distinction observable violates it silently.** Both stale forms are worse than a loose one for the same reason: they get **cited as proof**. Guarded by two new tests plus one **UPDATED, NOT LOOSENED** (`env.test.ts` 15 → 17): the pre-existing key-set-equality test keeps the half it actually guards — **"nothing is STRIPPED"** still fails on any ambient variable that goes missing — and merely admits `FOO` to the expected set. Typecheck clean, full suite **996/996**. | RESOLVED 2026-07-27 |
| D90 | **⚠ THE LAUNCH DIALOG GAINS A MODEL PICKER, AND `opencode` JOINS THE REGISTRY AS ITS HARNESS — ONE DECISION, BECAUSE NEITHER HALF IS USEFUL ALONE.** Settled 2026-07-28 at Matthew's request: *"in the launch agent window we still need an option to select an OpenRouter + Model connection… next to the KIMI box… when clicked a dropdown appears that allows me to select a model."* **THE HARNESS QUESTION CAME FIRST, because OpenRouter has no CLI of its own** — a card that launches nothing is a lie, and the three candidates were codex (D47's proven vehicle), a native API pane (defers on the non-existent chat pane), or a new adapter. **Matthew chose `opencode`**, so `agentKindSchema` and `staticRegistry` go from THREE entries to FOUR, together, under D86's widen-together rule — F25 returns the moment they disagree, and the `Record<AgentKind, …>` type is what makes that a build failure in both directions. It found four files this time (`notifications.ts`, `TerminalPane.vue`, `FilmstripRenderer.vue`, `palette/commands.ts`). **⚠ THE FACT THAT MADE `opencode` ADOPTABLE AT ALL, D4-verified 2026-07-28 against the installed `opencode 1.18.8` (npm `opencode-ai`) by RUNNING THE BINARY: THE KEY REACHES IT THROUGH THE ENVIRONMENT.** With `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` set, `opencode providers list` grows an `Environment → OpenRouter OPENROUTER_API_KEY` section and `opencode models openrouter` returns **339 ids**; with the variable removed the same command fails **`Error: Provider not found: openrouter`**. So the route is GATED on the env var and **nothing need be written to `~\.local\share\opencodeuth.json`** — which is the only reason this adapter can exist under CLAUDE.md's standing secret rule. Had opencode required a file, the answer would have been no. **⚠ AND THE TRAP, WHICH IS KIMI'S TRAP AGAIN (D87): `-c` IS `--continue` ON OPENCODE, NOT `--config`.** Verbatim from `opencode -h`: `-c, --continue  continue the last session`. A copied codex `buildLaunch` would silently resume a stale conversation instead of configuring a route. It is never emitted, and `adapters.test.ts` asserts that over real argv. **Other measured declarations:** `-m/--model` IS on the top-level TUI invocation (checked, because `opencode --help` and `opencode run --help` are genuinely different flag sets); `reasoningEffort: null` because the effort knob `--variant` exists **only under `run`**, which is not the path Chorus takes; `skills: false` — a DIFFERENT answer from the other three, because opencode 1.18.8 has no skills concept in `--help` at all and declaring `true` by analogy would be the training-memory guess CLAUDE.md's CLI rule forbids; `requiredEnvVars: []` **measured, not assumed** — opencode was spawned under a CLEARED env block of exactly `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` + `PINNED_ENV_VARS` + the key, and exited 0 listing all 339 models. **MODEL TRANSLATION LIVES IN THE ADAPTER (`qualifyModel`)**: opencode namespaces models as `openrouter/deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` while Chorus stores the OpenRouter id every other consumer needs (`model_catalog`, `model_shortlist`, the council, codex's `-m`). One translation, in the one file whose business opencode's vocabulary is — the same reasoning `kimi.ts` records for aliases. It is idempotent, matches on HOST, and **passes an unrecognised base_url straight through rather than inventing a prefix** (D76). **⚠ THIS REVISES D81 AND DOES NOT REOPEN D48.** D81 said `LaunchDialog` has no model input; it now has one. **The distinction that makes this legal: D48 refused a FREE-TEXT field standing beside the route's own default — two hand-authored homes for one fact, drifting apart. This is a CLOSED `<select>` over a list main already owns** (`model_shortlist` first, `model_catalog` as fallback — **Matthew's call among three options, 2026-07-28**), whose null default means *use whatever main resolves*, which is never persisted and **writes nothing**: grep the feature for `UPDATE provider_configs` and the answer is still zero. The route's stored default survives every launch. `ImplementationSpec-3c-4`'s struck *"an `<input>` with a `<datalist>"`* check **stays struck** — a free-text box is precisely what D48 refused. **D56'S ORDER GAINS A RANK 0** — payload model > `launch_profiles.model` > `provider_configs.model` > nothing — resolved in MAIN like every other rank, so no `.vue` file holds a second precedence table. **⚠ AND IT EXPOSED A LATENT DEFECT WORTH RECORDING SEPARATELY: rank 1 WAS DEAD.** `resolveLaunchProfile` has computed `plan.model` since 3a-5 and `toWire` DISPLAYS it on the profile chip, but **no launch path ever read it** — the route always carried `provider_configs.model`, so a saved profile's own model silently did nothing. Fixed in the same change, because a live rank 0 sitting on top of a dead rank 1 is worse than either. **Verified end-to-end** on an ISOLATED second instance (port 9223, copied user-data-dir with `Local State` per F31) so the live dev app was never disturbed: four agent cards render (`oc / opencode / 1.18.8` fourth, beside Kimi as asked), the dropdown offers the four shortlisted ids, and Vue binds the default option's `null` correctly (`_value: null`, so the payload omits `model`). `npm run typecheck` exits 0; **`npx vitest run` = 1007/1007 across 30 files** (was 996 — 11 new opencode/`qualifyModel` cases). **⚠ AND ONE DEFECT THE FIRST REAL USE IMMEDIATELY EXPOSED, FIXED IN THE SAME CHANGE.** `SettingsProviders.onAdapterChange` reset the auth mode to *"the new adapter's first declared method"* on ANY adapter change. Every adapter declares `subscription` first, so repointing `OpenRouter (route only)` from `none` to `opencode` **silently rewrote a key-bearing `api_key` route into a `subscription` one** — invisible at the time (the form still saves) and surfacing only later as a credential that is no longer eligible in the launch dialog, whose `eligibleProfiles` filters on `auth_mode === 'api_key'`. It now PRESERVES a still-valid mode and falls back only when the new adapter genuinely cannot honour it, tested against `authMethods` (which includes `MANAGEMENT_METHOD`) so the account-level key survives an adapter switch rather than being quietly relabelled. **The real dev DB was never written by the VERIFICATION**: the repoint used to prove the dropdown happened in the copy. The live row was later changed at Matthew's direction, through the app's own IPC, to `adapter_type='opencode'` + `auth_mode='api_key'` + `model='moonshotai/kimi-k3'`. | **ACCEPTED** |
| D91 | **⚠ PHASE 6 IS STAGED CONNECT-FIRST, AND THE DOCKER PROVISIONER IS STAGE 5 RATHER THAN STAGE 1.** Settled 2026-07-28 at Matthew's direction (*"plan now, execute after 3e"*) — a **design decision taken outside the phase's own kickoff**, recorded as a numbered decision so the kickoff inherits it rather than re-deriving it. Full plan: [`Phase-6-MemoryPlan.md`](Phase-6-MemoryPlan.md). **THE FIVE STAGES:** **0** CR brief + D4 pass (no code) · **1** MCP capability honesty + codex wiring (**ZERO new dependencies**) · **2** connect to an existing Neo4j (`neo4j-driver`, migration **v13**) · **3** graph schema + provenance + validator · **4** `writeMcpConfig` for claude + opencode (**milestone met here**) · **5** Docker provisioner, then `skill.yaml` + `index-codebase`. **⚠ STAGE 1 GOES FIRST BECAUSE IT WRITES NOTHING, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT.** codex's MCP mechanism is per-launch argv (`-c mcp_servers.<name>.command=…`), which `codexAdapter.buildLaunch` **already** emits tokens into for the D47 route — so **the first MCP commit in this repo cannot cross a bright line by construction**, and needs no file-writing guard yet. **⚠ STAGE 2 BEFORE STAGE 5 IS THE LOAD-BEARING ORDERING CHOICE.** "Connect to an existing Neo4j" is smaller than the provisioner and — the real reason — it lets Stage 3's schema and provenance work be **proven against a real graph without simultaneously debugging container lifecycle**. Two hard problems in one commit is how a phase loses a week; `Plan.md` §10's one-click flow reads as the headline and is the *least* testable part of it. **WHAT THIS DECISION DOES NOT DO:** it does not reorder the committed 3c → 3d → 3e queue (settled 2026-07-26, all three COMMITTED WORK), and nothing in it licenses starting Phase 6 early. **The milestone is met at Stage 4**; Stage 5 is the tail, and if the phase runs long that is where it gets cut. | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (design pass, pre-kickoff) |
| D92 | **⚠ THE ISOLATION UNIT IS ONE CONTAINER PER PROJECT — WHICH IS ALSO HOW "ONE DATABASE PER PROJECT" IS ACHIEVED, BECAUSE COMMUNITY EDITION CANNOT DO IT ANY OTHER WAY.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-07-28, in his words: *"one database per project. And each project will have its own docker container. It's a little heavy on overhead, but it will all run in Docker Desktop so the user can see any container they want to turn on or off, and delete any stale containers no longer needed."* **THE CONTRADICTION IT RESOLVES:** `Plan.md` §10 states "one database per project by default", but **Neo4j 5 Community Edition supports exactly one user database** — `CREATE DATABASE` is Enterprise (⚠ D4-verify, §10 item 4 of the plan). So the *database* boundary is unachievable and the **container** boundary delivers what §10 was reaching for, with no Enterprise licence: each container's default `neo4j` database serves exactly one project. **⚠ THE DOCKER DESKTOP VISIBILITY REQUIREMENT IS DESIGN-BINDING, NOT INCIDENTAL** — it is the stated reason for the choice, and four consequences follow that are not optional. *(1)* **Container names must be self-explanatory to a human reading a list**: `chorus-neo4j-<project-slug>`, volume `chorus-neo4j-<slug>-data`. A hash or a uuid would satisfy the code and defeat the requirement. *(2)* Every container carries **labels** (`com.chorus.project.id`, `com.chorus.project.name`, `com.chorus.managed=true`) so the boot reconcile finds Chorus's own containers **structurally rather than by parsing names** — the name is for the human, the label is for the app, and conflating them is how a renamed container becomes an orphan. *(3)* **⚠ CHORUS MUST TOLERATE THE USER WINNING.** A container stopped, deleted or port-remapped behind the app's back is the **EXPECTED** case, not an error state — the user was explicitly invited to do it. The reconcile is therefore the `worktrees` pattern (D26): **the DB is journal, the world is authority, and divergence heals rather than throws.** A `project_memory` row whose container is gone is a row to heal, not an exception to surface. *(4)* **The cost is accepted and written down so it is not rediscovered as a defect:** ~512 MB–1 GB heap and two published ports per **RUNNING** project. Containers are **not auto-started**; the per-project "start memory when Chorus launches" toggle defaults **off**, which is also what keeps this clear of D33/D53 (see D93). **⚠ AND THE COST IS A CR QUESTION RATHER THAN A CLOSED ONE:** at what project count does one-container-per-project stop being reasonable, and does that answer change the design? The plan §12 puts it to the council. **ALTERNATIVE REJECTED: one shared container partitioned by `chorusProjectId` on every node.** Cheaper to run and it would make the cross-project graph `Plan.md` §10 calls "a later option" free — **rejected because isolation would become a query convention rather than a boundary**, and "destroy this project's memory" would become a `DELETE` whose correctness depends on every future query remembering the partition key. It also cannot satisfy the visibility requirement that motivated the ruling: there would be one container, and it would tell the user nothing about which projects live in it. | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (Matthew) |
| D93 | **⚠ NO SECRET VALUE REACHES ANY CLI's CONFIG FILE, IN ANY MODE — D49's BRIGHT LINE RESTATED FOR MCP RATHER THAN A NEW RULE.** Settled 2026-07-28 in the Phase 6 design pass, because wiring a Neo4j MCP server **ordinarily** means putting a bolt password into a CLI's own on-disk config, which is precisely what D49 and the AUTH-PRECEDENCE FINDING forbid. **⚠ THE LINE IS NOT ABOUT WHICH FILE, AND GETTING THAT WRONG IS HOW IT GETS CROSSED BY SOMEONE ACTING IN GOOD FAITH.** It protects four properties: *(1)* no decrypted secret at rest outside the DPAPI blob; *(2)* **vault lifecycle retained** — rotate (`shouldReEncrypt`), revoke (`deleteProfile`), mark-unavailable — none of which a key in a vendor file has; *(3)* Chorus owns the file it writes; *(4)* **detectability — and `npm run grep:secrets` covers `src/`, `scripts/`, `_verify/`, `package.json` and root configs, reaching NEITHER `~/.codex/` NOR a project's `.mcp.json`.** **G4 does not cover this phase's riskiest write.** **THE RULING, PER MODE.** **Local Docker: `NEO4J_AUTH=none`, bolt and http published to `127.0.0.1` ONLY** — there is no secret, so there is nothing to write, nothing to decrypt, and no vault interaction at any point in the container's life. **The reasoning, stated properly because it LOOKS like a weakening and is not:** DPAPI's threat model is *another user account* and *an offline disk* — **not** another process running as this user, which can read `chorus.db` and call `CryptUnprotectData` regardless. A loopback-bound auth-disabled Neo4j is therefore **no more exposed to the adversary DPAPI actually addresses**, and strictly **less** exposed in the dimension that differs: there is no credential to steal, replay, or leak into a transcript. **Manufacturing a password whose only purpose is to be handed to a config file CREATES the problem the rest of the design then has to solve.** **Existing / Aura: the password lives in `credential_profiles`, is decrypted per launch, and only its variable NAME travels** — codex via `-c mcp_servers.x.env_vars=[NAME]` (**no file at all**), claude via `${NAME}` in `.mcp.json`, opencode via a **Chorus-owned** file under `%APPDATA%\chorus\mcp\` reached by `OPENCODE_CONFIG` — **never the repo, never the user's global config.** This is the same class of fact D47/D49 already ratified for `model_providers.<key>.env_key`: **a name in argv, not a value.** **kimi keeps `mcp: null`** until its config location and interpolation behaviour are D4-established; the capability-honesty test makes that free rather than a silent omission. **⚠ REJECTED: A SCOPED, LOW-VALUE GENERATED PASSWORD WRITTEN TO THE CONFIG FILE.** Not because the secret is cheap — **because a key in a vendor file cannot be rotated by the vault, revoked by `deleteProfile`, or seen by `grep:secrets`, and it establishes the precedent that Chorus writes secrets into vendor files when the secret is JUDGED low-value. The next such judgement gets made by someone who never read D33.** "Low value" is also not obviously true: the graph will hold the project's decisions, architecture, file map and agent observations — the most concentrated description of the codebase in the app. **THREE HAZARDS, RECORDED SO THEY ARE CARRIED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED. H1 — argv is world-readable** (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, the fact `types.ts:170` already records against `extraArgs`); codex carries only names, so no widening, but say so explicitly. **H2 — the secret reaches a GRANDCHILD** (Chorus → codex → `uvx` → the MCP server), and an MCP server that prints its own password to stderr surfaces it in the pane; **mitigation costs one line** — register the value via `LaunchOptions.secrets` (`sessionManager.ts:99`) exactly as a BYOK key is, the seam D45(1) proved live over 19 streamed chunks. **⚠ H3 — THE `composeChildEnv` POLICY FLIP, AND IT IS THE NON-OBVIOUS ONE.** `env.ts:142` selects its policy on `Object.keys(secretEnv).length === 0`, so turning **credentialed** memory on for a **subscription** session would put a value in `secretEnv` for the first time and **silently flip that pane from inherit-wholesale to the eight-variable allow-list — the developer's ambient environment vanishes from a pane that worked yesterday.** Accept the flip and surface it in the UI; **DO NOT "fix" it by routing the password through `envAdditions`**, which would put a secret in the channel D33 defines as non-secret and destroy the invariant D89 just finished repairing. Local mode's `NEO4J_AUTH=none` makes H3 **disappear entirely** — `secretEnv` stays empty. **ENFORCEMENT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A CONVENTION:** a pure `assertNoSecretInRendered(rendered, knownSecrets)` runs over the **rendered bytes** of anything Chorus writes to a CLI config file or emits as MCP argv, reusing `scrubber.ts` + `secret-patterns.json`, and **refuses the write** — the `headersContainSecret` precedent (`ipc.ts:1350`). `scripts/secret-grep.mjs`'s scope comment gains a line recording that CLI config files are outside its reach, because **a gate believed to cover more than it does is worse than one that states its limit.** **AND A MACHINE-CHECKABLE COROLLARY WORTH NARRATING IN THE COMMIT:** if this holds, Phase 6 adds **no TOML writer dependency**, and its absence from `package.json` is evidence that `~/.codex/config.toml` is never written. **⚠ STATUS: this posture is itself a CR question** — the plan §12 asks the council whether "names not values" genuinely holds the line or is a rationalisation, and that is the item most worth an adversarial read. | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (design pass) · **posture referred to CR-6.0** |
| D94 | **⚠ `Plan.md` §10 IS SUPERSEDED ON FOUR CHECKABLE POINTS — ANNOTATED, NOT REWRITTEN, per the D42/LiteLLM precedent** (the roadmap wins on current status; the spec document stands and is superseded on these points only). **(1) "Uniqueness on `File.path` / `Class.fqn`" IS WRONG AND BREAKS ON CHORUS'S OWN CORE FEATURE.** Bare `path` uniqueness fails the moment a project has **git worktrees** — Phase 2's entire subject — or more than one repository. Must be **composite with a repo key**: `(File.repo, File.path)`, `(Class.repo, Class.fqn)`. **(2) "One database per project by default" IS UNACHIEVABLE on `neo4j:5-community`** — multi-database is Enterprise. Superseded by **D92**: the boundary is the container. **(3) `confidence: number` SHOULD BE DROPPED.** A self-reported LLM confidence is **uncalibrated and not comparable across models, and it WILL be read as rigor** — exactly the failure **D55** legislated against when it ruled that no attention number may be obtained without its denominator, and that **D76** restated at the pixel level. Replace with **checkable** facts: `assertedBy` (model id + adapter id) and a **derived** `corroborations` count of independent `:SUPPORTED_BY` sources. **(4) THREE PROVENANCE FIELDS MUST BE EDGES RATHER THAN STRING PROPERTIES.** `supersededBy` as a string makes the commonest query — *what do we currently believe* — a **scan**; model it as `(:Memory)-[:SUPERSEDES]->(:Memory)` with `validTo` set on the superseded node, so "current facts" is the indexable `WHERE m.validTo IS NULL`. `sourceReference` keeps its human-readable citation **and** carries a real `(:Memory)-[:SUPPORTED_BY]->(:File\|:Commit)` edge, **or provenance is unverifiable by construction**. `agentSessionId?` becomes `(:AgentSession)-[:PRODUCED]->(:Memory)` — **attribution is the entire point of this phase, and an optional field is the one that gets omitted.** **⚠ AND THE UNSTATED CONSEQUENCE OF §10's "AGENTS WRITE VIA MCP DIRECTLY IN v1 (SIMPLEST)", WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT ITEM HERE: CHORUS THEN CANNOT *ENFORCE* PROVENANCE AT ALL.** The MCP write tool takes **Cypher**; nothing stops an agent creating a `:Memory` with no source and no session. So either say plainly that provenance is **advisory** in v1, or ship the answer — **`memory:validate`, a query reporting "43 of 512 memories have no source."** That converts an unenforceable rule into a **measured** one, the same move `attributionCore`'s "% of spend attributed" already makes, and it is a direct answer to the `[CR]`'s own question. **Whether measurement is SUFFICIENT, or v1 needs app-mediated writes, is put to the council** (plan §12 question 2). | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (design pass) · `Plan.md` §10 annotated, not rewritten |
| D95 | **⚠ COUNCIL TIME IS TASK WORK, NOT OVERHEAD.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-07-28 (Phase 3e kickoff). `attentionCore.classify()` credits every non-workspace view to `overhead`, so a **~14-minute** council run contributes **nothing** to the task it was run for — which understates what that task cost, and **per D50 cannot be backfilled**, so every run under the old rule is permanently mis-attributed. **⚠ IT DOES NOT REVERT D70.** D70 ratified `!== 'workspace'` on the reasoning that *no pane is mounted* is what makes a view overhead — true of every future view **by construction**, which is why it cannot be forgotten the way a list can — and **that reasoning still holds for `settings` and for every view Phase 4 and 5 add.** What D95 adds is a narrow exception: *a view with no pane mounted is overhead UNLESS it is itself performing work attributable to a project*, and the council view is the only such view today. **⚠ A RUN WITH NO PROJECT ID STILL CREDITS `overhead`** — inventing an attribution is the D76 failure at the telemetry layer. **No sixth `AttentionClass`** (considered and declined: nothing reads a distinction that does not exist yet) and **no `view` column on `attention_spans`**, which D70 established does not exist. Owned by **Task 3e-3**. |
| D96 | **⚠ F39 IS MEASURED BY ADDING AN INSTRUMENT, NEVER BY RAISING THE CAP ON A GUESS.** Settled 2026-07-28 while authoring Task 3e-1, on the roadmap's own instruction (*"a cap raised on a guess is how an unbounded stream gets re-authorised, and D63(e) put that bound there on purpose"*). **⚠ THE QUESTION WAS UNANSWERABLE WITH THE SHIPPED CODE, AND NOBODY HAD NOTICED:** `apiSession.ts` accumulated `totalBytes`, compared it to the cap, and **discarded it** — a refusal said "too large" and nothing else. `ApiSessionDeps.onStreamBytes` now fires **once per send/receive cycle from the `finally`, on EVERY exit path**, because a capped figure alone is compatible with both of F39's hypotheses and it is the comparison against the turns that SUCCEEDED that separates them. **Carries byte counts only — never stream content**, asserted by a dedicated test, because model output can carry a credential. **No bound moved** (`RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES` still 4,000,000). Landed `49066ef`. **⚠ IT ANSWERED F39 ON ITS FIRST RUN — see the Phase 3e entry.** |
| D97 | **⚠ THE COUNCIL TRANSCRIPT GETS THE READER THE MOCK ALREADY DRAWS.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-07-28. `council_messages` is written on every run and **has never been read** — `getCouncilMessagesForRun` and `deleteCouncilRun` both have **zero callers** — so a **~$0.83 / ~14-minute** artifact cannot be re-opened. `docs/design/v2/Chorus Council.dc.html` already specifies it (a `findings \| transcript · 13 turns` toggle), so this is not a new design question. **⚠ A DECLARED EXCEPTION to Phase 3e's "no new surface" scope and to the frozen channel count**, bounded to **one read-only channel** (`IpcChannel` 57 → 58, `ipcMain.handle(` 52 → 53), **no schema change and no migration**. **⚠ RETENTION IS PART OF THE SAME DECISION, NOT A FOLLOW-UP** — a reader that makes runs re-openable forever over a table nothing deletes trades one problem for a slower one, so 3e-4 ships **either** a wired purge path **or** a written bound with the growth arithmetic done. Owned by **Task 3e-4**. |
| D98 | **⚠ THE RE-MEASUREMENT USES THE SAME BRIEF, VERBATIM.** Settled 2026-07-28. `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` already carries **four data points** (external Cursor council · Chorus cheap roster @700 · Chorus real roster @16,000 · Chorus real roster @32,000), and **F38's 4-of-6 was measured on it**. A re-measurement on a different brief measures a different thing and discharges nothing. Not edited, not shortened, not modernised. |
| D99 | **⚠ COUNCIL TRANSCRIPTS ARE KEPT INDEFINITELY, ON PURPOSE, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS DONE RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Settled 2026-07-28 in Task 3e-4, discharging D97's retention clause with option **(b)**, a written bound. **THE MEASUREMENT:** the largest transcript stored on this machine is **112,531 characters over 8 turns** (run `c06874ad`, a full four-member council); per-turn mean **14,066**, so a 13-turn run projects to **~183 KB**, and all four runs ever stored total **220,648 characters**. At a deliberately pessimistic **4 runs/week** that is **~730 KB/week, ~38 MB/year**. **⚠ THE REASON THIS IS NOT THE LAZY ANSWER — AND D97 SAYS EXPLICITLY THAT (b) MUST NOT BE TREATED AS SUCH: THE PRICE OF A RUN IS A FAR TIGHTER BOUND THAN ANY RETENTION POLICY.** A run is **~$1.09 and ~21 minutes** (3e-2's measured figures, not the old ~$0.83/~14 min estimate); the whole of Phase 3e authorised **four**. Nobody accumulates hundreds of these by accident, and **a purge is irreversible and deletes something the user paid for** — 174 KB is a trivial price for keeping a $1.09 artifact. **Shipping the reader and an auto-reaper in the same task would also be self-defeating.** **⚠ `deleteCouncilRun` STAYS UNCALLED DELIBERATELY, AND IT IS NOT DEAD CODE:** it is the atomic transaction a future **user-initiated** delete would use (it already purges `council_messages`, which SQLite will not cascade — `schema.ts:501`). A background reaper nobody asked for is a different decision with a different blast radius, and **D97 declined to make it.** **⚠ FALSIFIABLE RATHER THAN PERMANENT — the revisit triggers are stated so this cannot become a standing excuse:** if `council_messages` passes **50 MB**, or a single transcript passes `council:transcript`'s **1,000,000-character** cap (8.9× the largest measured), the arithmetic above has stopped holding and the position must be re-taken. | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (Task 3e-4) |

### Gates

| ID | Gate |
|---|---|
| G1 | Typecheck: zero errors. |
| G2 | **Run, don't just compile** — drive the real app window, observe both TUIs; screenshots when headless. |
| G3 | **One** intentional narrated commit per execution session (style of `80e69c3`). |
| G4 | **Secret-grep gate** before any phase touching credentials — no keys in args, logs, or transcripts. |
| G5 | Council Review checkpoints per D6 on all **[CR]** phases. |

## 7. Phases

| Sub-phase | Delivered | Commit |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1 Scaffold | Electron + Vue + Vite + TS skeleton (folded into 0.2) | _(in 0.2)_ |
| 0.2 Single terminal | One Claude Code TUI live in an xterm.js pane | `80e69c3` |

**Milestone (met):** two real agent TUIs run side-by-side in one window, backed by a persistent SQLite store, with exit notifications wired.

### Phase 1 — Grid + Projects — ✅ COMPLETE (2026-07-19)

_Five serial tasks, each planned (`Task-1-#.md` + `ImplementationSpec-1-#.md`), executed in its own session, and coordinator-reviewed (`Task-1-#-CompletionSummary.md`). Two council reviews ran: CR-1.2 (layout engine → D9) and CR-1.5 (restore contract → D16). Decisions D7–D17 and findings F2–F11 all trace to this phase._

| Task | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **1-1** Tailwind + session lifecycle UI | Status dots driven by exit codes; restart/kill per pane, incl. clean process-tree kill; exit banner removed. | ✅ `185f972` |
| **1-2** Data layer | Drizzle typed queries; migration v2 (`sessions`); stable session ids; flat layout → versioned binary tree (lazy conversion); Vitest (24 tests). Zero visual change. | ✅ `81e8a0b` |
| **1-3** Layout view | `LayoutRenderer.vue` over the persisted tree via splitpanes; `layout:set` IPC; debounced PTY resize; close-kills-session. **Spike gate returned GO** (12/12 checks, ~45 min of a 4h box). Split buttons disabled as planned. Filmstrip spike ran and was deleted; notes kept. Produced D14 + findings F2/F3/F4. | ✅ `e7d6e60` |
| **1-4** Launch dialog + multi-session | D10 rekey landed first as a verified standalone refactor; explicit launch flow (`session:launch` + dialog + empty state); N sessions per agent kind proved (3 panes, 2 independent Codex TUIs); both 1-3 guards cleared; D11 wired. - **Milestone — MET 2026-07-24, and here is precisely what was and was NOT proven.** **Proven:** a real credential, held in the real dev vault, decrypted at launch only, injected into a child PTY's environment block and **nowhere else** — verified by walking the live process tree and reading each descendant's command line and environment (codex.exe plus the `node_repl.exe` and MCP `server.mjs` children it spawns on its own, all clean); the agent then **actually answered** over the OpenRouter route on a non-GPT model, so the machinery is exercised rather than dormant. Note the milestone wording above says "planted fake keys only" — the final proof went further and used a **real** key, deliberately (D49). **NOT proven, and must not be claimed:** **(1) multi-turn** — OpenRouter's Responses endpoint is **beta and stateless-only** (400s on `store: true` / `previous_response_id`), so only a **single turn** was driven end-to-end; compaction is untested. **(2) A universal "the key can never appear in a transcript" guarantee is FALSE, per F27** — Claude Code 2.1.218 prints a **masked ≥8-char fragment** of an injected key in its own auth prompt, and exact-value scrubbing cannot match a derived form by construction; Chorus is protected here only because **D44 makes `claude` subscription-only**, i.e. by policy, not by mechanism. **(3) The APPDATA-free allow-list is settled for LAUNCH + TUI ONLY** — both CLIs start and run without `APPDATA`, but MCP servers, plugin sync, and other deeper features were never exercised and remain unknown. **(4) Four `running` session rows are stale in the live DB** and one of them is credentialed — the documented heal runs at next boot; expected behaviour, not a defect (§5).

### Phase 3a — Profiles & Catalog — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26** _(kicked off 2026-07-24 per D50; created by D28)_

**Sequenced first by D50 on asymmetric decay: 3a is the only candidate with a clock running against it.** It carries Mission Control's spec-Phase-0 telemetry capture, historical actuals cannot be backfilled, and the projection phases are worthless without 3–4 weeks of data — so **telemetry capture is the FIRST task of this phase, not its last**. 3b and 3c lose nothing by waiting; 3a loses data permanently.

**Kickoff ran 2026-07-24 and settled four decisions (D51–D54, §6).** The phase is decomposed into **five serial tasks** in [`Tasks/Phase-3a-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3a-Overview.md), with `Task-3a-1.md` … `Task-3a-5.md` and their five paired ImplementationSpecs authored against the verified `15a016e` codebase.

| Task | Scope | Migration | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **3a-1** | **TERM pin (flagged chore, D54) + the dispatch telemetry spine.** Two commits. `dispatches` (wall-clock, agent, model, `auth_mode`, outcome; token/cost columns declared and written NULL) + `attention_spans` created empty; `DispatchRecorder` + `classifyOutcome`. No UI, no IPC, no renderer file. | **v7** | ✅ **COMPLETE** — `6a7c6ae` (chore, D54/F28) + `8a2e8c3`, 2026-07-25 |

Writes `attention_spans`; authors no migration. **The one-tap correction control is DEFERRED, not dropped** — see below. | — | ✅ **COMPLETE** — `fd4c9e5`, 2026-07-25 |
| **3a-3** | **Per-dispatch token & cost attribution — and the multi-turn proof, which is a GATE allowed to fail.** Per-dispatch OpenRouter keys minted with a hard limit, revoked and read back; attribution keyed on `AuthMethodDefinition.type` (D42); `tokens_cached` separate; "% of spend attributed" surfaced; subscription sessions **never** gateway-routed. | **v8** | ✅ **COMPLETE** — `f56dcaf`, 2026-07-25 |
