Dependency chain: **3a-1 → 3a-2 → 3a-3 → 3a-4 → 3a-5** (strictly serial). **Migration numbers are FIXED, not conditional** — the five docs were authored in parallel and each hedged; serial execution makes them deterministic. Every implementer confirms `MIGRATIONS.length + 1` matches before appending and **stops on divergence** rather than renumbering.

- **Coordinator amendment during authoring, 2026-07-24:** Task 3a-2 raised a dependency finding against 3a-1's not-yet-shipped v7 DDL — `attention_spans` lacked `class` and `tick_seconds`, without which attention has a numerator but no denominator and the accounting identity is uncomputable. **Both columns were added to v7 before it shipped, at a cost of two columns.** This is the "raise it, don't work around it" boundary working exactly as designed — **and it paid off**: 3a-2 shipped against the amended DDL with no migration of its own, exactly as the boundary was drawn to allow.

**Task 3a-1 landed 2026-07-25** (`6a7c6ae` chore + `8a2e8c3`): migration v7 on the real dev DB through the full three-dump protocol, the `DispatchRecorder` hung off spawn/exit/boot-heal/quit, and `TERM`/`COLORTERM` pinned per D54, closing F28.

**Task 3a-2 landed 2026-07-25** (`fd4c9e5`, 13 files). **The clock is running — which was the entire point of D50's sequencing.** What it settled, and what later phases inherit:

- **Sampled runs, not intervals.** One `setInterval` in main at a 15 s cadence; each tick extends the open row **in place**. Worst-case loss on a tree-kill is **one tick, independent of session length** — measured at **5.973 s**, against **87.410 s** for the write-on-transition design deliberately tested against it. The cadence is recorded **per row** (`tick_seconds`) so changing it cannot silently rewrite what historical rows mean.
- **Credited time is `samples × tick_seconds`, never a wall-clock delta.** A firing that arrives ten minutes late credits **one** sample and starts a fresh run, so suspend, hibernate and clock skew cannot inflate the number. The gap stays visible between runs, where `coverage()` finds it.
- **No retro-debit (an ambiguous spec clause, ruled).** §5.3's "anything over a 60-second idle threshold does not count" admits two readings; ruled that the **excess** does not count and the first ≤60 s is **never revoked**, because revoking means mutating committed rows and reopening the crash window the design exists to close. Three minutes of no input credits **4 pane samples then 8 idle** — pinned by test and observed at runtime.
- **⚠ The denominator is now STRUCTURAL, and this is the part Phase 8 inherits.** `attention:summary` has **no `minutes` field at all**: it returns `samples`, `byClass`, `expectedSamples`, `missingSamples`, `coveragePct` and a required `estimateBound: 'lower-bound'`, and the caller multiplies. A denominator-less response **fails the outbound Zod parse in main** rather than shipping a bare figure — the D33-clause-3 move applied to a different kind of dangerous value. See **D55**.
- **`blurred` is recorded, not dropped.** §5.3 scopes overhead to "time spent in Chorus", so another app on top is not overhead — but dropping it would leave a consumer unable to distinguish "the day happened elsewhere" from "the day did not happen".
- **⚠ The one-tap correction control from §5.3 is DEFERRED with a named home, not dropped.** It needs a task-completion moment, and Chorus has no tasks, no dispatch outcomes and no completion surface until the board ships — building it now would mean building a fake screen to hang it on (the Task 3-4 "no dead UI" bar). **It is owed a shape that makes it purely additive, and it has one:** every row 3a-2 writes carries `source='measured'`, so a correction arrives later as a **new row** with `source='corrected'` rather than an edit. **Its home is Phase 8**, alongside the completion surface. The preserved pair is itself the payoff — **"% of attention hand-corrected" is this subsystem's honesty metric**, exactly as "% of spend attributed" (D42) is for cost.
- **A recorded finding, deliberately NOT fixed here:** `LayoutRenderer` binds no `@focus` on its `<TerminalPane>`, so grid mode never updates the persisted `focusedSessionId`. Fixing it changes persisted view state and belongs in its own commit. **It is also why the renderer walks the DOM for focus instead of reading `focusedSessionId`** — a tracker on the persisted value would attribute an entire grid-mode session to whichever pane was last focused in the filmstrip, which is *confidently wrong* and therefore worse than missing.
- **Verified, not asserted (G2 was load-bearing here and was treated that way).** Driven against the real dev DB with real OS input: pane A **2.25 min**, pane B **1.25 min**, overhead **1.25 min**, each exact; blur froze every other counter; the grid repeat attributed identically to filmstrip; the accounting identity held across **190 ticks**; one clock proven by rate (15 panes mounted → 4 ticks/min, 1 pane → 4 ticks/min); the kill switch flipped on a **running** app wrote 0 rows while off and resumed with no restart. Both §9.3 falsification inversions were driven and reverted, with the reverts proven against the commit diff. Full record: `_verify/3a-2/RESULTS.md` and `STEP0-RESULTS.md`.

**Task 3a-3 landed 2026-07-25** (`f56dcaf`, 16 files, migration **v8**). **The multi-turn gate PASSED**, so the phase is no longer building on an assumption. What it settled, and what later phases inherit:

- **⚠ MULTI-TURN IS PROVEN; COMPACTION IS NOT — and F-36-1 named BOTH.** Three turns through the shipped codex → OpenRouter route: turn 2 returned the planted token, turn 3 returned it **and** correctly counted the conversation; zero `400`s, `previous_response_id`, `store` or config-load errors across 138 KB of ring buffer. The endpoint is stateless; **codex resends the history**, which is what the gate actually measured. But the run also emitted `⚠ Model metadata for 'moonshotai/kimi-k3' not found. Defaulting to fallback metadata` — so codex decides *when to compact* from fallback numbers. **No later phase may assume compaction over this route** on the strength of this gate.
- **⚠ A HARD CAP IS A PRE-AUTHORIZATION CEILING, NOT A BUDGET — and $0.50 was too small to run anything.** OpenRouter pre-authorizes each request against the key's remaining limit; codex asks for 65,536 output tokens, so a $0.50 key is refused outright (`402 Payment Required … you requested up to 65536 tokens, but can only afford 46666`) before any work happens. The cap was not bounding spend, it was preventing the dispatch. **Raised to $1.00 by Matthew's explicit decision**, the task having required asking rather than raising. Any future cap must clear the model's max **output allocation**, not its expected spend.
- **⚠ THE PUBLISHED ANALYTICS METRIC LIST IS INCOMPLETE, and D4's "verify, don't remember" earned its keep.** The docs list `cache_hit_rate` and `usage_cache` and **no cached-token metric**, so the design was built to derive one and label it `analytics-derived`. The live `GET /api/v1/analytics/meta` returned **35 metrics including `cached_tokens`** — a measured count. The code prefers it and falls back to the derivation, recording which it used. **The beta analytics schema is now logged once per boot** so this drift stays visible instead of being rediscovered.
- **The separate `tokens_cached` column is now justified by measurement, not argument:** the first real dispatch was **40,960 of 83,232 prompt tokens cached — 49.2%**. Folding that into `tokens_in` would have priced 41k cached tokens at fresh-input rates.
- **Two D4 non-answers changed the design rather than being worked around.** `PATCH {disabled:true}` is not documented as immediate → **the disable step was deleted**; `DELETE` is the revocation and usage is always read first. Analytics freshness is undocumented → **the deferred backfill is mandatory**, and it fired twice at runtime.
- **The subscription prohibition is STRUCTURAL, not behavioural.** `keys.mint` has **exactly one call site**, inside the `'minted-key'` branch, decided by a function whose first statement returns on `AuthMethodDefinition.type` and reads nothing else. Proven behaviourally too: a real `claude` launch minted nothing, carried no `OPENROUTER_*` variable, inherited the full 91-var environment (vs 15 on a credential-bearing launch), and its command line had no `-c model_provider`.
- **The positive injection check turned out decisive, and the technique is reusable.** A 402 revealed that **OpenRouter's key `hash` is the SHA-256 of the key**, so `sha256(child's environment value) === dispatches.minted_key_hash` — not "a key was injected" but "that process holds exactly the key Chorus minted", verified without any script printing a byte. Zero ≥8-char key fragments across 454 processes.
- **The management key is a new credential class with a home and two guards.** An ordinary vault row on a provider whose `auth_mode` is `'management'` — no migration, no wire-schema change. **`resolveCredential` refuses it BEFORE the decrypt**, so it is never decrypted on a launch path; `LaunchDialog` already filters `auth_mode === 'api_key'`, so it is invisible to the picker for free. Proven at runtime: naming it directly on `session:launch` is refused **by label**, with no spawn and no session row.
- **⚠ ONE `.vue` FILE WAS TOUCHED, widening the task's own non-goal by exactly one file, with approval.** Step 3 needed a management-key entry affordance and none could exist — the auth-method `<select>` renders only what the adapters declare. The option is sourced from a shared constant, **deliberately NOT from `AuthMethodDefinition`**: widening that union would have made "Management key" read as a way to launch an agent, pushing the highest-privilege credential toward the path the task exists to keep it away from.
- **Mint failure DEGRADES, and the boundary with D33 is exact.** A failed mint launches on the user's own key with `attribution_state='mint-failed'`, counted against "% attributed". **D33 governs credentials; this governs a meter** — a decrypt failure still refuses the launch, unchanged.
- **"% of spend attributed" is real and the gap is legible:** **26.4% of gateway dollars, 5.6% of dispatches**, with 66 subscription dispatches **counted and priced at nothing**. Every ratio ships with the counts it came from and a `spendBasis: 'gateway-only'` qualifier; the outbound parse rejects a denominator-less response, and the response key set is pinned by test so no field able to carry a key or a hash can be added. **D55 now has a second enforcement site.**
- **⚠ THE COST ENVELOPE WAS EXCEEDED: $0.533 actual against `< $0.30`.** The overrun is **the gate's, not attribution's** — three kimi-k3 turns at `xhigh` against this repo's context cost ~$0.39 against a $0.05 estimate, while the attributed dispatches came in at $0.14 against $0.20. **No per-key ceiling was ever breached.** Future estimates for this model must budget per-turn context, not per-turn text.
- **Known gaps, recorded rather than smoothed:** `reasoning_tokens` has **no column and is therefore not captured** (deliberately not folded into `tokens_out` — whether `tokens_completion` already includes it is unverified, and folding on an unverified relationship is the cached-input error again); `expires_at` remains **unproven** as revocation (D4 obligation 5), so of the three orphan layers only the hard `limit` and the boot reconcile are demonstrated; and **subscription metering has never produced a number in anger** — its ambiguity guard returns "unknown" whenever two CLI sessions share a dispatch's window, which on this machine is common. Full record: `_verify/3a-3/RESULTS.md`, `STEP0-RESULTS.md`, `D4-VERIFICATION.md`.

**Task 3a-4 landed 2026-07-25** (`3433c91`, 20 files, migration **v9**). Its headline output is a ruling rather than a feature, and four decisions came out of it — **D56** (the model-precedence order), **D57** (refresh ≠ Test key), **D58** (D33(d) widened by exactly one call), **D59** (effort precedence and the one-home mapping). What later phases inherit:

- **⚠ THE CATALOG IS A LIST, NOT AN AUTHORITY — and that is D56, which binds 3a-5 directly.** The full reasoning and its three structural enforcement sites are on the decision. The part worth repeating here: **every worked combination of (catalog × route default × launch choice) resolves to exactly what it would resolve to with no `model_catalog` table in the database at all.** That equivalence *is* the acceptance test for the whole ruling, and it was driven — not argued — with the route's default pointed at a missing model and the `provider_configs` row coming out byte-identical.
- **Staleness is a rendered fact with THREE states, and they must never collapse into two.** *never refreshed* / *fresh* / *stale* — with `'never'` its own rendering, because an implementation that shows it through the stale branch looks right on a populated database and wrong on **every fresh install**. The 24 h threshold has **one home, in main**; the renderer receives a computed value and does no date arithmetic. **A stale list is still SHOWN** — hiding it pushes the user back to typing ids from memory, which is the behaviour that produced `kimi-k2.7`. All three states were captured rendering side by side in one screenshot.
- **A model that disappears is MARKED, never deleted, and its date NEVER MOVES.** Deleting the row destroys the only evidence the id was ever real — precisely the fact someone staring at a failing saved route needs. A date reset on every refresh would read "missing since today" forever. Proven with a control: on the second still-missing refresh the mark held while a live model's `refreshed_at` moved in that same write.
- **⚠ Row 11 of the precedence table is the DISCIPLINE row, and it is easy to lose.** Only a model that was **catalogued and then disappeared** earns a warning. An id the catalog has **never seen** produces none — users legitimately name ids a provider's list does not carry, and a warning that fires on the normal case is a warning nobody reads.
- **Nothing auto-refreshes. Ever.** Not at boot, on Settings open, on provider create, on credential create, on launch, or on a timer. This is D33 resolution (d)'s discipline applied to the app's second live call (**D58**), and it is also what keeps the cost envelope at zero and the user's key un-decrypted unless they asked.
- **The effort mapping is one home, and the raw override suppresses it entirely (D59).** All eight command lines were read off live child processes — four codex `-c model_reasoning_effort="…"`, four `claude --effort …` — walking **down** the tree from the electron main PID rather than name-matching. **A no-effort launch is byte-identical to the pre-change launch**, captured at `f56dcaf` via a temporary stash and again after, and diffed. When an adapter declares no effort axis the control is **ABSENT, not greyed** — proven with a temporarily nulled descriptor: not even the word "Effort" appears in the DOM. **No explanatory text in its place either; absence is the message.**
- **D4 was made to answer, not remembered — and it moved the weakest fact in the authoring set.** The evidence for codex's `model_reasoning_effort` had been *strings read out of the binary*, which proves a symbol exists, not that a value is accepted. This session made the binary reject an invented field by name and accept this one, then name this one's type in an error (**F33**). The same probes found the honest limit: the field is typed String, so **request-time behaviour for a level the model does not support remains unmeasured** and is recorded as a gap.
- **Two deliberate deviations, both narrated in the commit.** **No `tier` column**, though PLAN §13 names one — nothing can fill it honestly, so it could only hold a hardcoded guess about third-party names that would rot within weeks. **No pricing stored at all** — a cached price is a number that is one day wrong in an expensive direction, and 3a-3 already reads real spend. And one deviation from the task's own spec: **the ingest charset gained `~`**, because the spec's regex would have dropped 10 of 345 real models on every refresh (**F32**).
- **A harness lesson worth carrying, because it cost real data before it was caught.** A cleanup script that walked the **pane layout's leaves** deleted the sessions they pointed at — four pre-existing *exited* rows, unrelated to the task. The layout is not a list of live sessions; it is a list of ids, some of them stale. They were restored byte-for-byte from a start-of-session dump and the restoration proved by re-comparison, but **the general rule is the one to keep: a harness that deletes should enumerate what it means to delete, never dereference a pointer structure and trust it.**
- **Cost: $0.00, against a $0.00 envelope** — the first task in this phase to hold it exactly. `/models` is unmetered, and the effort proof is an argv read rather than a completion.

**Task 3a-5 landed 2026-07-26** (`341ea5c`, 13 files, migration **v10**) — **and Phase 3a is CLOSED.** Its headline output, like 3a-4's, is a ruling rather than a feature, and three decisions came out of it: **D60** (the no-unattended-decryption guarantee has two credential classes, and one was widened without a decision), **D61** (the derived fail-safe credentialed mark, and the defect that would have reintroduced F26), **D62** (`launch_profiles` takes the opposite FK ruling from its two neighbours, deliberately). What later phases inherit:

- **⚠ THE PHASE'S SECURITY SENTENCE WAS ALREADY FALSE, AND NOTHING HAD RECORDED IT (D60).** Task 3a-5's governing docs stated the invariant as *"exactly TWO `decryptForLaunch` call sites, both inside `registerIpc`; `index.ts` shows the vault constructed and passed to `registerIpc`, and nothing else."* At `3433c91` there were **four**, and one was in `index.ts` on a path reachable from `app.whenReady` — added by **Task 3a-3**, for the management key, with sound reasoning written in the code but **never as a numbered decision**. An instrumented boot at the unmodified HEAD logged **three** management decrypts before the window existed. The guarantee is now stated **by credential class** and the management class is **explicitly admitted** to unattended boot-time resolution on four constraints. **The standing lesson: an invariant that names call-site COUNTS goes stale the moment any task adds a caller, and a stale invariant is worse than a loose one, because it gets cited as proof.**
- **⚠ THE RETIREMENT NEARLY REINTRODUCED THE FAILURE IT PRESERVED AGAINST (D61).** The spec's own launch handler left `sessions.launch_profile_id` NULL for a launch on a **bare credential with no profile** — a path 3a-5 deliberately keeps. NULL reads as *not credentialed*, so restore would have relaunched those sessions **keyless**. Caught at execution; fixed by giving them the same unresolvable sentinel the migrated legacy rows carry. **The fail-safe branch is mutation-tested**, because inverting it is a one-character change that compiles and passes every happy path.
- **The credentialed fact is now STRUCTURAL, not remembered.** Written on the **same INSERT** as the session row (closing the crash window 3-6's mark-after-launch left open) and dying with the row, so `session:delete` needs no unmark call. Restore's behaviour is unchanged and the diff is the evidence: **one line moved** — same healed status, verbatim title, verbatim log line, same `session:restart` refusal. The negative control ran in the same boot, so "nothing decrypted" could not be confused with "restore is broken".
- **The model-precedence ruling (D56) survived contact, and the proof is a database column.** A profile naming no model inherits the route's default at resolve time and is **never back-written**: after a live launch the profile's `model` column was still **NULL** while the child process carried `-m moonshotai/kimi-k3`. The effort vocabulary is **imported** — the membership set is built from `effortLevelSchema.options`, so it cannot drift — and `src/main/adapters/` is **byte-identical** across the commit.
- **Three tables, two opposite FK rulings, both written down (D62).** `dispatches` and `model_catalog` carry none (a fact and a cache tolerate dangling); `launch_profiles` carries two, because a live instruction must not outlive its targets **and because RESTRICT forces the refusal to be authored in main**. `credential:delete` had **no guard at all** before this task. Both guards driven live, with the new provider guard **isolated on a route with no credential profiles** so the pre-existing 3-2 guard could not mask it — an earlier run proved nothing precisely because it did.
- **⚠ F31 IS SOLVED, and it is a one-line fix rather than a mystery.** `safeStorage` blobs are wrapped with **Chromium's OSCrypt key, stored in `<user-data-dir>/Local State`**. Copy `chorus.db` without it and every pre-existing blob is undecryptable while same-boot blobs work — exactly the asymmetry that cost Task 3a-3 an hour. Demonstrated both directions during this task's migration rehearsal. **Copy `Local State` beside the database, and treat a credential blob as bound to the user-data DIRECTORY, not just the Windows user.** See the F31 row in §5.
- **The migration proof, and two exceptions declared rather than discovered.** v10 on the real dev DB, rehearsed on a copy first: every prior `applied_at` byte-identical, every pre-existing table row-identical, `launch_profiles` created empty with both `REFERENCES` and `UNIQUE(label)`, not re-applied on boot 2. **`dispatches` and `attention_spans` are append-only live telemetry** and cannot be byte-identical across a boot that ran at all, so what is asserted is that **no existing row moved**. The docs said the retired settings list held **two** session ids; it held **nine**. All nine migrated.
- **Cost: $0.00 against a `< $0.05` envelope.** The argv read, the environment-block read and the discriminated decrypt counts were the proofs, so no completion was submitted. Test key was never pressed against "OR milestone key".
- **⚠ Known gap, recorded rather than smoothed over.** The launch and relaunch drives ran through **IPC, not the dialog UI**. Main's resolution, the decrypt discrimination, argv and the child environment block are all proven; **the dialog's `submit()` wiring and the Settings rename/delete controls are typechecked but never clicked**, and the two-project scoping drive was not run (Chorus-Second has no sessions) — the per-project key is proven by `settings` dump instead. First UI-touching work in this area should exercise them.



The Phase-3 scope split out so the vault and the adapter interface got undivided sessions: `model_catalog` table + refresh (~~the "list models" call doubling as Test key~~ — **⚠ SUPERSEDED by D57, 2026-07-25: the merge was argued and REJECTED on a measured fact — that endpoint answers 200 with no credential, so a "Test key" built on it could not fail**); **effort normalization** (one app-level Fast/Balanced/Deep/Max slider mapped per adapter, raw override in `extra_args` — PLAN §4); `launch_profiles` (saved provider + agent + model + effort + permission mode + workspace mode + env); the launch dialog defaulting to last-used profile; and **`usage_records`** capture — which needs an api-mode producer to be honest, since PTY agents emit no token counts.

- **⚠ TELEMETRY FIRST — this is the decay-sensitive item and the reason 3a is next (D41, D50).** Mission Control's spec **Phase 0** is ~half a day of capture work whose value is destroyed by delay: **actuals cannot be backfilled**, and spec phases 2+ are worthless without 3–4 weeks of accumulated data. It must be the phase's **opening task**, ahead of catalog and profile UI work, so the clock starts on day one rather than at phase close.
- **⚠ `usage_records`'s stated blocker is SOLVED — but by OpenRouter, not LiteLLM (D42; supersedes the LiteLLM producer named in D41(d)).** This phase records that `usage_records` "needs an api-mode producer to be honest, since PTY agents emit no token counts." The producer is a **per-dispatch OpenRouter key** minted via its Provisioning API with a hard `limit`, revoked and read back at dispatch end. Attribution then holds *regardless of whether the CLI forwards metadata* — which is the real reason the naive `metadata: {task_id}` approach fails — and budget caps plus revocation of a stuck pane come free. **Two consequences:** (1) `usage_records` should be designed against this producer rather than waiting for an api-mode adapter; (2) Mission Control's time-sensitive spec-Phase-0 telemetry capture is *this same work* — build it once, here, not twice. **⚠ Newly relevant: Task 3-6 has PROVEN the OpenRouter route end-to-end** (`15a016e`; codex 0.145.0 → `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` → `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, key injected via `env_key`, answer received), so the producer's transport is no longer theoretical — **but only single-turn is proven** (F-36-1: the endpoint is beta and stateless-only), and **this phase must prove multi-turn before depending on it**.
- **⚠ Attribution strategy is keyed on AUTH MODE, not on the gateway (D42).** Routing a subscription-authenticated agent through any gateway converts a flat-rate subscription into per-token API billing — so cost tracking would *increase* cost. `AuthMethodDefinition.type` (`'subscription' | 'api_key'`) has existed per-adapter since 3-3 and is the discriminator: **`api_key` sessions** get the per-dispatch OpenRouter key (full fidelity); **`subscription` sessions** are never gateway-routed and are metered from the CLI's own local session logs (lower fidelity, keeps the flat rate). Surface **"% of spend attributed"** as a first-class metric so the gap is visible rather than silently under-reported. **Must capture `tokens_cached` separately:** cached input is priced roughly an order of magnitude below fresh input and a PTY agent against a large `CLAUDE.md` hits cache constantly — a schema that ignores it projects badly wrong in the expensive direction.
- **⚠ `launch_profiles` is the (agent × route × model) triple with a stable id and a renameable label (D43).** The friendly-name picker Matthew wants *is* this table: the same model reached by different routes must list as distinct, user-named entries (`OR/DeepSeek v4 Pro` vs `Direct/DeepSeek`; Claude Opus 4.8 by subscription CLI vs by OpenRouter). The **route half already exists** — `provider_configs` (`name`, `adapter_type`, `auth_mode`, `env_var_name`, `base_url`, `extra_headers_json`) is the route and its `name` is already user-authored, so routes need **no schema change**. Only the model half (`model_catalog`) and the pairing (`launch_profiles`) are new here.
- **⚠ INHERITED FROM 3-6 — `provider_configs.model` ALREADY EXISTS (migration v6, D48), and it is the ROUTE'S DEFAULT, not an authority.** The v6 column shipped in `15a016e` and is populated on the live route (`moonshotai/kimi-k3`). Its semantics are fixed by D48: **`launch_profiles` OVERRIDES it**; it is read only when a provider carries one, and it is nullable because subscription routes have no model to name. **Therefore 3a must NOT create a second competing home for "which model."** `model_catalog` is a *list of what exists*, `provider_configs.model` is *this route's default*, `launch_profiles.model` is *the choice for this launch* — three roles, one precedence order, and the kickoff must write that order down explicitly. Phase 3's non-goal barring catalogs/caching/refresh applied **to Phase 3**; `model_catalog` is this phase's declared scope, so the kickoff should record the lift explicitly rather than assume it.
- **⚠ INHERITED FROM 3-6 — `launch_profiles` is the home for the DEFERRED restore option (a), and it SUPERSEDES the global marked-session list (D49, closes F26).** 3-6 implemented decision (b): credentialed sessions are marked in a `settings` id list and **healed to `exited`** on restore rather than relaunched keyless. Option (a) — **re-resolve the credential at restore** — was declined because it needs **unattended boot-time decryption** and would have added a second `sessions` schema change to the v6 session; a profile row that already names provider + credential + model is the natural place to resolve from. The existing list is **global rather than per-project**, an accepted **Phase-3-only expedient** (uuid ids, collision risk nil) that this phase should retire. **If 3a implements (a), it must also rule on the vault-lifetime question D33 never sanctioned: what may be decrypted without a user present?**
- **⚠ Two new PTY adapters are wanted, and this is their most natural home — but placement is a KICKOFF DECISION, not settled here (D44/D45).** A launch profile of the shape "Aider × OpenRouter × DeepSeek" is meaningless until an OpenAI-compatible **agent CLI adapter** exists, and **Kimi CLI** is wanted as the third native subscription adapter. Both are adapter additions rather than catalog/profile work, so folding them in grows this phase; the alternative is a small dedicated phase. Whichever is chosen, **`agentKindSchema` and the static registry must widen TOGETHER** (see Phase 6's standing constraint) and the D34 Q5 "frozen registry for Phase 3" ruling must be consciously lifted rather than quietly ignored. ~~**The agent-CLI-over-OpenRouter adapter is also what gives Phase 3's BYOK milestone a vehicle (D44)** — if it lands here rather than in Phase 3, the milestone's end-to-end *proof* moves with it.~~ **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-24: the pressure is now LOWER, not higher.** D47/D49 gave Phase 3's BYOK milestone its vehicle **without** a new adapter — codex-the-binary driving a non-GPT model over OpenRouter — and the milestone is **already met and proven** (`15a016e`). These two adapters are therefore wanted for **reach**, not to unblock anything.
**Kickoff questions — ALL SETTLED 2026-07-24.** Resolutions are recorded inline below; the questions are kept because the reasoning is what the decisions rest on. **Adapters → D52 (moved to Phase 3d) · TERM → D54 (pinned) · restore option (a) → D53 (stays declined, relaunch added) · model precedence → Task 3a-4's normative table (`launch_profiles.model` > `provider_configs.model` > nothing emitted; `model_catalog` never authoritative) — **⚠ NOW SHIPPED AND NUMBERED AS D56 (2026-07-25); cite the decision, not this line** · multi-turn → Task 3a-3's gate, allowed to fail with a pre-declared re-scope branch · `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` → re-deferred, no consumer.**

**Kickoff must settle (at minimum):**

- **Do the two new PTY adapters — Kimi CLI (native, subscription) and the OpenAI-compatible agent CLI — belong IN 3a, or in a small dedicated phase?** Either answer requires **consciously lifting D34 Q5's frozen-registry ruling** and recording the lift as a numbered decision, plus widening `agentKindSchema` and the static registry **together** (Phase 6's standing constraint).
- **Rule on `TERM` (F28) — and whatever is ruled becomes a DECISION amending D33's seven-var allow-list, not a quiet code change.** 3-6 proved live that an inherited `TERM=dumb` puts codex into a fallback renderer that interleaves cursor-advance escapes **between characters** (`-  a  p  i  0  3  -  K  7 …`), leaving a secret legible on screen and invisible to exact-match scrubbing. The options are: **pin** `TERM=xterm-256color` in `composeChildEnv` for every launch; **pin per-adapter** (each `AgentAdapter` declares its terminal expectation); or **keep inheriting** and accept the render risk as a documented limit. Weigh whether `COLORTERM` must travel with it. Since `TERM` is a **non-secret eighth variable** on a council-set allow-list, the ruling belongs in §6 with a number — silently adding it to `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` would be the "quietly ignored" failure D34 Q5 warns against in the adjacent case.
- **The model precedence order**, written down: `model_catalog` (what exists) vs `provider_configs.model` (route default, v6/D48, already shipped) vs `launch_profiles.model` (this launch). One order, no second home.
- **Does 3a implement restore option (a)?** If yes, it must answer the boot-time-decryption question D33 left unsanctioned; if no, the global `credentialed_sessions` list stays and its per-project scoping debt carries forward explicitly.
- **Does the OpenRouter Responses route survive MULTI-TURN?** Prove it before `usage_records` or any profile depends on conversational continuity (F-36-1).
- **Is `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` needed after all?** 3-6 declared it **null** (absent from `claude --help`); the claude base-URL env mapping was deferred to this phase and should be either resolved or re-deferred deliberately.

- **Milestone:** a saved launch profile reproduces an agent's full configuration in one click, and per-credential spend is recorded where a producer exists.

### Phase 3b — Native Council Review — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-26** _(kicked off 2026-07-26 by `15c1b1c`; closed 2026-07-26 by `426ee81`; all four tasks landed)_ **[CR-3b.0 CLOSED as D63 · CR-3b.1 CLOSED as D67 — the deliberation & arbitration protocol, deferred here by D64(3) and fired mid-task on 2026-07-26]**

The §4 CR mechanism becomes a first-class feature (D27): a `CouncilService` in main orchestrating 3–5 API-mode members + a frontier arbiter over the Phase-3 adapter/vault infrastructure; council configuration UI (~~each member = credential profile + base URL + model id + role `member|arbiter` + params~~ — **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-07-26 (Task 3b-2, `01556a8`): each member = credential profile + model id + role `member|arbiter` + params, and `council_members` carries NO `base_url` column and NO `provider_id` column.** The struck phrasing **predates D48/D56**: a member names a route **by naming a credential**, reached through `credential_profiles.provider_id` → `provider_configs.base_url`, which is D48's one home. `launch_profiles` needs both columns only because **D33 clause 9** makes a route-with-no-credential first class; **a council member always authenticates**, so two columns able to disagree would be a bug class rather than a convenience); a council **VIEW/ROUTE** (palette: "Run council…") that takes a brief-`.md` path, streams the deliberation live, and writes a findings `.md` beside the brief; run transcripts persisted; **per-member cost read from each response's own `usage` block**; pino redaction + **G4 secret-grep mandatory**.

**⚠ ~~TWO~~ THREE SENTENCES IN THE LINE ABOVE HAVE NOW BEEN CORRECTED — two at the 2026-07-26 kickoff and a THIRD at Task 3b-2's close on 2026-07-26 — and all three had gone stale exactly the way the previous pass warned about.** The third is the member definition, struck inline above: ~~credential profile + base URL~~ → **the credential ONLY, with the route derived through it (D48/D56, `01556a8`)**. ~~a council pane/window~~ → **a VIEW/ROUTE (D64(1))**, the `SettingsProviders.vue` precedent — which is what keeps **D45(3) entirely out of this phase** (no versioned migration of the persisted layout tree, no non-OSC auto-titling, no capability divergence pushed into `LaunchDialog` or the worktree panel). ~~per-member cost via `usage_records`~~ → **`usage_records` IS A DEAD NAME (D64)**; the table was superseded by `dispatches` plus 3a-3's mint ledger, and **F35 now proves the account-scoped `attribution:summary` cannot substitute for per-call `usage` at member grain.**

**Kickoff ran 2026-07-26 (`15c1b1c`), immediately after Phase 3a closed, and settled two decisions before a line was written — D63** (CR-3b.0's ruling on the api-mode session producer, plus coordinator resolutions (a)–(g), Matthew-ratified) **and D64** (three scope rulings). The phase is decomposed into **four serial tasks** in [`Tasks/Phase-3b-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3b-Overview.md), with `Task-3b-1.md` … `Task-3b-4.md` and their four paired ImplementationSpecs authored against the verified `341ea5c` codebase.

One seam, at the consumer, exactly as D63(d) struck the second one.

Measured from each call's own usage block at $3/$15 per 1M: **$0.0073 across the four calls that reported usage.** **One aborted drive never delivered a usage frame, so its cost is genuinely unknown, bounded above only by its `max_tokens` of 1000** — therefore **the total lies between $0.0085 and $0.0226 and CANNOT be claimed under $0.02.** Five completions were submitted rather than the doc's four (one superseded by F34); **Matthew was asked before the extra spend and chose to continue on kimi-k3.** Two refusal drives and one bad-model-id drive cost $0.

What later tasks inherit:

- **⚠ THE HEADLINE OUTPUT IS A CORRECTION TO THIS ROADMAP'S OWN TEXT, NOT A FEATURE.** `council_members` carries **NO `base_url` column and NO `provider_id` column.** A member names a route **BY NAMING A CREDENTIAL**; the route is reached through `credential_profiles.provider_id` → `provider_configs.base_url`, which is **D48's one home**. Both the phase scope line and this task's own row said *"credential profile + base URL + model id + role + params"* — **phrasing that predates D48/D56** — and both are struck and corrected above. **Why no `provider_id` either:** `launch_profiles` needs both columns only because **D33 clause 9** makes a route-with-no-credential first class; **a council member ALWAYS authenticates**, so that case does not exist and two columns that can disagree would be a bug class.
- **The FK ruling SPLIT THREE WAYS INSIDE ONE MIGRATION, and D62 is amended rather than merely cited.** `council_members.credential_profile_id` → REAL `REFERENCES`, RESTRICT (a member is a live INSTRUCTION). `council_runs` → **ZERO references on every column** (a run is a historical FACT — the `dispatches`/`model_catalog` ruling). `council_messages` → **ZERO** as well, because a transcript stays true after its member is deleted and a FK there would make deleting **one** member throw for **every** run it ever joined. Verified by `PRAGMA foreign_key_list` — **1 / 0 / 0** — with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1` re-confirmed (F16). **Because SQLite will not cascade a soft pointer, `deleteCouncilRun(id)` purges its own `council_messages` in one transaction** — the `deleteProviderConfig` → `model_catalog` precedent.
- **`credential:delete` gained ONE ADDED COUNT, not a second guard.** 3a-5's `launch_profiles` count was neither replaced nor duplicated: the refusal now reads `countLaunchProfilesForCredential(id) + countCouncilMembersForCredential(id)`, **both named distinctly in the message**, so it tells the user what to remove rather than only that something blocks.
- **D56's order has exactly ONE expression in the codebase, and the wire is built so it cannot be collapsed by accident.** `resolveMemberModel` is the only place the precedence is written. The wire carries **`model` AND `resolvedModel` as two separate fields on purpose**, so *"NULL, inheriting"* and *"explicitly set to the route default"* stay distinguishable — collapsing them is how a **back-write into rank 1** gets authored by someone reading the UI. The Settings model input is **free text with an additive `<datalist>`** — D56's **third enforcement site**, never a closed `<select>`.
- **The wire carries ids and labels only.** `councilMemberWireSchema` is `.strict()` and carries **no base URL, no provider id, no key, no fingerprint and no `params_json`** — params are **write-only inbound**, settable at create and never echoed back.
- **The management refusal exists at BOTH ends, at two named call sites** — `validateMemberShape` (create) and `resolveCouncilMember` (resolve). **One without the other is precisely the 3a-5 defect that D62 records.**
- **⚠ DRIVE 4 WAS DRIVEN ISOLATED, AND THE ISOLATION IS QUOTED RATHER THAN CLAIMED.** `launch_profiles` held **ZERO rows**, so only the new council-member count could produce the refusal — 3a-5's own review records an earlier run that **proved nothing** because the older guard fired first. **Zero `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` in any log.**
- **⚠ THE COORDINATOR DECLINED THE DESTRUCTIVE HALF OF DRIVE 4 AS SPECIFIED — AND THIS IS A STANDING LESSON FOR EVERY FUTURE TASK DOC.** `Task-3b-2.md` drive 4 asked to delete the `OR milestone key` credential and *"re-create both"*. **Re-creating it requires re-entering the real key — a HUMAN step the session is forbidden to perform** (standing condition: never ask for, read, or accept key text). So the **refusal half** ran against `OR milestone key` (non-destructive) and the **delete-proceeds half** ran against a throwaway credential created and destroyed inside the same drive. **A drive that asks an agent to destroy a credential fixture cannot be discharged by the agent, and should be written as two halves from the start.**
- **The migration proof, rehearsed BEFORE the first real boot.** The rehearsal ran on a **copy of the real DB with `Local State` copied beside it (F31)** — **25/25 assertions** — before v11 touched the real file. The full three-dump protocol then passed **27/27** on the real DB, with `dispatches` and `attention_spans` **exempted IN ADVANCE inside the assert script itself** as append-only live telemetry (**0 and 1 row appended, 0 moved**) — 3a-5 declared that exemption in prose; this task declared it in code. **Six live drives, all free.**
- **Cost: $0.00 against a $0.00 envelope — this task made NO API call at all**, the first task in Phase 3b to spend nothing.
- **⚠ Owed at Task 3b-3, and it is now four items rather than one.** **(1)** `council_runs` and `council_messages` are **EMPTY and 3b-3 is their FIRST WRITER** — `council_runs` already carries the shape to fill (`project_id`, `brief_path` NOT NULL, `findings_path`, `status`, `started_at` NOT NULL / `ended_at`, **D64(2)'s mint quartet** `minted_key_hash` / `minted_key_limit` / `minted_at` / `revoked_at`, and `tokens_in` / `tokens_out` / `tokens_cached` / `cost_usd`). `deleteCouncilRun` exists and purges messages transactionally, but **nothing calls it yet and there is still NO retention policy.** **(2)** The management refusal must fire a **THIRD** time, in **3b-3's run-assembly path**, not just at create and resolve. **(3)** `params_json` is **write-only inbound** — settable at create, never echoed on the wire, therefore **not editable after creation**; 3b-3 decides which parameters it actually sends (`ImplementationSpec-3b-2.md` §8 left this open deliberately). **(4)** The **`api:probe` adopt-or-delete decision is STILL OWED**, unchanged from 3b-1 — and `IpcChannel` is now at **49** keys, which sharpens it.

- **⚠ THE MOST IMPORTANT CORRECTION IS THAT THE COUNCIL'S OWN PSEUDOCODE WOULD HAVE DESTROYED A PAID-FOR RUN.** It aborted the deliberation when a string check found the arbiter had elided a dissent — after every member had answered and been billed — on a check the same findings' risk R7 admits is fragile in exactly the false-negative direction that triggers it, and which its own code-enforces item 9 made redundant by appending the dissent section unconditionally. **The shipped form appends unconditionally and records the elision as an observation.** Dissent preservation no longer depends on a string comparison being right.

- **D66 is DISCHARGED AND PROVEN WITH A REAL KEY.** A run was tree-killed mid-deliberation, leaving a live funded $1 key and an open ledger row; the next cold boot logged the heal **then** the reconcile, recognised the key by its own `chorus-council-` prefix, read `spend 0.000127155` **before** revoking, wrote `revoked_at`, and left all four of the account's real keys untouched (`key census: 5 live · 1 ours · 4 not ours`). Every pre-existing reconcile assertion is byte-identical.
- **D60/D63 Q2 proven STRUCTURALLY, in its strongest form.** `sessions` stayed **20 across six real council runs**; `councilService.ts` and `councilCore.ts` contain **zero** references to `SessionManager` or `sessions`; `council.start` has exactly one caller, an `ipcMain.handle`. Not *"restore refuses to relaunch a council member"* but *"there is nothing for restore to find, and no unattended path can even ask."* **⚠ Recorded as a gap rather than claimed: the vault logs only decrypt FAILURES**, so the class-discriminated decrypt log D60 would ideally cite does not exist and the evidence here is structural.
- **`api:probe` is DELETED**, discharging the item owed since 3b-1. No test referenced its schemas, so it cost no coverage; the transport now has a real consumer with real tests and live runs, which is strictly better proof than a probe no interface can reach.
- **⚠ TWO DEFECTS WERE FOUND BY THE DRIVES AND NOT BY REVIEW, and both are recorded because that is the point of G2.** **(1)** The first run reported `membersAnswered: 8` against `membersPlanned: 4` — it counted turns and called them members, which is not a denominator but a riddle; members and turns are now counted separately. **(2)** A run was rate-limited by the provider mid-deliberation and became a genuine PARTIAL RUN by accident, exercising the refusal floor, the partial-run header and dissent extraction live — better evidence than the test written for it. **It also confirmed the reviewing council's own predicted weakness of 5C:** mechanical extraction is noisy, and one talkative member produced six of nine dissent entries. Left for 3b-4 rather than tuned away.
- **Cost: $0.001306 measured across SIX real runs, against a `< $0.25` envelope**, with **zero** runs of unknown cost and **zero** open ledger rows at the end. The models were chosen deliberately against F34 — four cheap NON-reasoning instruction-followers rather than the route's default `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, which live pricing on the day put at **$3/M in, $15/M out** and which F34 already showed can bill an entire cap for an empty answer.

- **D68(2)–(4), each discharged as ruled.** `assembleFindingsDocument` gained a **provenance table** — every member, its model, what it actually did, the run id, the start time — and an **unconditional standing caveat placed ABOVE the synthesis**, with run id and start time arriving as **PARAMETERS on `CouncilState`** because the core has no clock. `App.vue`'s `activeView` widened `'workspace' | 'settings'` → **`+ 'council'`**, with **exactly 1 new `.vue` file**. And `brief_text` is **REMOVED from `councilStartRequestSchema`, not deprecated** — `ipc.test.ts` asserts that a request still carrying it **FAILS to parse**, which is the only form of removal a later reader cannot mistake for an optional field.
- **The two rulings `ImplementationSpec-3b-4.md` §6 deliberately left open are SETTLED as D69**, and both went the way that costs the user less. An existing findings file is **NEVER overwritten** — it is suffixed (`-Findings-2.md`, `-3.md`) — **proven live**: a re-run on the stub brief wrote `StubBrief-Findings-2.md` and left the original's byte length unchanged. A **sanitization hit REFUSES the run** rather than redacting it.
- **The brief-path file boundary is main-side, ORDERED, and echoes NOTHING.** `councilService.ts` gained `validateBriefPath`, `findingsPathFor`, `nextFreeFindingsPath`, `scanBriefForSecrets`, `describeSecretHits` and `MAX_BRIEF_BYTES = 200_000` — **the same number `council:start` already enforced as a string length in 3b-3**, kept deliberately so the bound did not move when the input changed from text to a path (the largest real brief in the repo is **36 KB**). The refusals run in order: not absolute → null byte → UNC → not `.md` → normalize → **re-check** → `statSync().isFile()` → size cap. **No refusal echoes any path at all**, because a *resolved* relative path would leak main's cwd. The new `council:pick-brief` channel is a main-side `dialog.showOpenDialog` with an `.md` filter and a structured cancel (the `project:add` precedent) and **is explicitly NOT the boundary** — it sits in front of it. `councilStartResponseSchema` gained `findings_path` and `findings_error`, **BOTH required and nullable**, so an "ok" response can never be silent about where the file went.
- **⚠ THE DEFECT THE DRIVE FOUND, AND NO TEST WOULD HAVE.** The first live run rendered **291 message fragments where 8 turns belonged.** A blind round asks every member **CONCURRENTLY**, so their deltas interleave — and the store matched each incoming delta only against the **MOST RECENT** block, opening a new block on every switch between members. Fixed to key on **(member, phase, round)**; the re-run rendered **7**. A regression test was added. **This is a class that only exists when several models stream at once, which is precisely the shape the whole protocol is built around — and it binds the native chat pane D45 commits to.** See **F37** (§5).
- **The four G2 drives ran on the REAL dev DB** (projects `985d547b…` / `f47ac10b…`), with vault decryptability proven **FREE via `model:refresh` before any spend** and **"Test key" never pressed**. **Drive 1** drove the whole refusal table **LIVE through `window.chorus.startCouncilRun`**, not merely in vitest: relative, null byte, UNC, not-`.md`, non-existent, **a DIRECTORY named `.md`**, traversal — each refused **by rule**. **Drive 2** caught all six known secret shapes with **pattern name + 1-based line, value never echoed**, and passed the four false-positive fixtures clean (40-char SHA, Windows path, UUID, `chorus/<repo>/<8hex>` branch). **Drive 3** ran the full UI path — Ctrl+K → `Run council…` → view → **the real native `#32770` dialog, driven by SendKeys and returning a real path** → streaming → findings beside the brief ($0.000220137). The palette gained `council.run` / `Run council…`, **disabled without an active project**, because a run is recorded against `council_runs.project_id`.
- **⚠ DRIVE 4 IS THE DOGFOOD RUN, AND IT WAS POINTED AT `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` DELIBERATELY** so that the comparison against the externally-produced `CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md` is **SAME-BRIEF**, not same-topic. Run id `c17c8c29-67c7-4b44-a207-a2141148f3e4`. Findings landed beside the brief carrying provenance, the standing caveat, the detection table and **9 preserved dissents**; the transcript persisted **r0–r3 with per-turn tokens**; the key was minted **20:24:19**, **read back THEN revoked 20:25:30**, cap **$1**, **ZERO open ledger rows**. **$0.001175081.**
- **⚠ THE HONEST COMPARISON — SAME BRIEF, EXTERNAL vs NATIVE — AND THE NATIVE RUN IS THE WEAKER DOCUMENT.** External `CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md`: **20,900 bytes, 11 sections.** Native: **7,316 bytes, 5 sections.** **(1) THE SYNTHESIS WAS TRUNCATED MID-SENTENCE** — it stops at *"Q1: B — The best answer is option B:"* — because **four of eight turns returned EXACTLY 700 output tokens**, the 3b-3 fixtures' `max_tokens`; the document never reached per-question rulings, risks or action items. **(2) No verdict, no confidence, no severity-ranked issues**, where the external run carries **PASS / 8-of-10 / three severity-ranked issues**, each with a recommended fix and a ruling. **(3) ⚠ VERDICT-TOKEN COMPLIANCE COLLAPSED ON A REAL BRIEF — 4 of 6 questions fell to `model-judged` (not measured)**, only Q2 and Q3 structurally measured, against near-total compliance on the short stub brief. **That is the FIRST EVIDENCE against one of D67's three explicitly "not checkable" predictions, and the evidence is bad.** **(4) The talkative-member skew reproduced EXACTLY as 3b-3 predicted** — one member (CR Beta) produced **6 of the 9** dissent entries. **(5) Two "dissents" are noise** — text mechanically extracted from a `DISAGREE:` label that in fact says nothing challenges the synthesis; **the matcher cannot tell a non-objection from an objection.** **Where native is STRONGER, all three are structural rather than qualitative:** provenance is machine-generated and exact; the dissent section is core-generated and the arbiter cannot drop it; the detection path is labelled per question — at **$0.0012 against frontier models**. **The truncation is the 3b-3 fixtures' 700-token cap, not a defect in 3b-4's design** — but the honest read is that **the pipeline works and *these models at this cap* cannot produce a document of the external run's completeness.** Recorded as **F38** (§5).
- **The dogfood findings file was REMOVED from the repo and preserved as drive evidence under `_verify/3b-4/`.** A truncated, partly-garbled model document named `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer-Findings.md` sitting beside the authoritative `CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md`, for a CR **already closed as D63**, is exactly what the standing caveat exists to prevent. **The removal is the caveat being obeyed rather than quoted.**
- **Cost: $0.001901248 across FOUR real runs, against a `< $0.30` envelope.** `council_runs` 6 → **10**, `council_messages` 32 → **62**, `council_members` **4 (unchanged)**. **`sessions` 20 → 20 and `dispatches` 87 → 87** — no session row, no dispatch write; **D63 Q2 holding structurally for the third consecutive task.** **One honest overshoot, recorded rather than quietly absorbed:** a boundary-table case labelled *"must reach assembly"* went **PAST** assembly and ran a full council ($0.00027062), writing outside the repo into the session scratchpad.
- **⚠ `deleteCouncilRun` STILL HAS NO CALLER — AND NEITHER DOES `getCouncilMessagesForRun`.** The view renders from the **live broadcast and the returned text**, not from the DB, so **3b-2's prediction that the view might become its first caller did NOT come true.** The transcript store is therefore **write-only**: no reader, no retention policy. A later phase must give it one or justify the absence.
- **The boundary claims are checkable because each gate lands at exactly one:** **exactly ONE findings-write call site** in `councilService.ts` · **zero `fetch(` and zero `fs`** in any renderer file · **zero new secret-pattern literals** (`secret-patterns.json` imported) · **zero `createScrubber` outside `sessionOutput.ts`**. New files: `src/renderer/src/views/CouncilView.vue`, `src/renderer/src/stores/council.ts` (+ its test), `src/main/services/councilService.test.ts`.
- **⚠ THE MILESTONE IS MET, AND IT IS EXPLICITLY NOT AT PARITY.** Point the council at a brief `.md`; get a findings `.md` from live multi-model deliberation with keys never leaving the vault — **driven end-to-end through the real UI, on a real Chorus governance brief, for $0.0012.** The dogfood check passes as a **mechanism** proof. **It does not pass as a quality claim:** on the same brief the native run produced a third of the document, lost four of six structural verdicts, and truncated its own synthesis mid-sentence. **The honest statement of what shipped is "a working council whose output does not yet match what the external one produced" — and F38 exists so that sentence cannot be quietly dropped later.**

**Task 3b-3's kickoff prompt was authored 2026-07-26** (`Task-3b-3-ExecutionPrompt.md`, committed with 3b-2's in `a53ba96`) **against the code at `01556a8` rather than against the task docs — and that is what produced D66.** Four things the task docs do not say came out of the pass; three needed no decision and are carried in the prompt as stop-and-read sections, one did:

- **⚠ D66 — the boot reconcile is blind to a council run in BOTH directions**, and both do-nothing options are wrong in opposite ways. Resolved above as a chore commit at the head of the task, because the fix crosses 3b-3's file-ownership boundary into `dispatchAttribution.ts` / `attributionCore.ts` and **a session should not widen its own remit mid-task, nor silently skip a requirement its own task doc states.**
- **The live drive cannot run as `Task-3b-3.md` writes it: `council_members` is EMPTY** (0 rows, `_verify/3b-2/final-restored.json`) — 3b-2's drives created members and cleaned them up — and the dev vault holds **exactly one usable inference credential** (`OR milestone key`). So the members are created by the drive and **differ by MODEL, not by credential**, which puts **F34** directly in the path: picking those models carelessly is the most likely way the phase's largest envelope is blown.
- **The minted key and the member's own credential are BOTH in play, and the docs do not reconcile them.** `ImplementationSpec-3b-3.md` §3(3) says every member session uses the run's minted key; §4 still opens with `resolveCredential`. Both are required — the latter for its five ordered refusals, the management refusal and the route; the former for the bounded spend surface — which means **a member's own key is decrypted and then never sent**, and that must be narrated rather than left for a later reviewer to reverse-engineer. Its unstated corollary: **the minted key authenticates only against OpenRouter**, so a member on any other route cannot use it and the run is refused **by label** at assembly (never a silent drop).
- **The task doc's open question about whether `usage` is obtainable on a streamed response is ALREADY ANSWERED** — `_verify/3b-1/D4-VERIFICATION.md` obligation 2: **yes, and no request flag is needed** (`stream_options.include_usage` and `usage.include` are documented as deprecated and inert). **D55 still binds on the absent case:** a member whose stream ended without a usage frame reports **null, never 0**.

- **⚠ BINDING CONSTRAINT from D45(2): the council must be an ORCHESTRATOR OVER `ApiSessionHandle`, never a parallel implementation.** This phase is the first consumer of api-mode sessions, so whatever it builds becomes the de-facto api-mode machinery for the whole app — including the native chat pane D45 commits to. `ApiSessionHandle` (`send` / `receive(): AsyncIterable<string>` / `dispose`) is already declared in `types.ts` and is the single primitive; building a council-shaped API client beside it would fork the mechanism, because the blind-round batch shape and the interactive-chat shape are not the same. **Also binding here: D45(1)** — api-mode text arrives via `receive()`, not the PTY `onData` where 3-5's scrubber lives, so the ingest-scrub seam must be unified *before or with* this phase or api sessions ship unredacted (the F26 failure shape). **⚠ AMENDED 2026-07-26: the D45(1) half of this bullet is DISCHARGED by Task 3b-1** — proven live over 19 streamed chunks, above — **and the D45(2) half stands unchanged and unweakened for Task 3b-3.**
- **⚠ BINDING ON THE COUNCIL BRIEF (F27): quote the Claude Code auth prompt VERBATIM wherever this phase words a scrub or exfiltration guarantee.** Claude Code 2.1.218 prints "Detected a custom API key in your environment — ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: sk-ant-...<tail> — Do you want to use this API key?", disclosing a **masked ≥8-char fragment** of an injected key to screen and ring buffer. Exact-value scrubbing **cannot match a derived form by construction**, so **the agent's own auth UX is a fragment-leak channel** — the sharpened form of D33's council risk 1. **This phase must never word a guarantee as "agents cannot echo the key."** The honest wording is: *Chorus redacts registered exact values on ingest; it cannot redact values an agent derives.* Council members reasoning about api-mode redaction must be given that limit up front, or their findings will over-promise on a false premise.
- **Protocol sketch (Task 3b-3's kickoff to refine):** independent blind positions → cross-critique round → disagreement detection → arbiter ruling → synthesized findings with dissents preserved.
- **[CR] question — DEFERRED, NOT WAIVED (D64(3)):** the deliberation protocol shape — blinding, round count, disagreement detection, arbitration trigger. **The trigger fires at Task 3b-3's kickoff, once a protocol design exists to review**; briefing a council on a blank page spends the pass for nothing. §4's obligation stands until it fires, and **this is deliberately NOT the D49 waiver.** Fittingly, the external council's last job before retiring. **Its findings are recorded as D67** — the brief is `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.1-DeliberationProtocol.md`, the findings `CouncilBrief-3b.1-Findings.md`, and **D66 took the next free number first**, so the numbering is fixed in advance rather than raced for mid-session.
- **Milestone:** point the council at a brief `.md`; get a findings `.md` from live multi-model deliberation, keys never leaving the vault. Dogfood check: a real Chorus governance CR runs natively end-to-end. **⚠ And the output is model deliberation, NOT verified fact.** CR-3b.0 is the live example and the lesson: its rulings were sound and its verbatim TypeScript carried four errors, because it had the brief and not the repo. **Nothing in this phase may present findings as verified**, and the view must not imply it — §4's coordinator-verification step is not replaced by this feature; it is the reason the feature is safe to build.

### Phase 3c — Design Adoption — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-28** _(kicked off 2026-07-26 by D38; decomposed against the verified codebase at `1cf23ff`; closed by `c4f82fb`, all five tasks landed)_

**Kickoff settled five decisions (D72–D76) and decomposed the phase into five serial tasks** in
[`Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md), with `Task-3c-1.md` … `Task-3c-5.md`
and their five paired ImplementationSpecs. **Dependency chain: 3c-1 → 3c-2 → 3c-3 → 3c-4 → 3c-5.**
**Five further decisions (D77–D82) were settled DURING the phase**, every one of them found by
authoring a task's execution prompt against the code rather than against its task doc — the same
pass that produced D66 and D68 in Phase 3b.

| Task | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **3c-1** | Faithful `@theme` token extraction (D73), `@fontsource` fonts (D75), the four colorblind-safe `StateMarker` shapes, `chorusPulse` + reduced-motion. Restyles nothing. | ✅ **landed `b8f2b1e`** (+ `00fed15` docs), 2026-07-27 |
| **3c-2** | `frame: false` + the custom titlebar (D74) — the phase's **only** main-process change and **only** IPC change. | ✅ **landed `fbb6d2b`**, 2026-07-27 — all twelve behaviour boxes driven on the real window |
| **3c-3** | The workspace: 208px project rail **replacing** `ProjectTabs.vue`, filmstrip right rail, pane header, 30px status bar. | ✅ **landed `0476e54`**, 2026-07-27 (+ `98191ec`, a **declared behavioural follow-up** — see below) |
| **3c-4** | Overlays: `LaunchDialog`, `CommandPalette`, `EmptyState` + `WorktreePanel` (the last two **unmocked — conformance only**). | ✅ **landed `070f381`**, 2026-07-27 — extracted `assets/overlays.css`; **took `#1e1e1e` to 0** |
| **3c-5** | Settings + Council. Closes the phase. | ✅ **landed `c4f82fb`**, 2026-07-28 — new `assets/settings.css`; **192 → 0** stock-palette hits in `views/` |

**Baseline at close (`c4f82fb`), all re-run:** typecheck **0** · vitest **1007/1007 across 30
files** · `grep:secrets` **clean** · `IpcChannel` **57** · `ipcMain.handle(` **52 / 0** ·
`sqliteTable(` **16** · `MIGRATIONS.length` **12**.

**⚠ THOSE NUMBERS ARE NOT THE ONES THE PHASE WAS SCOPED AGAINST, AND THE REASON IS THE PHASE'S
LAST LESSON: PHASE 3d LANDED IN THE MIDDLE OF IT.** Tasks 3d-1 … 3d-4 shipped **between 3c-4 and
3c-5**, out of order at Matthew's request because they blocked his daily use of the app. The
kickoff froze `IpcChannel` **56** · `handle` **51** · `sqliteTable` **15** · `MIGRATIONS` **11** ·
vitest **941/29**; every one of those was correct when written and stale by the time 3c-5 ran.
**The purity contract itself held — 3c-5 moved nothing** (its diff is four `views/` files plus one
new stylesheet, with zero `stores/`, `main/`, `shared/`, `components/` or `palette/`). **The
lesson is that a frozen number is only frozen relative to a phase that runs uninterrupted**, and a
prompt authored against the code catches this while a prompt authored against the task docs does
not. Full correction table in [`Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md).

**⚠ THE MILESTONE IS MET WITH ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION AND FOUR UNMOCKED REGIONS, AND BOTH ARE
STATED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT IN A TASK REPORT** — "visually indistinguishable" is the milestone's
own wording, so a reader deserves to know where it is knowingly false.

- **The deviation: the settings mock draws MASKED KEY PREVIEWS** (`sk-ant-…Xq4F`, `sk-proj-…9dKm`,
  `sk-proj-…T2wa`, `sk-or-…v81A`) and **Chorus does not draw them.** **D33 clause 3 admits no
  exception**; even four characters narrow the search space for anyone who sees a screenshot or a
  screen share. The refusal is **structural, not incidental**: `settings.css` has **no
  `.set-row-hint` class**, so there is nowhere to put one. **A mock is the authority on appearance
  and is never an authority on what the app may disclose** — the same precedence
  `ImplementationSpec-3c-5.md` §3 sets for verification chrome in the council view. Two smaller
  D76 omissions in the same file: the mock's **six** nav entries (one is live) and its
  **`neo4j :7688`** chip (Phase 6; no source).
- **The unmocked count is FOUR REGIONS ACROSS THREE FILES, not the two surfaces D83 left it at.**
  `WorktreePanel.vue` and `EmptyState.vue` (declared), plus **two found by authoring 3c-5's prompt
  against the code**: the **council-member surface** (3b-2 — `grep -ci "council"` over the mock
  returns **0**) and the **model shortlist section** (3d/D85 — `grep -ciE "shortlist|favourite|
  star|pin"` returns **0**). Both post-date the mock. **`SettingsProviders.vue` is 1,334 lines and
  the mock describes roughly half of it**; any later claim that this file "matches the mock" is
  false without that qualification.

**⚠ D76 BOUND HARDER IN 3c-5 THAN ANYWHERE ELSE IN THE PHASE, AND ONE OMISSION IS A REASONED
EXCEPTION TO D55.** The council mock's phase header draws `elapsed 4:38`, `est. remaining ~9m`,
`round 1 **of 2**` and `$0.31 so far`; **none has a source** — no run start time in the store
(adding one is store logic the task may not touch), an estimate is the dishonest number the
five-stop track exists to refuse, the renderer is never told how many rounds are planned, and
`costUsd` arrives only with the end-of-run accounting. **What ships is the round ordinal alone.**
⚠ That is a **deliberate exception to D55's "no number without its denominator"**: an ordinal
position is not a measured quantity out of a total, and inventing "of 2" to satisfy D55's letter
would breach D76. Recorded so the next reader does not "fix" it.

**Two defects the RUNNING APP exposed that reading the code did not** — both found in the G2 pass,
both fixed in the task commit. **A route whose credential had never been verified rendered "0 of 1
verified" in the same healthy green as "1 of 1"** — D55 inverted: the number was right and the
*rendering* of it lied; zero-verified now takes the neutral tone. And **a two-word provider name
wrapped and pushed the status chip onto a second line.**

**⚠ THE COUNCIL BEHAVIOUR RE-CHECK WAS NOT PERFORMED, AND IT IS RECORDED AS UNPROVEN RATHER THAN
REASONED AWAY.** `Task-3c-5.md` asks for a real run streaming into the restyled view.
**`council_members` is EMPTY — zero rows**; the D71 frontier roster is gone (the DB was rebuilt
2026-07-27, though the three OpenRouter credential profiles survive and verify). Rebuilding that
roster belongs to **Phase 3e**, which has the authorised budget and the measurement questions that
need it; doing it here would spend frontier money on the wrong question. So **streaming,
Esc-during-run, and the findings-file write are UNPROVEN by this phase.** They are unchanged code
paths — `stores/council.ts`, `councilCore.ts`, `councilService.ts` all have an empty diff — but
**"unchanged code" is an argument, not evidence, and this phase's whole rule is that the two are
different.** 3e's first real run closes it.

**G2 covered 13 of 14 surfaces fully; surface 4 (a pane in each of four states) is PARTIAL** —
`running` observed on three live panes, `exited`/`error` **not induced because that means killing
sessions Matthew has real work in**, and `needs-you` still has **no data source** (D78, owed by
Phase 4). **The six council states were driven SYNTHETICALLY** by writing the renderer store over
CDP in the exact shape main's broadcast produces — **`$0.00`, no IPC, no API call, no database
write, reverted by a reload** — which is *better* evidence than a real run for a styling task
(`refused` and `error` are states a live run may not produce on demand), and is stated plainly so
nobody later mistakes them for photographs of a live deliberation.

**⚠ The phase now has TWO intentional behavioural changes, not one.** `frame: false` (D74) was
declared at kickoff. The second, `98191ec`, was landed at Matthew's explicit request **after**
3c-3's task commit and deliberately kept out of it: 3c-3 put two session **counts** on screen for
the first time (the rail's and the status bar's) and nothing told them a pane had closed, so the
counts went stale until a project switch or restart. The fix is a refresh cadence — a
`chorus:session-closed` window event and a re-read from main — which is behaviour, not styling.
**It is a defect this phase created by displaying facts that were previously undisplayed**, and it
sits in its own commit precisely so the "no behavioural change" claim over the task commit stays
literally true. No channel, no handler, no store logic, no test edited.

**Kickoff decisions.** **D72 — RAISED AND DISCHARGED THE SAME DAY.** The council view had no mock,
so the ruling was **design it, do not defer it**: a detailed prompt was authored
([`docs/design/CouncilView-DesignPrompt.md`](../../design/CouncilView-DesignPrompt.md)), Matthew
produced the design in Claude Design, and delivered `docs/design/v2/Chorus Council.dc.html`
(**69,011 B**, all six states). **Coordinator-reviewed at delivery: all five invariants pass** —
the F27 redaction wording and the standing caveat both **verbatim**, the caveat above the
synthesis, unavailable members shown *and* explained, every accounting figure carrying its
denominator, and **zero** verification chrome. **⚠ AND IT EXCEEDS THE SHIPPED VIEW IN TWO PLACES
THAT ARE NOT COSMETIC:** refused turns render as transcript **rows rather than gaps**, and the
cost line states ***"true total is at least this"*** — **F39's under-reporting made visible in the
UI**, which the shipped view does not say. Both are adopted into 3c-5's scope. **⚠ The whole
`v2/` folder is now the cited authority and it is NOT a fork: all seven pre-existing screens are
BYTE-IDENTICAL to their originals** (verified by `cmp`), so v2 adds one mock and changes nothing
else — citing root for six files and v2 for one would be a two-homes hazard for no benefit. **D73** — **faithful** extraction of all ~45 hex values,
near-duplicates included, so the screenshot diff stays literal; collapsing them later is a design
change, not a refactor. **D74** — `frame: false` with **fully custom** window controls. **D75** —
**`@fontsource` packages**, which are **two dependencies not in `CLAUDE.md`'s locked stack and were
explicitly approved by Matthew at kickoff**; the CDN link must be gone, proven by an offline cold
boot. **D76 (coordinator)** — **the mocks draw data that does not exist** (per-project cost, a
daily rollup, a Neo4j chip): **render what the data supports, omit the rest, and never render a
placeholder or a zero** — the pixel-level form of the D55 rule that a number without its
denominator is worse than no number.

**Decisions settled DURING the phase (D77–D82), each found by authoring a task's execution prompt
against the code.** Full text in [`Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md);
recorded here at roadmap level because three of them change what later phases owe.

| # | Ruling | Consequence beyond its own task |
|---|---|---|
| **D77** | **No component-test harness** — the repo has none (`vitest` is `environment: 'node'`, no `@vue/test-utils`, no jsdom), and building one for a single component would need two off-stack dependencies plus a changed test environment. Shape-distinctness is proven on the **running app via CDP grayscale screenshot** instead. | 3c-1 mounts `StateMarker` **nowhere**, so the runtime proof was **owed by 3c-3** — ✅ **performed and passed** for the three states that exist. `vitest.config.ts` stays byte-identical through the phase. |
| **D78** | **The renderer can derive exactly THREE session states, not four.** `sessionStatusSchema` is `running \| exited`; the `attention:*` machinery is write-only outbound and measures the **human's** attention, not the agent's. Nothing reads the PTY stream for an agent prompt. | **No attention badge, no "waiting" tally, no pulsing card in 3c-3.** `chorusPulse` ships with **no first caller**. **`docs/design/v2/Chorus Needs Attention.html` becomes PHASE 4's normative spec** — it specifies a *capability*, not a skin. The fourth marker geometry is **UNPROVEN at runtime and owed by Phase 4**. |
| **D79** | The `needs-you` marker is **8px canonical** (`Chorus Needs Attention.html`), not the 7px 3c-1 read from the Workspace mock; the mock's 6px rail badge stays an intentional density variant. | Source-only edit in 3c-3 with **no visible effect this phase** — it hands Phase 4 the right value. |
| **D80** | **`project:list` gains `sessionCount`**, computed in main by one `GROUP BY project_id`. A **declared exception to the purity contract's "no channel is RESHAPED" clause**, admitted on D74's terms — before the task, bounded to one field on one response. | **No channel or handler added** (56 / 51 hold). Forced edits to **two** pre-existing tests, both IPC-fixture-only: `ipc.test.ts` (predicted) and `palette/commands.test.ts` (**not** predicted — it stopped compiling). **No other task in this phase may reshape a payload.** |
| **D81** | **`LaunchDialog` has NO model input.** `ImplementationSpec-3c-4.md` §3/§6.3 require verifying it is "an `<input>` with a `<datalist>`, not a `<select>`" — there is no such element; 3a-4 moved model resolution into main and the dialog merely **displays** `resolvedModel`. | **The check is struck and must not be ticked. ⚠ The hazard inverts the spec's intent:** an implementer who "restores" a model input creates exactly the second home for "which model" that **D48** forbids. **3c-4 adds no model input.** |
| **D82** | **The 3c-4 spec's shared overlay anatomy contradicts BOTH mocks.** §1 names `--color-surface-card` / `--color-border-inset` / `--radius-card`; the Workspace and Launch Dialog mocks **agree** on `#10141A` / `#262D35` / `8px` — which 3c-1 already tokenised as `--color-surface-overlay` / `--color-border-badge` / `--radius-overlay`. | **D73 applies: the mock wins.** Because §1 is the *extract-once* shared shell, the error would have propagated into all three mocked overlays at once. The scrim base `#050608` has **no token** and its two alphas (`.62` palette, `.55` dialog) are reproduced **as drawn, not unified**. |
| **D83** | **`Chorus Startup.dc.html` is an animated 2.75 s LAUNCH SPLASH over the workspace — not an empty state**, and `grep -rniE "splash\|startup"` across `src/main/` and `src/renderer/src/` returns **nothing**: the app has no splash. `EmptyState.vue` (App.vue `:434`, rendered when `layout.tree` is null) is a different surface, and **no mock draws it**. | **⚠ AMENDS THE MILESTONE: there are TWO unmocked surfaces, not one.** `EmptyState.vue` joins `WorktreePanel.vue` at token-and-primitive conformance. **The splash is a NEW FEATURE and is not built in 3c** — its boot line *"waking 7 voices · restoring 3 sessions"* would need live restore progress the renderer is never told, which is **D76** again. Unbuilt and unscheduled, recorded so it is not silently dropped. **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27 BY `2ace8bb` — THE SPLASH WAS BUILT, AND THIS CLAUSE IS NO LONGER TRUE.** `components/StartupSplash.vue` + `boot/bootInfo.ts` ship. **It was not built in defiance of this ruling; it was built by REMOVING THE RULING'S PREMISE.** D83's objection was **D76** — the boot line needs restore progress the renderer is never told — and `bootInfo.ts` gives it a source: main **stamps the write-once boot constants onto the renderer URL** before the window exists, parses them as **untrusted input** (clamped, length-capped, rendered as text), and enforces D76 **in the parser rather than the template**, so an absent, malformed or zero value resolves to the same *"nothing to say"* state and the splash **cannot render a placeholder or a zero even if main sends a broken one**. **The D76 lesson survives intact and is the durable half of this row: the answer to "the mock draws data that does not exist" is omit it OR GIVE IT A SOURCE — never fake it.** See Phase 3d. |

**⚠ Three things the kickoff found that the roadmap's own text did not say:**

- **The phase's purity contract needed an exception before it was written down.** "No IPC change"
  is impossible with `frame: false` — a frameless window's buttons **cannot** minimize, maximize
  or close without asking main. **3c-2 adds exactly four channels** (`window:minimize`,
  `window:toggle-maximize`, `window:close`, and a `window:maximized-changed` event **required**
  because the state also changes via double-click and `Win+↑`), taking `IpcChannel` **52 → 56**
  and `ipcMain.handle(` **48 → 51**. Every other task in the phase holds at those numbers.
- **⚠ THERE ARE NO COMPONENT TESTS IN THIS REPO — ZERO.** All 6 renderer test files are
  stores/logic. **No visual claim in this phase can be discharged by vitest**, which makes the
  F15-in-reverse warning literal: verification is a **per-surface CDP screenshot pass over a
  named 14-surface inventory**, re-run in full at 3c-5 after the last change lands.
- **`Chorus Micro Surfaces.dc.html` is the mission-control + push-to-talk mock — it is PHASE 5's,
  not 3c's**, and `Chorus Attention Inbox.dc.html` is Phase 4's. Of the seven files in
  `docs/design/`, **four are 3c's** (Workspace, Launch Dialog, Settings Providers, Startup), one
  is an index, and **no CSS custom property exists in any of them** — every value is an inline
  literal, so "extract tokens" had no existing naming to inherit.

**Milestone — ⚠ AMENDED AT KICKOFF, because "every surface that exists" now includes surfaces with
no mock.** Mocked surfaces (Workspace and its parts, Launch Dialog, Settings, Startup, **and
Council once D72's mock lands**) are held to **visually indistinguishable, screenshot-diffed**,
which D73 makes literal. **`WorktreePanel.vue` has no mock and no plan for one** and is held to
**token-and-primitive conformance only** — explicitly not redesigned, and **recorded as the
phase's one known visual gap** rather than quietly satisfied by a screenshot of something nobody
drew. No behavioral change, with `frame: false` as the single declared exception.
