#### Original scope note _(D38, retained)_

The `docs/design/` mockups become the app: design tokens extracted into the Tailwind v4 theme (the five-surface ladder, jade accent, state colors); **Archivo + JetBrains Mono bundled locally**; the frameless custom titlebar (`BrowserWindow` `frame:false` + drag regions — a main-process change); the left project rail replacing `ProjectTabs`; the filmstrip as the right rail; the bottom status bar; the colorblind-safe state components (dot/diamond/triangle/square); pane-header enrichment to the design's anatomy where the data already exists. Restyles every existing surface incl. 3-4's settings shell — which was built to the design's skeleton precisely so this phase recolors rather than rearranges.

- **Placement rationale:** before Phase 4, so the notification center, inbox, and tray chrome are built *in* the design language rather than retrofitted into it — the same land-the-frame-first principle as D30's logger and D35's scrubber, applied to pixels. Sequencing relative to 3a/3b is Matthew's call at the next `/architect`.
- **Kickoff must settle:** the mock's own open-questions list (filmstrip right-rail vs bottom strip at 16+ sessions; amber pulse strength; inbox mode vs overlay; mission-control orientation; card width), plus the F15 lesson applied in reverse — an app-wide CSS/token change needs a per-surface visual regression pass, not a compile check.
- **Milestone:** the running app is visually indistinguishable from the Workspace and Settings mocks for every surface that exists, with no behavioral change — screenshot-diffed against the mocks.
### Phase 3d — Provider Routes & New Adapters — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-28** _(created by D52 2026-07-24 as "Two New PTY Adapters"; **RENAMED and reconciled by the architect pass of 2026-07-28**; closed by `35a592f`)_

**⚠ RENAMED, AND THE RENAME IS THE POINT — Matthew's ruling, 2026-07-28.** The phase was created to hold two PTY adapters. It ended up holding **two different kinds of work**: *(1)* **provider/route semantics** — what `provider_configs.adapter_type` actually means, which has nothing to do with a PTY agent — and *(2)* **the two adapters**. The prior entry carried a flag asking whether that should be one phase or two. **It is one phase, renamed**, because all of it landed in a single uninterrupted run of commits over two days and splitting it after the fact would invent a phase number for work already done. **The flag is discharged and removed.**

**⚠ THIS PHASE RAN OUT OF ORDER, AT MATTHEW'S REQUEST, BECAUSE IT WAS BLOCKING HIS DAILY USE OF THE APP** — it interleaved with Phase 3c, landing entirely between **3c-4** (`070f381`) and **3c-5** (`c4f82fb`). That is why 3c's frozen numbers went stale mid-phase; see the Phase 3c entry.

**⚠ AND IT SHIPPED WITH NO PHASE OVERVIEW, NO TASK DOCS AND NO IMPLEMENTATION SPECS — only `Tasks/Task-3d-1-ExecutionPrompt.md` exists.** Verified 2026-07-28. That is the mechanical reason this entry sat stale for four days while seven commits landed under it: **there was no task doc whose close would have prompted an update.** Recorded as a process fact, not a reproach — the work is sound and its decisions (D84–D90) were all recorded properly in §6 as they were settled. **The lesson is that the decision log stayed honest while the phase entry did not, because only one of the two had a ritual attached to it.**

| Commit | Decision | What shipped |
|---|---|---|
| `717d312` | **D84** | `adapter_type` names **the harness that runs the traffic**, and `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE = 'none'` is a legal answer. New `adapters/noHarness.ts`. **No schema change, no migration** — the column was already `TEXT NOT NULL`. |
| `a62aad7` | **D85** | The **model shortlist** — `model_shortlist(provider_id, model_id, added_at)`, migration **v12**, `sqliteTable` 15 → 16. Rejected the `favourite`-column alternative on a property: the catalog is a **cache** (D56) and a curation column would make one table mean two things. **No FK onto `model_catalog`** — a user must be able to shortlist an id no refresh has returned. |
| `e87037a` | **D86 · D87** | **Kimi CLI.** `agentKindSchema` + `staticRegistry` **2 → 3, together** — the D34 Q5 freeze lifted as a numbered decision. D87 is the D4 pass: **`-c` is `--continue` on kimi, NOT `--config`**, so kimi has **no per-launch configuration door** and declares `apiKey: false` / `reasoningEffort: null` honestly. |
| `0d1c93f` | **D88** | **A real user-facing defect, found in use:** every Claude pane was printing *"Transcript saving is off — inherited CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION marker"*. **The cause was Chorus**, inheriting the launcher's session markers wholesale. `stripHostAgentSessionVars` removes four names from `parentEnv` **once, before either branch reads it**. |
| `7a738d5` | **D89** | **A launch profile's `env_json` was silently dropped on every SUBSCRIPTION launch** — the common case. `envAdditions` now applies on **both** branches of `composeChildEnv`. Recorded as an **amendment to D34(d)**, not a bug fix, because the shipped behaviour matched its own governing clause exactly. |
| `2ace8bb` | *(see D83 note below)* | The **startup splash**, `renderer/src/boot/bootInfo.ts`, and docs. **Mixed commit** — it also carries 3c doc updates. |
| `35a592f` | **D90** | **`opencode`** as the harness behind the launch dialog's OpenRouter card — registry **3 → 4**, same widen-together rule. Plus the launch dialog's **model picker**, a **closed `<select>`** over `model_shortlist`. D4-verified by running the binary: the key reaches opencode **through the environment**, so **nothing is written to its auth file** — the only reason this adapter was adoptable at all. |

**Ground facts, verified 2026-07-28 at `331267b`:** `agentKindSchema` = `['claude','codex','kimi','opencode']` · `staticRegistry` holds the same four, typed `Readonly<Record<AgentKind, AgentAdapter>>` so coverage is **compiler-enforced in both directions** · `adapters/` holds `claude.ts`, `codex.ts`, `kimi.ts`, `opencode.ts`, `noHarness.ts` · `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE = 'none'` at `shared/ipc.ts:258` and is **still not in the enum** · `MIGRATIONS.length` **12** · `sqliteTable(` **16** · `IpcChannel` **57** · `ipcMain.handle(` **52 / 0**.

**⚠ THE MILESTONE AS ORIGINALLY WORDED IS NOT MET, AND SAYING SO IS THE MAIN VALUE OF THIS PASS.** It read: *"a launch profile can name an agent CLI Chorus did not ship with, and it launches on the route and model the profile specifies."* **Kimi and opencode are adapters Chorus DID ship with.** The registry is still `Object.freeze`d and still static; `registry.ts` says in its own comment that **`register()` behind a Map-backed registry is Phase 6's**. So the phase delivered **reach — four harnesses instead of two — and not extensibility.** A reader who took the old wording at face value would believe a user-registration path exists. It does not.

- **Milestone, as actually met:** four agent harnesses ship (`claude`, `codex`, `kimi`, `opencode`), a provider may honestly declare that **no** harness runs it (`'none'`), and a launch profile launches on the route and model it specifies — with the model chosen from a user-curated shortlist rather than a 341-entry catalog. **The registry-freeze lift (D34 Q5) is spent** (D86, D90).
- **⚠ What is therefore still owed, and by whom:** **runtime adapter registration is Phase 6's**, unchanged and now the *only* remaining part of the original wording. Phase 6's own entry already says the D34 Q5 seam is spent; that stays correct — what it inherits is `register()`, not the freeze.

**⚠ D83's PROHIBITION WAS OVERRIDDEN, THE SPLASH WAS BUILT, AND THE ROADMAP'S OWN D83 ROW IS NOW FALSE UNTIL CORRECTED.** D83 (Phase 3c) ruled `Chorus Startup.dc.html` a **new feature**, said **"DO NOT BUILD IT"**, and recorded it as *"unbuilt and unscheduled… so it is not silently dropped"*. It was built in `2ace8bb` (2026-07-27) — `components/StartupSplash.vue` (7,326 B) and `boot/bootInfo.ts` (4,379 B), both verified present 2026-07-28.

**⚠ AND IT WAS BUILT LEGITIMATELY RATHER THAN IN DEFIANCE, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING.** D83's objection was **D76**: the mock's boot line *"waking 7 voices · restoring 3 sessions"* needs live restore progress **the renderer is never told**. `bootInfo.ts` answers exactly that objection — main **stamps the boot constants onto the renderer URL** before the window exists, so the splash has a real source on its first frame. Its header records why this is a query string and not an IPC channel (write-once boot constants that can never change while the window lives), that it is **parsed as untrusted input** (clamped, length-capped, rendered as text), and that **D76 is enforced in the parser rather than the template** — an absent, malformed or zero value resolves to the same *"nothing to say"* state, so the splash **cannot render a placeholder or a zero even if main sends a broken one**.

**So D83 is SUPERSEDED, not violated:** the ruling was correct on the facts it had, and the facts changed when someone built the source it said was missing. **The standing lesson is D76's, and it survives intact — the answer to "the mock draws data that does not exist" is either omit it or GIVE IT A SOURCE, and never fake it.**

Full record: [`Investigations/3e-2-Proving-Run.md`](Investigations/3e-2-Proving-Run.md). Run

four members answered.** `4/4 answered · 0 refused · 8 turns · usage reported for 8, absent for 0`.

  only after normalising to a shared unit.** Read in the raw BYTES the table uses, 3e-1's figures
  look like row 1 "pathological", **which is the reading 3e-1 retracted.** `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES`
  **4_000_000 → 8_000_000**.
- **⚠ THE RAISE IS LOAD-BEARING ON A REAL TURN, NOT ONLY ON ARITHMETIC.** Kimi's critique streamed
  **4,168,377 bytes — 104% of the old cap — and completed.** Under 4 MB that turn refuses and the
  run is partial again. Its *positions* turn in the same run streamed **2,446,913**, which the old
  cap would have passed: **kimi's streams straddle the old line**, so no single observation could
  have set this bound.
- **KIMI'S REAL RATIO, MEASURED AT LAST: 177.9 – 407.2 bytes/token.** ⚠ **3e-1's bound of "> 250"
  was TOO STRONG** — that was a property of one capped turn. Kimi varies **2.3× within one run**.
- **⚠ MY OWN FIRST DERIVATION OF THE NEW CAP WAS THE SAME UNIT ERROR 3e-1 RETRACTED, AND IT IS
  CALLED OUT IN THE CONSTANT'S COMMENT RATHER THAN QUIETLY REPLACED.** It used qwen's 205.1 ratio ×
  the **arbiter's** 32,000 allowance — a cross-model product — and landed within 1% of the right
  answer **by luck**. Corrected: each model's own ratio × its **own** allowance, worst case **kimi
  407.2 × 16,000 = 6,515,200**, so 8 MB is **1.23×** it and tolerates **500 bytes/token** at a
  16,000-token allowance.
- **✅ F39's SECOND HALF DISSOLVED, NOT REPRODUCED.** `usage for 8, absent for 0` with kimi
  participating fully. **The missing `usage` block was a property of the ABORT — an aborted stream
  never receives the frame that carries it — not of the model.** Fixing the cap fixed the cost
  under-reporting too: **one cause, two observed defects**, and F35 narrows accordingly.

  `4c17069c`; **it returns 2** (lines 364 and 420), so **F40 reproduced on the very document recorded

  `grep -c` = **1**, with the core's append rendered as `### Dissents preserved — the orchestrator's

#### ✅ Task 3e-4 — the transcript reader, and the retention position (D97)

**`council_messages` had been written on every run since 3b-3 and read by nothing. It has a door
now.** One channel, `council:transcript`, **read-only** — `IpcChannel` **57 → 58**,
`ipcMain.handle(` **52 → 53**, `index.ts` still **0**, `sqliteTable(` **16**, `MIGRATIONS.length`
**12**. The declared exception D97 stated in advance, spent exactly once.

- **It calls the read function that already existed** (`storage.ts:1819`, zero callers since 3b-2)
  rather than writing a second query over the same table.
- **⚠ BOUNDED AT THE BOUNDARY, AND THE PAYLOAD ADMITS IT WHEN IT BIT.** Cap **1,000,000
  characters** — **8.9× the largest transcript measured** (112,531 over 8 turns). Over it, the
  response returns what fits and sets `truncated`; `total_turns` travels with every response so a
  truncated read can never read as a short deliberation (D55).
- **⚠ THE UNIT IS CHARACTERS AND THE FIELD NAMES SAY SO.** `content` is a JS string; `.length` is
  UTF-16 code units, not bytes. **Calling that "bytes" is the exact class of error F39's retraction
  cost a run to learn**, so `chars` / `cap_chars` it is.
- **The historical transcript is state of its own, never `messages`.** Loading it into the live
  array would push finished rows through the delta-append path whose `(member, phase, round)`
  identity is **F37's fix** — 291 fragments where 8 turns belonged. **F37's regression test is
  green and unedited**, and a new store test asserts the independence **in both directions**.
- **The toggle is the mock's:** `FINDINGS [ findings | transcript · N turns ]`, active segment
  raised, count carrying its noun, all 3c-1 tokens and no stock palette utility.
- **⚠ Task-3c-5's invariant 2 holds:** switching panes does **not** move the standing caveat out of
  view — it is rendered above and outside the toggled region, so no state of the toggle can hide it.
- **Runtime (G2), twice over.** The channel was proven end-to-end against 3e-1's run **before** the
  paid run — 7 of 7 turns, 93,868 chars, `truncated: false`, plain objects (D14) — and the toggle
  was then driven on **3e-2's own run**: 8 stored turns, rendered as turns with member, phase and
  round, live blocks untouched. **Cost $0.00; it reads runs already paid for.**
- **Retention: answered as D99** — kept indefinitely, with the growth arithmetic (**~38 MB/year** at
  a pessimistic 4 runs/week), the reason a purge is the wrong trade, and **stated revisit triggers**
  so the position is falsifiable rather than permanent.

#### ✅ Task 3e-1 — the two numbers this phase exists to obtain

Full record: [`Investigations/3e-1-Measurement.md`](Investigations/3e-1-Measurement.md). Run
`4c17069c` on `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` **verbatim** (D98), frontier roster rebuilt
through the app's own channel with **all four ids re-checked against OpenRouter's live `/models`**
(339 returned, 2026-07-28).

- **VERDICT-TOKEN COMPLIANCE: 4 of 6 questions resolved STRUCTURALLY, 2 fell to `model-judged`.**
  **F38 measured the inverse** (4 of 6 falling through) on the cheap roster at the 700-token cap.
  **⚠ THE IMPROVEMENT IS NOT ATTRIBUTABLE TO ANY SINGLE DIAL, AND READING IT AS "THE TUNING FIXED
  IT" IS THE EXACT OVER-READ D67 FILED F38 TO PREVENT** — the roster, the ceiling, the mint cap and
  the timeout all moved between the two, and **this run had only 2 of 3 members answering**, so the
  structural arm had fewer verdicts to reconcile. **F38's compliance half is discharged as
  MEASURED, not as FIXED.**
- **⚠ F39 — THE FIRST READING WAS "PATHOLOGICAL" AND IT WAS WRONG. RETRACTED AND CORRECTED
  2026-07-28, BEFORE ANY CODE ACTED ON IT.** Kimi streamed **4,000,372** bytes against a largest
  completed turn of **692,858**, and that 5.8× was read as "unbounded". **⚠ THE COMPARISON WAS
  INVALID: IT COMPARED BYTES ACROSS MODELS WHOSE BYTES-PER-TOKEN DIFFER BY 20×.** Measured on this
  run's own rows — stream bytes ÷ `tokens_out` per turn:

  | Member | bytes / output token |
  |---|---|
  | CR Arbiter (opus-5) | **9.9 – 13.2** |
  | CR GLM (5.2) | **61.4 – 66.1** |
  | CR Qwen (3-coder) | **201.2 – 205.1** |

  **SSE framing overhead per token is a property of the MODEL's chunking, not of how much it
  said.** At Qwen's observed ratio a **16,000-token allowance is ~3.3 MB** — so kimi reaching 4 MB
  is fully consistent with it **spending the allowance Chorus gave it**, not with being unbounded.
  **The 4 MB byte cap and the 16,000-token allowance are mutually inconsistent for a verbosely
  framed reasoning model, and THAT is the defect** — a bound on bytes and a bound on tokens that
  were never reconciled with each other.
- **⚠ WHAT IS ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED, STATED NARROWLY:** kimi's bytes-per-token is **> 250** (it
  exceeded 4 MB inside a 16,000-token budget) and is **otherwise UNKNOWN**, because the capped turn
  reported no `usage` at all. **F39 is NOT resolved.** It is better understood and still open.
- **⚠ MATTHEW'S RULING, 2026-07-28: KIMI STAYS.** The roster is unchanged and no replacement member
  is needed — which also means D67 Q6's two-member floor is not being approached deliberately.
  `ImplementationSpec-3e-2.md` §3 already offers **"bound the member, or drop it"**; 3e-2 takes the
  bound. **The two candidate bounds, both to be decided on evidence rather than taste:** lower
  kimi's own `max_tokens` until its worst-case stream fits the existing cap (**touches no global
  constant**), or raise `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES` on a **computed** basis — worst observed ratio (205)
  × the largest allowance (32,000, the arbiter) = **6.6 MB**, which would make the existing 8 MB
  `modelCatalog` cap the natural value and replace the current "half of 8 MB" relationship, which
  the constant's own comment shows was never computed either.
- **F39's second half REPRODUCED: the capped turn contributed NO `usage` block**, so **every cost
  figure from a run kimi participates in is a FLOOR.** Observed now, not inferred.
- **⚠ A DECISION FOR MATTHEW BEFORE 3e-2 CAN CLOSE F39:** dropping kimi leaves **two** deliberating
  members — **D67 Q6's floor with zero margin**. This run already demonstrates the exposure: it
  survived on 2 only because neither survivor failed. **If kimi is dropped the roster needs a
  replacement member, and choosing one is Matthew's call.**
- **Three of the four proofs Phase 3c-5 left UNPROVEN are discharged**: a real run streams into the
  restyled view · the findings `.md` landed beside the brief (**39,832 B**, ending cleanly) · **F37
  grouping holds — 7 blocks for 7 turns.** ⚠ **Esc-refuses-to-leave-mid-run stays UNPROVEN** and is
  owed by 3e-2's run.
- **⚠ F40 DID NOT REPRODUCE, AND THAT IS NOT EVIDENCE IT IS ABSENT.** The document carries exactly
  one `## Dissents preserved` — but this was a **partial** run whose arbiter may simply not have
  written the section, leaving the core's unconditional append nothing to duplicate. **3e-2 must
  re-check on a full run and must not read this as a fix.**

**⚠ THE ABORTED FIRST ATTEMPT COST $0.037 AND WAS WORTH MORE THAN THAT.** It died on D67 Q6's
two-member floor because `params_json` was written with **`max_completion_tokens`** — OpenRouter's
own API field name — while `resolveMaxOutputTokens` reads **`max_tokens`** and falls back
**silently** to `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_DEFAULT = 1200`. Two reasoning members spent the whole shrunken
budget on reasoning and returned **empty** content. **⚠ D71's row records the VALUES and not the
KEY, which is what made this mis-settable**, and `ImplementationSpec-3e-1.md` §2 carried the same
wrong key until a run disproved it. **Also learned: `params_json` is settable at CREATE ONLY** —
`updateCouncilMember` takes `{id, label}` — so fixing it meant deleting and recreating all four
members.

**Spend: `$0.037` + `$0.658` = `$0.695` of the ~$4.00 authorised.** ⚠ Both figures are Chorus's
own and are **floors**; neither was checked against OpenRouter's billing page.

#### Original scope note _(retained — see the Overview for how D95 and D97 widen it)_


**⚠ THIS PHASE EXISTS BECAUSE THE WORK HAD NO OWNER, AND THAT IS THE ENTIRE JUSTIFICATION FOR NUMBERING IT.** Phase 3b closed with a residual that neither 3c (design) nor 3d (adapters) touches, and the close record said in as many words that it "should be assigned deliberately rather than rediscovered the next time someone runs a real CR natively." Post-close tuning (**D71**) then discharged one half of it and **added two new findings**, which is evidence that the residual is live work rather than a filing artefact. **A named phase is the difference between a queue item and a thing that gets found again by accident.**

Scope is **tuning and measurement over a shipped, working feature** — no new milestone capability, no schema, and (with one exception below) no new surface:

- **⚠ THE HEADLINE ITEM IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A FIX, AND IT MUST BE DONE FIRST: re-measure verdict-token compliance on the frontier roster.** F38 measured **4 of 6 questions falling to `model-judged`** rather than structurally measured — on the **cheap** roster, at the 700-token cap. **Nobody has re-measured it on D71's roster.** The document got dramatically better (F38's other half is discharged), and **reading that as evidence the structural arm now works is exactly the over-read D67 filed it as a not-checkable prediction to prevent.** The honest status is **unmeasured**. If it still fails, the fix is a prompt/detection question and belongs here; if it passes, this bullet closes on evidence rather than on hope.
- **F39 — one member refuses every run, and the fix depends on a fact nobody has established.** `moonshotai/kimi-k3` hit `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES = 4_000_000` on **three consecutive runs**, contributes **no `usage` block** (so Chorus's own reported run cost **under-reports the truth** whenever it participates — narrowing F35), and leaves the council sitting on **exactly D67 Q6's two-member floor with zero margin**, where one more failure aborts the run. **⚠ Whether kimi is pathological or the 4 MB cap is simply too small for a model that streams its chain of thought is NOT ESTABLISHED, and the two answers have opposite fixes** (raise the cap vs drop the member). **Measure before touching the constant — a cap raised on a guess is how an unbounded stream gets re-authorised**, and D63(e) put that bound there on purpose.
- **F40 — the duplicated `## Dissents preserved` heading, whose obvious fix is forbidden.** The arbiter writes one section and the pure core appends one unconditionally. **The core's unconditional append is the whole enforceability argument** (D67 Q5 ruling 5C as corrected) and **must not be the thing removed** — deleting it hands dissent preservation back to the arbiter's goodwill, which is 5A, explicitly rejected. The fix lands on the **core's heading** or the **synthesis prompt**, never on the append.
- **The dissent matcher is noisy** — on the dogfood run **two of nine "dissents" say nothing challenges the synthesis**, because mechanical extraction from a `DISAGREE:` label cannot distinguish a non-objection from an objection; and **one talkative member produced six of the nine**. 3b-3 predicted both and left them rather than tuning them away.
- **F41 — decide whether council time is task work or overhead**, and record the answer as a numbered decision either way. **⚠ TIME-SENSITIVE for the same D50 reason as everything else in the telemetry story: actuals cannot be backfilled**, so this one is cheap now and unrecoverable later. If the answer is "task work", it is the only item here that touches a surface outside the council files.
- **⚠ `council_messages` IS A WRITE-ONLY STORE WITH NO READER AND NO RETENTION POLICY.** `deleteCouncilRun` has no caller and **`getCouncilMessagesForRun` has none either** — the view renders from the live broadcast, so 3b-2's prediction that the view would become the first reader **did not come true**. Runs now cost **~$0.83 and ~14 minutes** each (D71), which makes a transcript nobody can re-read a materially worse deal than it was at $0.0012. **Give it a reader or justify the absence in writing** — a store with no delete path is a decision, not an oversight, and it has now been inherited across three tasks.
- **Not in scope:** the deliberation protocol itself. D67 ruled it, CR-3b.1 is closed, and re-opening the protocol is a new CR (§4), not a tuning pass.

- **Sequencing:** after 3c, and **its position is deliberate rather than a default** — every item here is measurement or a constant, none of it blocks daily use of the app, and the one genuinely decaying item (**F41**) is a one-line classification decision that does not need a phase's worth of runway if it becomes urgent. **⚠ But the decay is real and should not be allowed to justify indefinite deferral twice** — that is precisely how it became a residual the first time.
- **⚠ Cost is a first-class constraint here, unlike every earlier phase.** At D71's dials a single full run is **~$0.83 pre-authorized against a $10 minted cap** and takes **~14 minutes**, so a measurement pass that re-runs the same brief four times is a **~$3.30, one-hour** exercise. **State the envelope before the first run and measure against it** (3b-1's standing lesson), and note that **F39 means the reported figure under-reports the truth whenever kimi participates** — so the envelope must be checked against OpenRouter's own billing page, not only against Chorus's number.
- **Milestone:** verdict-token compliance is **re-measured on the frontier roster and the number is written down** whatever it says; F39 is resolved by measurement rather than by guess; F40 and F41 are closed as numbered decisions; and the transcript store has either a reader or a written justification for not having one.

### Phase 4 — Notifications _(provisional)_ **[CR: localhost hook listener security]**

Hook listener + hook injection into `.claude/settings.json`; append-only `agent_events` bus; notification policies; toast → focus-pane; tray badge; notification center; Attention Inbox; per-session event timeline sidebar; sub-agent lifecycle surfaced as read-only awareness (D39).

- **Note:** OS toast delivery is already **proven blocked** by the dev machine's `ToastEnabled=0` — the **in-app notification center must be first-class**, not an afterthought.
- **Sub-agent awareness (D39):** the `agent_events` bus + per-session event timeline surface sub-agent lifecycle (e.g. Claude Code's Task tool) as **read-only** awareness — an "N sub-agents running / done" indicator on the parent pane and per-child entries in the timeline. In-process children are **not** separate sessions and get **no** per-child stop/interact; real per-agent isolation stays the launch-a-sibling-session path. Not a new process model — it rides the hook bus this phase already builds. Exact CLI hook event names verified at build time (D4).
- **[CR] question:** how do we secure the localhost hook listener against local processes spoofing agent events?
- **Milestone:** agent attention events reliably surface in-app regardless of OS toast state.

### Phase 5 — Voice _(provisional)_

uiohook push-to-talk; whisper.cpp local + cloud toggle; mic overlay; injection with target ring; mission-control overlay (shares always-on-top plumbing).

- **Milestone:** voice dictation injects into the targeted session.

### Phase 6 — Neo4j Memory + Skills _(provisional — DESIGN-PLANNED 2026-07-28, NOT kicked off)_ **[CR: memory schema + provenance model]**

Design plan: [`Phase-6-MemoryPlan.md`](Phase-6-MemoryPlan.md) (2026-07-28, authored against `35a592f`) — **authoritative on design**; this entry records placement, scope and status only. **Matthew's sequencing ruling, 2026-07-28: plan now, execute after 3e.** The committed queue 3c → 3d → 3e is unchanged and nothing here licenses starting early.

dockerode provisioner; per-project containers; schema templates + provenance; MCP wiring into CLIs; index-codebase skill; lifecycle UI; provider-neutral skill format (`skill.yaml`).

- **⚠ TWO OF THIS PHASE'S LISTED DELIVERABLES ARE ALREADY SPENT, and the bullets that assigned them are DELETED rather than left to be inherited as false scope.** ~~D34 Q5's adapter-registration seam lands here~~ — **D86 lifted the freeze in Phase 3d (registry 2 → 3) and D90 widened it again (3 → 4)**, so the seam and its widen-together rule are **shipped, enforced code**, not a Phase 6 obligation. ~~Kimi CLI and an OpenAI-compatible agent CLI may arrive before this phase~~ — **they did**: `e87037a` (kimi) and `35a592f` (opencode). What remains of the original scope is the provisioner, schema + provenance, the MCP *config-writing implementation*, `index-codebase`, the lifecycle UI, the D76 status chip, and `skill.yaml`.
- **⚠ THE `SupportsMcp` SEAM EXISTS BUT IS WRONG-SHAPED, AND ITS VOCABULARY POINTS AT THE FILE D49 FORBIDS.** `McpDescriptor` (`types.ts:88`) can only describe adapters that write a **file** — the only shape fitting codex is `{format:'toml', location:'home', configPath:'.codex/config.toml'}` — and `McpServerRef` (`:337`) has no `env` field, which makes env-var indirection **untypeable**. Both are fixed in Stage 1. See the plan §3.
- **⚠ THE BRIGHT LINE IS THE PHASE'S CENTRAL DESIGN CONSTRAINT, not a footnote — see D93.** Wiring a Neo4j MCP server normally means putting a bolt password into a CLI's own config file, which is exactly what D49 and the AUTH-PRECEDENCE FINDING forbid. It is avoidable in every mode, but **only by design**: local Docker runs `NEO4J_AUTH=none` on `127.0.0.1` (no secret exists), and credentialed modes pass a variable **name**, never a value. **Note `npm run grep:secrets` reaches neither `~/.codex/` nor a project's `.mcp.json`** — G4 does not cover this phase's riskiest write, and a structural guard is what does.
- **Staged connect-first per D91:** Stage 0 CR + D4 · Stage 1 MCP honesty + codex (**zero new deps, writes nothing**) · Stage 2 connect to an existing Neo4j (`neo4j-driver`, migration **v13**) · Stage 3 graph schema + provenance + validator · Stage 4 `writeMcpConfig` for claude + opencode (**milestone met**) · Stage 5 Docker provisioner, then skills.
- **⚠ `Plan.md` §10 IS WRONG IN FOUR CHECKABLE PLACES (D94)** — `File.path` uniqueness breaks on worktrees; "one database per project" is unachievable on Community Edition; `confidence: number` is uncalibrated self-report; and three provenance fields must be edges rather than string properties. The spec is annotated, not rewritten (the D42/LiteLLM precedent).
- **⚠ THE `[CR]` GATE IS UNFIRED AND G5 BLOCKS CODING UNTIL IT CLOSES.** It runs **natively** now: ~$0.83 and ~14 min per run against D71's roster. The brief's questions are specified in the plan §12 — and it must open with *"answer these questions; do not review this document"*, or the council reviews the brief instead of answering it.
- **[CR] question:** what memory schema and provenance model keeps agent-written knowledge trustworthy and attributable? **⚠ Sharpened by the design pass:** because agents write via MCP directly, **Chorus cannot ENFORCE provenance** — the write tool takes Cypher. So the real question is whether `memory:validate`'s measured answer ("43 of 512 memories have no source") is sufficient, or whether v1 needs app-mediated writes.
- **Milestone:** agents read/write a per-project memory graph via MCP. **Met at Stage 4; Stage 5 is the tail.**

### Phase 7 — Polish & Ship _(provisional)_

Pop-out windows; scrollback search; transcript export; cost rollups; secret-redaction audit; **NSIS installer + updater that registers the AUMID properly** (fixes the dev toast situation); crash-recovery pass; Windows terminal test matrix (Playwright) in CI.

### Phase 8 — Mission Control _(provisional — created by D41; POST-v1)_ **[CR: estimator honesty + attention measurement]**

Spec: [`docs/Features/Mission Control/chorus-mission-control-spec.md`](../Mission%20Control/chorus-mission-control-spec.md) (Matthew, 2026-07-24) — **authoritative on design**; this entry records placement, dependencies, and conflicts only.

A scheduling, costing, and forecasting panel: structurally a critical-path (CPM) scheduler over a committed task-graph seed, with two extra cost dimensions — **attention-minutes** and **tokens** — and the agent fleet as the resource pool. Its organising principle is **derived, never stored**: readiness, fan-out, critical path, float, review gates, estimates, and the ship date are all computed on read; only facts about the work are hand-authored. The argument for it living *inside* Chorus is strong and worth preserving: pane start/exit is wall-clock for free, the adapter knows agent+model for free, and window focus is the only tractable route to attention-minutes — all expensive from outside the process, free from inside.

Internal phasing (spec §9): **0** telemetry capture → **1** seed + validation + read-only board + pane task chip → **2** estimates + Monte Carlo projection → **3** routing + derived gates → **4** PM report.

- **⚠ Spec Phase 0 is TIME-SENSITIVE and must not wait for this phase.** Historical actuals **cannot be backfilled**, and spec phases 2+ are worthless without 3–4 weeks of data. It is ~half a day, has no UI, and disturbs nothing. **It is the same producer Phase 3a's `usage_records` needs** — see the Phase 3a note above; build it once, there, not twice.
- **⚠ NAMING COLLISION — resolve before either ships.** This roadmap already uses "mission control" for a *different* thing: Phase 5's **"mission-control overlay (shares always-on-top plumbing)"**, echoed in D38's mock inventory ("5 (mission control, push-to-talk)") and in Phase 3c's "mission-control orientation" kickoff question. That is an always-on-top **pane-switcher overlay**; this is a **scheduling/costing panel**. Two unrelated features cannot share a name in a roadmap this cross-referenced. **Recommendation (needs Matthew's ratification):** this feature keeps **Mission Control**; the Phase 5 overlay is renamed (e.g. **Fleet Switcher**) wherever it appears in §7, D38, and the design mocks.
- **Open question 1 is ALREADY ANSWERED by this roadmap's verified ground facts — do not re-investigate.** "Does the pane abstraction expose a stable id surviving restart?" **Yes:** session identity *is* the `sessions` DB row id; the PTY is ephemeral and re-created under the same id by the restore engine and `session:restart` (1-2 onward, D16). **Two caveats a dispatch record must handle:** (a) a restored session is a genuinely **fresh conversation**, not a resumed one — the id survives, the context does not; (b) pane close / `session:delete` **deletes the row** (D16 resolution d), so dispatch records must tolerate their session id disappearing rather than assuming referential integrity.
- **Open question 2 gets answered for free by Task 3-6.** "Can Codex/Gemini CLI be pointed at a custom base URL + key via env?" 3-6 is already D4-verifying codex's auth/env surface against 0.145.0; **add TWO questions to that verification list** — (i) does it accept a custom base URL + key via env, and (ii) can it be told to use an **arbitrary model id** at that endpoint (**D43**)? Both cost nothing there; (i) unblocks this feature's cost-attribution path and (ii) determines whether api-route launch profiles have any PTY host at all before api-mode sessions exist. Note the mechanism already exists in Phase 3: `provider_configs.base_url` + D34(e)'s `env_var_name` override were built for exactly this custom/OpenAI-compat endpoint case. **Gemini CLI is not installed on this machine** and is out of scope until it is.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 3 vault + provider/base-URL infrastructure (a gateway is just a custom base URL + key, which `provider_configs` already models); pane lifecycle events (shipped since Phase 0/1); ~~**new** — focus tracking and `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime()` for attention~~ **✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-25 by Task 3a-2 (`fd4c9e5`)** — focus tracking, the idle threshold, the five-class sampler and `attention:summary` all exist and have been accumulating real rows since that date; **new** — an OpenRouter Provisioning-API client for per-dispatch keys. **⚠ The spec's §5.1 LiteLLM recommendation is SUPERSEDED by D42 — OpenRouter is the single gateway and LiteLLM is dropped.** Consequently there is **no new runtime dependency and no `CLAUDE.md` dependency ask**: OpenRouter is reached over `fetch` with a base URL and a key. The spec document itself still says LiteLLM and is superseded on that point only (§2 authority split: the roadmap wins on current status).
- **[CR] question:** the estimator's honesty rules and the attention-measurement design — the spec's own §11 names both as top risks (confident numbers from thin data; an unreliable input driving the headline output). Sample-count surfacing and the hard refuse-to-project threshold are exactly the kind of "two approaches remain contested" surface §4 exists for. **⚠ NARROWED 2026-07-25 — the attention half is now half-answered in code, and the council must be briefed on that rather than asked to re-derive it.** Task 3a-2 fixed the *capture* contract (**D55**: no number without its denominator, enforced by the schema; the figure is a stated lower bound) and made the ambiguous §5.3 rulings explicitly — sampled runs over intervals, no retro-debit, `blurred` recorded rather than dropped. **What remains genuinely open for the CR is therefore narrower and sharper:** (a) the *estimator's* honesty rules — the refuse-to-project threshold and how sample counts drive confidence bands — which 3a-2 did not touch; (b) whether focus-plus-idle proves to be a good enough proxy **once weeks of real coverage data exist** (the spec's §10 Q3, answerable only by the readout, not by argument); and (c) the shape of the deferred one-tap correction control, whose additive schema is already fixed (`source='corrected'` as a new row) but whose *trigger moment* still needs the completion surface this phase builds. **The council must also be given F30 up front:** the `locked` class rests on an event that has never been observed firing on the dev machine, so any away-time reasoning built on it is standing on an unproven leg.
- **Milestone:** a real Chorus project shows a derived ready set, a calibrated ship-date band with visible sample counts, and per-dispatch token attribution — with the binding constraint (attention vs agent slots) correctly identified.

## 8. Out Of Scope (v1 horizon)

Explicitly **not** in v1 (per PLAN):

- Task board / card-dispatch; orchestration roles; automation scheduler. **⚠ Amended 2026-07-24 (D41):** this exclusion is a **v1 boundary, not a permanent one**. Phase 8 — Mission Control is precisely a task board + dispatch + scheduler, and it is admitted to the roadmap as **post-v1**. The exclusion therefore now reads: *not in v1*, revisited deliberately in Phase 8. Nothing before Phase 7 may build toward it beyond the telemetry capture folded into Phase 3a. (Related: **D39** rules per-sub-agent control out of v1 on this same line.)
- Built-in editor & diff viewer; TTS; wake word.
- Cloud sync; plugin marketplace; mobile companion.
- **macOS / Linux** support.
- **Auto-merge** of agent branches.

**Owed items carried forward out of Phase 3b — ⚠ BOTH ARE NOW CLOSED OR OWNED as of 2026-07-26, and neither is carried loose any longer:**

- **✅ D70 IS RATIFIED (2026-07-26) — AND THE RATIFICATION CORRECTED ITS ARGUMENT RATHER THAN ACCEPTING IT.** The 3b-4 session widened the attention report's `view` enum from `workspace | settings` to include `council` **without asking**, and rewrote `classify`'s branch from `=== 'settings'` to `!== 'workspace'`. **⚠ ITS STATED ARGUMENT WAS FALSE ON A CHECKABLE FACT:** it claimed the alternative would write *"a false value into a telemetry field Phase 8's estimator will read"*, invoking D50's no-backfill rule — but **`attention_spans` has no `view` column** (`storage.ts:198`), the value is **never persisted and never logged**, and `classify()` collapses every non-workspace view to `overhead`. **Ratify and revert produce byte-identical rows, permanently.** The widened enum is kept **on a different reason**: `!== 'workspace'` is right because *no pane is mounted* is what makes a view overhead, and that is true of every future view **by construction**, so the rule cannot be forgotten the way a list can. **The standing lesson is filed against the argument, not the code** — a flagged raise reasoned from memory about the schema and came one ratification away from being adopted as fact by this document. See **D70** (§6) and **F41** (§5), which the ratification surfaced.
- **⚠ THE COUNCIL'S OWN OUTPUT QUALITY — THE TRUNCATION HALF IS DISCHARGED, THE COMPLIANCE HALF IS NOT, AND THE SCOPE IS NOW SMALLER AND SHARPER RATHER THAN CLOSED.** **DISCHARGED, and by measurement rather than by argument: the 700-token truncation is gone.** Post-close tuning (**D71**, `edc3133` + `ad64e66`) raised `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_CEILING` **4,000 → 32,000**, `COUNCIL_MINT_LIMIT_USD` **$1.00 → $10.00** and added `COUNCIL_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` at **15 min**, with `params_json` at **32,000 for the arbiter / 16,000 for the members**; on run `87082401-9e0a-4387-b234-638deed648df` the arbiter spent **26,106** tokens arbitrating and **16,203** synthesizing — **both inside the ceiling, so neither turn was cut off** — and the document ends where the arbiter meant it to, at **action item 14**. **⚠ NOT DISCHARGED — AND THIS IS THE HALF THAT MATTERED MORE: the verdict-token compliance collapse is STILL AN OPEN QUESTION, NOT A SOLVED ONE.** F38's **4-of-6 `model-judged`** was measured on the **cheap** roster and has **NOT been re-measured on the frontier roster** — nobody has looked, so the honest status is *unmeasured*, and the temptation to read "the document got better" as "the structural arm now carries its weight" is exactly the over-read D67 filed it as a not-checkable prediction to prevent. **Also outstanding: F39** — one member (`kimi-k3`) **refuses every run** on the 4 MB transport cap, contributes **no usage block** so its spend is invisible in Chorus's own reported cost, and leaves the council sitting **exactly on D67 Q6's two-member floor with zero margin** — **and F40**, the duplicated `## Dissents preserved` heading, whose fix must land on the core's heading or the synthesis prompt and **never** on the unconditional append. ~~**Neither Phase 3c nor Phase 3d touches any of this**, and it should still be **assigned deliberately rather than rediscovered the next time someone runs a real CR natively.**~~ **✅ ASSIGNED 2026-07-26 — this is now `### Phase 3e — Council Deliberation Quality` (§7), committed in the queue after 3c.** The sentence above got its wish: **an item owned by no phase was the whole risk**, and it is now owned by one, with the re-measurement named as its first act and its milestone written so that *"the document got better"* cannot be substituted for *"the structural arm was measured."* **F41 (council time bucketed as `overhead`) is assigned there too**, and it is the only decaying item in the phase.

**⚠ POST-CLOSE ADDENDUM (2026-07-26, `edc3133` + `ad64e66`, D71): THE PHASE'S LARGEST RESIDUAL WAS PARTLY DISCHARGED AFTER THE CLOSE — and the "NOT AT PARITY" verdict recorded above is REVISITED, not quietly left standing.** Matthew swapped the council to the models he would genuinely pick and asked for the output cap raised; three coupled dials moved (**ceiling 4,000 → 32,000 · mint cap $1 → $10 · a new 15-minute per-turn deadline**), and the same brief was re-run. Same brief throughout — `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` — four data points:

| run | bytes | sections | ends cleanly |
|---|---|---|---|
| external council (Cursor), `CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md` | 20,900 | 11 | yes |
| Chorus, cheap roster @ 700 | 7,316 | 5 | **NO** — cut at ~5% |
| Chorus, real roster @ 16,000 | 32,510 | 8 | **NO** — cut at ~95% |
| **Chorus, real roster @ 32,000** | **45,718** | **11** | **YES** |

**THE NATIVE DOCUMENT NOW EXCEEDS THE EXTERNAL ONE ON BOTH SIZE AND SECTION COMPLETENESS** — 45,718 bytes against 20,900, 11 sections against 11, and it terminates where the arbiter intended (action item 14) rather than mid-sentence. It reaches sections the truncated attempts never got to: *Risks & mitigations for the winner*, *What Phase 3b implements vs declares*, and a full **14-item** action list. The 16,000 attempt had also written one the external council never did — ***"Facts we could not verify — implementer must confirm against vendor documentation"*** — telling the reader explicitly not to trust the listed items, which is the standing caveat behaving as a habit rather than a header. Cost **$0.83109262 against the $10 cap**, **~14 minutes**, key revoked, **zero open ledger rows**, secret-grep clean. **⚠ THE UPDATED VERDICT, STATED PRECISELY SO IT CANNOT BE OVER-READ: parity on DOCUMENT COMPLETENESS is now reached and exceeded; parity OVERALL is not claimed.** F38's other half — **4 of 6 questions falling to `model-judged` instead of structurally measured verdict tokens** — was measured on the **cheap** roster and has **never been re-measured on this one**, so the structural arm of D67 Q3's hybrid detection is **unmeasured on the frontier roster, not vindicated by it**. And two new problems arrived with the fix: **F39**, one member refusing every run on the transport's 4 MB cap while reporting no usage at all — so the council runs on exactly two members, the D67 Q6 floor, with no margin, and **Chorus's own reported run cost under-reports the truth** — and **F40**, a duplicated dissent heading. **The milestone was met at close; the document is now better than the thing it was measured against; the deliberation's structural quality remains the open half.**

**Open items Phase 3 inherits:**

- **F21 (data-loss-adjacent)** — `worktree:remove` computes `forceBranch` from `confirmation === w.path` outside the dirty branch, so main would `-D` an unmerged branch on a **clean** worktree if such a request arrived. Unreachable through the shipped UI, so latent. **ASSIGNED to Task 3-1's chore commit (D32)** as a main-side gate: a distinct `branchForceConfirmation` naming the branch. Note for the implementer: `branchForceAllowed` must also reject an **empty** branch, because adopted population-4 rows are born with `branch = ''` — the standing dev fixture is exactly such a row.
- **F23 (real UX/session-loss defect, pre-existing since 1b-3)** — a palette launch replaces the whole layout tree, orphaning every other pane's view; those leafless `running` rows are then healed to `exited` at the next boot, silently killing the agents. **ASSIGNED to Task 3-1's chore commit (D32).** Kickoff review found a **second defect in the same expression**: `splitPane` returns the tree unchanged for an unknown target, so a **stale** split target silently drops the newly launched leaf by a different route to the same leafless-row outcome. Both are fixed together — `insertLaunchedLeaf` becomes total.
- **⚠ AUTH-PRECEDENCE FINDING (coordinator, 2026-07-23, from reading both CLIs' own `--help` on this machine) — binds Task 3-6, recorded before it is authored into execution.** **(1) Claude Code accepts `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`** — proven by `--bare`, which documents itself as the mode where *"Anthropic auth is strictly `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `apiKeyHelper` via `--settings` (OAuth and keychain are never read)"*; a flag that must explicitly stop reading OAuth/keychain proves normal mode reads them. **But which wins when BOTH a subscription login and an injected key are present is UNDOCUMENTED and undetermined.** If OAuth wins, BYOK would appear to work while billing the subscription — and it would **pass every absence-check in the Phase 3 milestone inspection**, since the key genuinely is in the child's environment and genuinely is absent everywhere else. Task 3-6 must settle it empirically from the CLI's own reporting; an indeterminate result is a FAIL. `--bare` is **not** an acceptable auth switch (it also disables hooks, LSP, plugin sync, auto-memory and CLAUDE.md discovery). **(2) Codex's API-key path is not an environment variable at all** — it is `codex login --with-api-key` reading from **stdin** and persisting into Codex's own credential store (`~/.codex/config.toml`; `codex login status`; `codex logout`). Whether Codex honours `OPENAI_API_KEY` at runtime is unverified and may be false, in which case **env injection cannot deliver BYOK for Codex** and the sanctioned outcome is to declare `api_key` unsupported on that adapter for Phase 3 and ship BYOK for Claude Code only. **Bright line, and it is a §4 CR trigger if anyone wants to cross it:** Chorus must never invoke `codex login`, write a key into `~/.codex/config.toml`, or write a key into a `--settings` file or `apiKeyHelper` script — all of them persist a decrypted credential into another tool's on-disk store, outside DPAPI and outside the vault, which is a threat model **D33 never reviewed**. Recorded in `Task-3-6.md` (Steps 1a/1b, precedence proof, two acceptance criteria) and `ImplementationSpec-3-6.md` §3a. **⚠ RESOLVED-BY-MOOTING 2026-07-24, not by proof — read this carefully before reviving it.** **D44 made Claude Code subscription-only by product policy**, so Steps 1a/1b were dropped and **Chorus never injects a key into `claude`**; the undetermined OAuth-vs-`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` precedence question was therefore **never settled empirically and remains open**, mooted rather than answered. **What 3-6 DID observe on a deliberate probe is adjacent and useful:** Claude Code 2.1.218 does not silently pick a winner — it **prompts the user** ("Detected a custom API key in your environment… Do you want to use this API key?"), and that prompt is itself the F27 fragment-leak channel. **The two bright lines stand unamended and were never approached:** no `codex login`, no key written to `~/.codex/config.toml`, no `--settings`/`apiKeyHelper` file (D49). **Codex's half IS settled:** env injection works — not via `OPENAI_API_KEY` but via the D47 route's `env_key`, which names the variable codex reads at runtime (`15a016e`). **If any future phase wants BYOK on `claude`, it must first re-open D44 AND settle the precedence question empirically — the silent-subscription-billing hazard this item describes is still real and still undetected by every absence-check in the milestone inspection.**
- **D26f wording** — correct the Phase-2 acceptance phrasing to "a live session **in the main tree**" (the implementation is right; the wording is loose). No code change. Still open.

- **Diff-poll fan-out** — 2 git processes per mounted worktree pane per 15 s (≤16 panes ⇒ ≤128 spawns/min worst case; observed load trivial). If Phase 3 adds more per-pane git features, move to the 1b-2 shared-clock pattern or a main-side cache keyed on HEAD/index mtime.
- **Verification-provenance rule (F20, standing)** — execution sessions run with a **redirected `AppData` but a real `C:\Projects`**: their filesystem/git evidence is trustworthy, their **database** evidence describes a different DB. The coordinator re-verifies DB state against the real `%APPDATA%\chorus\chorus.db` (projects `985d547b…` / `f47ac10b…`). Every prompt should state this as a known condition, and every implementer dump should quote the `projects` table. **⚠ F31 (2026-07-25) is the corollary and is NOT optional reading: `--user-data-dir` reaches the real DB but NOT the DPAPI context, so a vault credential written by an earlier session may be undecryptable. Any prompt that depends on a live credential must make re-entry through Settings a stated precondition.**
